src/allglobal_mod.F90:293:!>      This depends on \c Mpol, \c Ntor and \c Lrad(vvol). </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:294:!> <li> The covariant components of the vector potential are written as
src/allglobal_mod.F90:295:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ A_\theta & = & \sum_i \sum_{l=0}^L {\color{red}  A_{\theta,e,i,l}} \; T_{l}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_i \sum_{l=0}^L {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} \; T_{l}(s) \sin\alpha_i \\
src/allglobal_mod.F90:296:!>                     A_\zeta  & = & \sum_i \sum_{l=0}^L {\color{blue} A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} \; T_{l}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_i \sum_{l=0}^L {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} \; T_{l}(s) \sin\alpha_i ,
src/allglobal_mod.F90:297:!>       \f}

src/allglobal_mod.F90:299:!> <li> The following internal arrays are declared in preset() :
src/allglobal_mod.F90:300:!>

src/allglobal_mod.F90:302:!>

src/allglobal_mod.F90:436:!> \addtogroup grp_par_deriv_mat Parallel construction of derivative matrix
src/allglobal_mod.F90:437:!> <ul>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:438:!> <li> The derivatives of force-balance, \f$[[p+B^2/2]]\f$, and the spectral constraints (see sw03aa()), with respect to the interface geometry
src/allglobal_mod.F90:439:!>      is constructed in parallel by dforce(). </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:440:!> <li> force-balance across the \f$l\f$-th interface depends on the fields in the adjacent interfaces. </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:441:!> </ul>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:442:!> @{
src/allglobal_mod.F90:455:!> @}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:459:!> \addtogroup grp_deriv_mul_polflux Derivatives of multiplier and poloidal flux with respect to geometry: dmupfdx
src/allglobal_mod.F90:460:!> <ul>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:461:!> <li> The information in \c dmupfdx describes how the helicity multiplier, \f$\mu\f$, and the enclosed poloidal flux, \f$\Delta \psi_p\f$,
src/allglobal_mod.F90:462:!>      must vary as the geometry is varied in order to satisfy the interface transform constraint. </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:463:!> <li> The internal variable \c dmupfdx(1:Mvol,1:2,1:LGdof,0:1) is allocated/deallocated in newton(), and hesian() if selected. </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:464:!> <li> The magnetic field depends on the Fourier harmonics of both the inner and outer interface geometry (represented here as \f$x_j\f$),
src/allglobal_mod.F90:465:!>      the helicity multiplier, and the enclosed poloidal flux, i.e. \f${\bf B_\pm} = {\bf B_\pm}(x_j, \mu, \Delta \psi_p)\f$, so that
src/allglobal_mod.F90:466:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ \delta {\bf B_\pm} = \frac{\partial {\bf B}_\pm}{\partial x_j          } \delta x_j
src/allglobal_mod.F90:467:!>                                       + \frac{\partial {\bf B}_\pm}{\partial \mu          } \delta \mu
src/allglobal_mod.F90:468:!>                                       + \frac{\partial {\bf B}_\pm}{\partial \Delta \psi_p} \delta \Delta \psi_p.
src/allglobal_mod.F90:469:!>      \f} </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:470:!> <li> This information is used to adjust the calculation of how force-balance, i.e. \f$B^2\f$ at the interfaces,
src/allglobal_mod.F90:471:!>      varies with geometry at fixed interface rotational transform. Given
src/allglobal_mod.F90:472:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ B_\pm^2 = B_\pm^2 (x_j, \mu, \Delta \psi_p),
src/allglobal_mod.F90:473:!>      \f}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:474:!>      we may derive
src/allglobal_mod.F90:475:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ \frac{\partial B_\pm^2}{\partial x_j} = \frac{\partial B_\pm^2}{\partial x_j          }
src/allglobal_mod.F90:476:!>                                                          + \frac{\partial B_\pm^2}{\partial \mu          } \frac{\partial \mu          }{\partial x_j}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:477:!>                                                          + \frac{\partial B_\pm^2}{\partial \Delta \psi_p} \frac{\partial \Delta \psi_p}{\partial x_j}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:478:!>      \f} </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:479:!> <li> The constraint to be enforced is that \f$\mu\f$ and \f$\Delta \psi_p\f$ must generally vary as the geometry is varied
src/allglobal_mod.F90:480:!>      if the value of the rotational-transform constraint on the inner/outer interface is to be preserved,
src/allglobal_mod.F90:481:!>      i.e.
src/allglobal_mod.F90:482:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ \left(\begin{array}{ccc} \displaystyle \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_-}{\partial {\bf B}_-} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}_-}{\partial \mu          } & , &
src/allglobal_mod.F90:483:!>                                             \displaystyle \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_-}{\partial {\bf B}_-} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}_-}{\partial \Delta \psi_p} \\
src/allglobal_mod.F90:484:!>                                             \displaystyle \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_+}{\partial {\bf B}_+} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}_+}{\partial \mu          } & , &
src/allglobal_mod.F90:485:!>                                             \displaystyle \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_+}{\partial {\bf B}_+} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}_+}{\partial \Delta \psi_p}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:486:!>                    \end{array} \right)
src/allglobal_mod.F90:487:!>                      \left(\begin{array}{c} \displaystyle \frac{\partial \mu}{\partial x_j} \\
src/allglobal_mod.F90:488:!>                                             \displaystyle \frac{\partial \Delta \psi_p}{\partial x_j} \end{array} \right) =
src/allglobal_mod.F90:489:!>                    - \left(\begin{array}{c} \displaystyle \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_-}{\partial {\bf B}_-} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}_-}{\partial x_j} \\
src/allglobal_mod.F90:490:!>                                             \displaystyle \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_+}{\partial {\bf B}_+} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}_+}{\partial x_j} \end{array} \right).
src/allglobal_mod.F90:491:!>      \f} </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:492:!> <li> This \f$2\times 2\f$ linear equation is solved in dforce()
src/allglobal_mod.F90:493:!>      and the derivatives of the rotational-transform are given in \c diotadxup, see preset.f90 . </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:494:!> <li> A finite-difference estimate is computed if \c Lcheck==4. </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:495:!> </ul>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:496:!> @{
src/allglobal_mod.F90:509:!> @}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:512:!> \addtogroup grp_trig Trigonometric factors
src/allglobal_mod.F90:513:!> <ul>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:514:!> <li> To facilitate construction of the metric integrals, various trigonometric identities are exploited. </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:515:!> <li> The following are used for volume integrals (see volume() ):
src/allglobal_mod.F90:516:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ a_{i,j,k} &=& 4 \; m_k \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \cos(\alpha_i)\cos(\alpha_j)\cos(\alpha_k) /(2\pi)^2 , \\
src/allglobal_mod.F90:517:!>                    b_{i,j,k} &=& 4 \; m_j \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \cos(\alpha_i)\sin(\alpha_j)\sin(\alpha_k) /(2\pi)^2 ,
src/allglobal_mod.F90:518:!>      \f} </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:519:!> </ul>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:520:!> @{
src/allglobal_mod.F90:540:!> @}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:544:!> \addtogroup grp_volints Volume integrals: lBBintegral, lABintegral
src/allglobal_mod.F90:545:!> <ul>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:546:!> <li> The energy functional, \f$F \equiv \sum_l F_l\f$, where
src/allglobal_mod.F90:547:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ F_l \equiv \left( \int_{{\cal V}_l} \frac{p_l}{\gamma-1} + \frac{B_l^2}{2} dv \right)
src/allglobal_mod.F90:548:!>                    = \frac{P_l}{\gamma-1}V_l^{1-\gamma}+\int_{{\cal V}_l} \frac{B_l^2}{2} dv, \label{eq:energy_global}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:549:!>      \f}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:550:!>      where the second expression is derived using \f$p_l V_l^\gamma=P_l\f$, where \f$P_l\f$ is the adiabatic-constant.
src/allglobal_mod.F90:551:!>      In Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:energy_global})\f$, it is implicit that \f${\bf B}\f$ satisfies (i) the toroidal and poloidal flux constraints;
src/allglobal_mod.F90:552:!>      (ii) the interface constraint, \f${\bf B}\cdot\nabla s=0\f$; and (iii) the helicity constraint (or the transform constraint). </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:553:!> <li> The derivatives of \f$F_l\f$ with respect to the inner and outer adjacent interface geometry are stored in
src/allglobal_mod.F90:554:!>      \c dFF(1:Nvol,0:1,0:mn+mn-1), where
src/allglobal_mod.F90:555:!>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:556:!>      \f$         F_l                      \equiv\f$ \c dFF(l,0,    0)
src/allglobal_mod.F90:557:!>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:558:!>      \f$\partial F_l / \partial R_{l-1,j} \equiv\f$ \c dFF(ll,0,   j)
src/allglobal_mod.F90:559:!>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:560:!>      \f$\partial F_l / \partial Z_{l-1,j} \equiv\f$ \c dFF(ll,0,mn j)
src/allglobal_mod.F90:561:!>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:562:!>      \f$\partial F_l / \partial R_{l  ,j} \equiv\f$ \c dFF(ll,1,   j)
src/allglobal_mod.F90:563:!>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:564:!>      \f$\partial F_l / \partial Z_{l  ,j} \equiv\f$ \c dFF(ll,1,mn j)
src/allglobal_mod.F90:565:!>      </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:566:!> <li> The volume integrals \f$\int dv\f$, \f$\int B^2 \; dv\f$ and \f$\int {\bf A}\cdot{\bf B} \; dv\f$ in each volume
src/allglobal_mod.F90:567:!>      are computed and saved in \c volume(0:2,1:Nvol). </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:568:!> </ul>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:569:!> @{

src/allglobal_mod.F90:627:  !> \brief Builds the covariant component of the vector potential, by evaluating the polynomial basis (chebyshev or zernike) 
src/allglobal_mod.F90:628:  !> and store them in efmn, ofmn, sfmn, cfmn.
src/allglobal_mod.F90:641:    !> @param[in] lvol index of volume
src/allglobal_mod.F90:643:    !> @param[in] iocons inner (0) or outer (1) side of the volume
src/allglobal_mod.F90:645:    !> @param[in] aderiv Derivative of A. 
src/allglobal_mod.F90:646:    !>            - -1: w.r.t geometrical degree of freedom
src/allglobal_mod.F90:647:    !>            -  0: no derivatives
src/allglobal_mod.F90:648:    !>            -  1: w.r.t mu
src/allglobal_mod.F90:649:    !>            -  2: w.r.t pflux
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1032:!> **reading of physicslist**
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1033:!> <ul>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1034:!> <li> The internal variable, \c Mvol=Nvol+Lfreebound , gives the number of computational domains. </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1035:!> <li> The input value for the fluxes enclosed within each interface, \c tflux(1:Mvol) and \c tflux(1:Mvol), are immediately normalized:
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1036:!>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1037:!>       \c tflux(1:Mvol) \f$\rightarrow\f$ \c tflux(1:Mvol)/tflux(Nvol).
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1038:!>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1039:!>       \c pflux(1:Mvol) \f$\rightarrow\f$ \c pflux(1:Mvol)/tflux(Nvol).
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1040:!>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1041:!>       The input \f$\Phi_{edge} \equiv \f$ \c phiedge will provide the total toroidal flux; see preset(). </li>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1042:!> <li> The input value for the toroidal current constraint (\c Isurf(1:Mvol) and \c Ivolume(1:Mvol) ) are also immediately normalized, using \c curtor .
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1043:!>       \f$Ivolume \rightarrow Ivolume \cdot \frac{curtor}{\sum_i Isurf_i + Ivolume_i}\f$
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1044:!>       \f$Isurf   \rightarrow Isurf   \cdot \frac{curtor}{\sum_i Isurf_i + Ivolume_i}\f$
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1045:!> </ul>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1063:!> **Current profiles normalization**
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1064:!>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1065:!> In case of a free boundary calculation (\c Lfreebound=1) and using a current constraint (\c Lconstraint=3),
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1066:!> the current profiles are renormalized in order to match the linking current \c curtor.
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1067:!> More specifically,
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1068:!> \f{eqnarray}{
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1069:!> Isurf_i & \rightarrow\ Isurf_i \cdot \frac{curtor}{\sum_{i=1}^{Mvol-1} Isurf_i+Ivol_i}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1070:!> Ivol_i  & \rightarrow\ Ivol_i  \cdot \frac{curtor}{\sum_{i=1}^{Mvol-1} Isurf_i+Ivol_i}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1071:!> \f}
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1072:!> Finally, the volume current in the vacuum region is set to \f$0\f$.
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1113:!> **reading of numericlist**
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1138:!> **reading of locallist**
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1147:!> **reading of globallist**

src/allglobal_mod.F90:1368:  !> \brief Write the restart file \c ext.sp.end
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1369:  !> \ingroup grp_output
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1644:!> \brief Check if volume vvol is associated to the corresponding MPI node.
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1645:!>
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1646:!> The global variable \c IsMyVolumeValue is updated to 0 or 1,
src/allglobal_mod.F90:1647:!> depending on \c vvol . A value of -1 is set if an error occured.

src/basefn.F90:1:!> \file
src/basefn.F90:2:!> \brief Polynomials evaluation
src/basefn.F90:4:!> \brief Get the Chebyshev polynomials with zeroth, first derivatives
src/basefn.F90:5:!>
src/basefn.F90:6:!> The Chebyshev polynomial has been recombined and rescaled.
src/basefn.F90:7:!> By doing so, the Chebyshev polynomial satisfy the 0.0_rkind Dirichlet boundary condition on the inner surface of the annulus with reduced ill-conditioning problem.
src/basefn.F90:8:!>
src/basefn.F90:9:!> Let \f$T_{l}\f$ be the Chebyshev polynomial of the first kind with degree \f$l\f$.
src/basefn.F90:10:!> This subroutine computes
src/basefn.F90:11:!> \f[\bar{T}_0 = 1, \f] and
src/basefn.F90:12:!> \f[\bar{T}_l = \frac{T_l - (-1)^{l}}{l+1} . \f]
src/basefn.F90:13:!>
src/basefn.F90:14:!> \f$ T_l \f$ are computed iteratively.
src/basefn.F90:15:!> \f[ T_0(s) = 1, \f]
src/basefn.F90:16:!> \f[ T_1(s) = s, \f]
src/basefn.F90:17:!> \f[ T_{l+1}(s) = 2 s T_l(s) - T_{l-1}(s). \f]
src/basefn.F90:18:!>
src/basefn.F90:19:!> @param[in] lss coordinate input lss
src/basefn.F90:20:!> @param[in] lrad radial resolution
src/basefn.F90:21:!> @param[out] cheby the value, first derivative of Chebyshev polynomial

src/basefn.F90:53:!> The Chebyshev polynomial has been recombined and rescaled.
src/basefn.F90:54:!> See get_cheby for more detail.
src/basefn.F90:55:!> @param[in] lss coordinate input lss
src/basefn.F90:56:!> @param[in] lrad radial resolution
src/basefn.F90:57:!> @param[out] cheby the value, first and second derivative of Chebyshev polynomial
src/basefn.F90:90:!> \brief Get the Zernike polynomials \f$\hat{R}^{m}_{l}\f$ with zeroth, first derivatives
src/basefn.F90:91:!>
src/basefn.F90:92:!> The original Zernike polynomial is defined by
src/basefn.F90:93:!> The Zernike polynomials take the form
src/basefn.F90:94:!> \f{eqnarray*}{
src/basefn.F90:95:!>    Z^{-m}_{l}(s,\theta) &= R^{m}_{l}(s) \sin m\theta,\\
src/basefn.F90:96:!>    Z^{m}_{l}(s,\theta) &= R^{m}_{l}(s) \cos m\theta,
src/basefn.F90:97:!> \f}
src/basefn.F90:98:!> where \f$R^{m}_{l}(s)\f$ is a \f$l\f$-th order polynomial given by
src/basefn.F90:99:!> \f{eqnarray*}{
src/basefn.F90:100:!>    R_l^m(s) = \sum^{\frac{l-m}{2}}_{k=0} \frac{(-1)^k (l-k)!}
src/basefn.F90:101:!>    {k!\left[\frac{1}{2}(l+m) - k \right]! \left[\frac{1}{2}(l-m) - k \right]!} s^{l-2k},
src/basefn.F90:102:!> \f}
src/basefn.F90:103:!> and is only non-zero for \f$l \ge m\f$ and even \f$l-m\f$.
src/basefn.F90:104:!>
src/basefn.F90:105:!> In this subroutine, \f$R^{m}_{l}(s) \f$ is computed using the iterative relationship
src/basefn.F90:106:!> \f{eqnarray*}{
src/basefn.F90:107:!> R_l^m(s) = \frac{2(l-1)(2l(l-2)s^2 -m^2 -l(l-2))R_{l-2}^m(s) - l (l+m-2) (l-m-2)R_{l-4}^m(s) }{(l+m)(l-m)(l-2)}
src/basefn.F90:108:!> \f}
src/basefn.F90:109:!>
src/basefn.F90:110:!> For \f$ m=0 \f$ and \f$ m=1 \f$, a basis recombination method is used by defining new radial basis functions as
src/basefn.F90:111:!> \f{eqnarray*}{
src/basefn.F90:112:!>    \hat{R}_{0}^{0} &= 1,
src/basefn.F90:113:!>    \hat{R}^{0}_{l} &= \frac{1}{l+1}R^{0}_{l} - \frac{(-1)^{l/2}}{l+1},
src/basefn.F90:114:!>    \\
src/basefn.F90:115:!>    \hat{R}_{1}^{1} &= s,
src/basefn.F90:116:!>    \hat{R}^{1}_{l} &= \frac{1}{l+1}R^{1}_{l} - \frac{(-1)^{(l-1)/2}}{2} s.
src/basefn.F90:117:!> \f}
src/basefn.F90:118:!> so that the basis scales as \f$s^{m+2}\f$ except for \f$\hat{R}_{0}^{0}\f$ and \f$\hat{R}_{1}^{1}\f$, which are excluded from the representation of \f${A}_{\theta,m,n}\f$.
src/basefn.F90:119:!> For \f$m\ge2\f$, the radial basis functions are only rescaled as
src/basefn.F90:120:!> \f[
src/basefn.F90:121:!>   \hat{R}^{m}_{l} = \frac{1}{l+1}R^{m}_{l}.
src/basefn.F90:122:!> \f]
src/basefn.F90:123:!>
src/basefn.F90:124:!> @param[in] r coordinate input, note that this is normalized to \f$[0, 1]\f$
src/basefn.F90:125:!> @param[in] lrad radial resolution
src/basefn.F90:126:!> @param[in] mpol poloidal resolution
src/basefn.F90:127:!> @param[out] zernike the value, first derivative of Zernike polynomial
src/basefn.F90:187:!> \brief Get the Zernike polynomials  \f$\hat{R}^{m}_{l}\f$ with zeroth, first, second derivatives
src/basefn.F90:188:!>
src/basefn.F90:189:!> See get_zernike for more detail.
src/basefn.F90:190:!>
src/basefn.F90:191:!> @param[in] r coordinate input, note that this is normalized to \f$[0, 1]\f$
src/basefn.F90:192:!> @param[in] lrad radial resolution
src/basefn.F90:193:!> @param[in] mpol poloidal resolution
src/basefn.F90:194:!> @param[out] zernike the value, first/second derivative of Zernike polynomial

src/bfield.F90:9:!> \brief Returns \f$\dot s \equiv B^s / B^\zeta\f$ and \f$\dot \theta \equiv B^\theta / B^\zeta\f$.
src/bfield.F90:11:!> \brief Compute the magnetic field.
src/bfield.F90:12:!> \ingroup grp_diagnostics
src/bfield.F90:13:!>
src/bfield.F90:14:!> Returns the magnetic field field line equations, \f$ d{\bf x}/d\phi = {\bf B}/B^\phi \f$ .
src/bfield.F90:15:!>
src/bfield.F90:16:!> **Equations of field line flow**
src/bfield.F90:17:!> <ul>
src/bfield.F90:18:!> <li> The equations for the fieldlines are normalized to the toroidal field, i.e.
src/bfield.F90:19:!>       \f{equation}{ \dot s      \equiv \frac{B^s     }{B^\zeta}, \qquad
src/bfield.F90:20:!>                     \dot \theta \equiv \frac{B^\theta}{B^\zeta}. \label{eq:stdot_bfield} \f} </li>
src/bfield.F90:21:!> </ul>
src/bfield.F90:22:!>
src/bfield.F90:23:!> **Representation of magnetic field**
src/bfield.F90:24:!> <ul>
src/bfield.F90:25:!> <li> The components of the vector potential, \f${\bf A}=A_\theta \nabla + A_\zeta \nabla \zeta\f$, are
src/bfield.F90:26:!>      \f{eqnarray}{
src/bfield.F90:27:!>        A_\theta(s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{red}  A_{\theta,e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \label{eq:At_bfield} \\
src/bfield.F90:28:!>        A_\zeta( s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{blue} A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \label{eq:Az_bfield}
src/bfield.F90:29:!>      \f}
src/bfield.F90:30:!>      where \f${\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \equiv \bar s^{m_i/2} \, T_l(s)\f$, \f$T_l(s)\f$ is the Chebyshev polynomial, and \f$\alpha_j \equiv m_j\theta-n_j\zeta\f$.
src/bfield.F90:31:!>      The regularity factor, \f$\bar s^{m_i/2}\f$, where \f$\bar s \equiv (1+s)/2\f$, is only included if there is a coordinate singularity in the domain
src/bfield.F90:32:!>      (i.e. only in the innermost volume, and only in cylindrical and toroidal geometry.) </li>
src/bfield.F90:33:!> <li> The magnetic field, \f$\sqrt g \, {\bf B} = \sqrt g B^s {\bf e}_s + \sqrt g B^\theta {\bf e}_\theta + \sqrt g B^\zeta {\bf e}_\zeta\f$, is
src/bfield.F90:34:!>      \f{eqnarray}{
src/bfield.F90:35:!>        \begin{array}{ccccrcrcrcrcccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/bfield.F90:36:!>        \sqrt g \, {\bf B} & = & {\bf e}_s      & \sum_{i,l} [ ( & - m_i {\color{blue} A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} & - & n_i {\color{red}  A_{\theta,e,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}        \sin\alpha_i + ( & + m_i {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} & + & n_i {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}        \cos\alpha_i ] \\
src/bfield.F90:37:!>                           & + & {\bf e}_\theta & \sum_{i,l} [ ( &                                       & - &     {\color{blue} A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \cos\alpha_i + ( &                                          & - &     {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \sin\alpha_i ] \\
src/bfield.F90:38:!>                           & + & {\bf e}_\zeta  & \sum_{i,l} [ ( &       {\color{red}  A_{\theta,e,i,l}} &   &                                     & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \cos\alpha_i + ( &       {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} &   &                                        & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \sin\alpha_i ]
src/bfield.F90:39:!>        \end{array}
src/bfield.F90:40:!>      \f}
src/bfield.F90:41:!> </li>
src/bfield.F90:42:!> <li> In Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:stdot_bfield})\f$ , the coordinate Jacobian, \f$\sqrt g\f$, cancels.
src/bfield.F90:43:!>   No coordinate metric information is required to construct the fieldline equations from the magnetic vector potential. </li>
src/bfield.F90:44:!> </ul>
src/bfield.F90:45:!> IT IS REQUIRED TO SET IVOL THROUGH GLOBAL MEMORY BEFORE CALLING BFIELD.
src/bfield.F90:46:!>
src/bfield.F90:47:!> The format of this subroutine is constrained by the NAG ode integration routines.

src/bnorml.F90:1:!> \defgroup grp_free-boundary Free-Boundary Computation
src/bnorml.F90:2:!>
src/bnorml.F90:3:!> \file
src/bnorml.F90:4:!> \brief Computes \f${\bf B}_{Plasma} \cdot {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta \;\f$ on the computational boundary, \f$\partial {\cal D}\f$.
src/bnorml.F90:50:!> @brief Computes \f${\bf B}_{Plasma} \cdot {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta \;\f$ on the computational boundary, \f$\partial {\cal D}\f$.
src/bnorml.F90:51:!> \ingroup grp_free-boundary
src/bnorml.F90:52:!>
src/bnorml.F90:53:!> **free-boundary constraint**
src/bnorml.F90:54:!> <ul>
src/bnorml.F90:55:!> <li> The normal field at the computational boundary, \f$\partial {\cal D}\f$, should be equal to
src/bnorml.F90:56:!>      \f$\left({\bf B}_P + {\bf B}_C\right)\cdot {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta\f$,
src/bnorml.F90:57:!>      where \f${\bf B}_P\f$ is the "plasma" field (produced by internal plasma currents) and is computed using virtual casing,
src/bnorml.F90:58:!>      and \f${\bf B}_C\f$ is the "vacuum" field (produced by the external coils) and is given on input. </li>
src/bnorml.F90:59:!> <li> The plasma field, \f${\bf B}_P\f$, can only be computed after the equilibrium is determined,
src/bnorml.F90:60:!>      but this information is required to compute the equilibrium to begin with; and so there is an iteration involved. </li>
src/bnorml.F90:61:!> <li> Suggested values of the vacuum field can be self generated; see xspech() for more documentation on this. </li>
src/bnorml.F90:62:!> </ul>
src/bnorml.F90:63:!>
src/bnorml.F90:64:!> **compute the normal field on a regular grid on the computational boundary**
src/bnorml.F90:65:!> <ul>
src/bnorml.F90:66:!> <li> For each point on the compuational boundary, casing() is called to compute the normal field produced by the plasma currents. </li>
src/bnorml.F90:67:!> <li> \todo There is a very clumsy attempt to parallelize this which could be greatly improved.
src/bnorml.F90:68:!>
src/bnorml.F90:69:!> </li>
src/bnorml.F90:70:!> <li> An FFT gives the required Fourier harmonics. </li>
src/bnorml.F90:71:!> </ul>
src/bnorml.F90:72:!> \see casing.f90
src/bnorml.F90:73:!>
src/bnorml.F90:74:!> @param[in] mn total number of Fourier harmonics
src/bnorml.F90:75:!> @param[in] Ntz total number of grid points in \f$\theta\f$ and \f$zeta\f$
src/bnorml.F90:76:!> @param[out] efmn even Fourier coefficients
src/bnorml.F90:77:!> @param[out] ofmn odd Fouier coefficients
src/brcast.F90:1:!> \defgroup grp_parallel Parallelization
src/brcast.F90:2:!>
src/brcast.F90:3:!> \file
src/brcast.F90:4:!> \brief Broadcasts Beltrami fields, profiles, . . .
src/brcast.F90:6:!> \ingroup grp_parallel
src/brcast.F90:7:!> \brief Broadcasts Beltrami fields, profiles, . . .
src/brcast.F90:8:!>
src/brcast.F90:9:!> **broadcasting**
src/brcast.F90:10:!> <ul>
src/brcast.F90:11:!> <li> The construction of the Beltrami fields is distributed on separate cpus. </li>
src/brcast.F90:12:!> <li> All "local" information needs to be broadcast so that the "global" force vector,
src/brcast.F90:13:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf F}_i \equiv [[p+B^2/2]]_i = (p+B^2/2)_{v,i} - (p+B^2/2)_{v-1,i}
src/brcast.F90:14:!>       \f}
src/brcast.F90:15:!>       can be constructed, and so that restart and output files can be saved to file. </li>
src/brcast.F90:16:!> </ul>
src/brcast.F90:17:!> @param[in] lvol index of nested volume
src/casing.F90:2:!> \file
src/casing.F90:3:!> \brief Constructs the field created by the plasma currents, at an arbitrary, external location using virtual casing.
src/casing.F90:5:!> \brief Constructs the field created by the plasma currents, at an arbitrary, external location using virtual casing.
src/casing.F90:6:!> \ingroup grp_free-boundary
src/casing.F90:7:!>
src/casing.F90:8:!> Compute the external magnetic field using virtual casing.
src/casing.F90:9:!>
src/casing.F90:10:!> **Theory and numerics**
src/casing.F90:11:!>
src/casing.F90:12:!> <ul>
src/casing.F90:13:!> <li> Required inputs to this subroutine are the geometry of the plasma boundary,
src/casing.F90:14:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf x}(\theta,\zeta) \equiv x(\theta,\zeta) {\bf i} + y(\theta,\zeta) {\bf j} + z(\theta,\zeta) {\bf k},
src/casing.F90:15:!>      \f}
src/casing.F90:16:!>      and the tangential field on this boundary,
src/casing.F90:17:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf B}_s=B^\theta {\bf e}_\theta + B^\zeta {\bf e}_\zeta,
src/casing.F90:18:!>      \f}
src/casing.F90:19:!>      where \f$\theta\f$ and \f$\zeta\f$ are arbitrary poloidal and toroidal angles,
src/casing.F90:20:!>      and \f${\bf e}_\theta \equiv \partial {\bf x}/\partial \theta\f$, \f${\bf e}_\zeta \equiv \partial {\bf x}/\partial \zeta\f$.
src/casing.F90:21:!>      This routine assumes that the plasma boundary is a flux surface, i.e. \f${\bf B} \cdot {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta = 0\f$. </li>
src/casing.F90:22:!> <li> The virtual casing principle (Shafranov & Zakharov (1972) \cite y1972_shafranov, Lazerson (2012) \cite y2012_lazerson and Hanson (2015) \cite y2015_hanson)
src/casing.F90:23:!>      shows that the field outside/inside the plasma arising from plasma currents inside/outside the boundary
src/casing.F90:24:!>      is equivalent to the field generated by a surface current,
src/casing.F90:25:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf j} = {\bf B}_s \times {\bf n},
src/casing.F90:26:!>      \f}
src/casing.F90:27:!>      where \f${\bf n}\f$ is normal to the surface. </li>
src/casing.F90:28:!> <li> The field at some arbitrary point, \f$\bar {\bf x}\f$, created by this surface current is given by
src/casing.F90:29:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf B}({\bf \bar x}) = -\frac{1}{4\pi} \int_{\cal S} \frac{\left( {\bf B}_s \times d{\bf s} \right) \times {\bf \hat r}}{r^2},
src/casing.F90:30:!>      \f}
src/casing.F90:31:!>      where \f$d{\bf s} \equiv {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta \; d\theta d\zeta\f$. </li>
src/casing.F90:32:!> <li> For ease of notation introduce
src/casing.F90:33:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf J} & \equiv & {\bf B}_s \times d{\bf s} = \alpha \; {\bf e}_\theta - \beta \; {\bf e}_\zeta,
src/casing.F90:34:!>      \f}
src/casing.F90:35:!>      where \f$\alpha \equiv B_\zeta =  B^\theta g_{\theta\zeta} + B^\zeta g_{\zeta\zeta}\f$ and \f$\beta \equiv B_\theta = B^\theta g_{\theta\theta} + B^\zeta g_{\theta\zeta}\f$. </li>
src/casing.F90:36:!> <li> We may write in Cartesian coordinates \f${\bf J} = j_x \; {\bf i} + j_y \; {\bf j} + j_z \; {\bf k}\f$, where
src/casing.F90:37:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ j_x     &    =   & \alpha \; x_\theta - \beta \; x_\zeta \\
src/casing.F90:38:!>                    j_y     &    =   & \alpha \; y_\theta - \beta \; y_\zeta \\
src/casing.F90:39:!>                    j_z     &    =   & \alpha \; z_\theta - \beta \; z_\zeta .
src/casing.F90:40:!>      \f} </li>
src/casing.F90:41:!> <li> Requiring that the current,
src/casing.F90:42:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf j} & \equiv & \nabla \times {\bf B}
src/casing.F90:43:!>                      =      {\sqrt g}^{-1}(\partial_\theta B_\zeta -\partial_\zeta  B_\theta) \; {\bf e}_s
src/casing.F90:44:!>                      +      {\sqrt g}^{-1}(\partial_\zeta  B_s     -\partial_s      B_\zeta ) \; {\bf e}_\theta
src/casing.F90:45:!>                      +      {\sqrt g}^{-1}(\partial_s      B_\theta-\partial_\theta B_s     ) \; {\bf e}_\zeta,
src/casing.F90:46:!>      \f}
src/casing.F90:47:!>      has no normal component to the surface, i.e. \f${\bf j}\cdot \nabla s=0\f$,
src/casing.F90:48:!>      we obtain the condition \f$\partial_\theta B_\zeta = \partial_\zeta B_\theta\f$, or \f$\partial_\theta \alpha = \partial_\zeta \beta\f$.
src/casing.F90:49:!>      In axisymmetric configurations, where \f$\partial_\zeta \beta=0\f$, we must have \f$\partial_\theta \alpha=0\f$. </li>
src/casing.F90:50:!> <li> The displacement from an arbitrary point, \f$(X,Y,Z)\f$, to a point, \f$(x,y,z)\f$, that lies on the surface is given
src/casing.F90:51:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf r} \equiv r_x \; {\bf i} + r_y \; {\bf j} + r_z \; {\bf k} = (X-x) \; {\bf i} + (Y-y) \; {\bf j} + (Z-z) \; {\bf k}.
src/casing.F90:52:!>      \f} </li>
src/casing.F90:53:!> <li> The components of the magnetic field produced by the surface current are then
src/casing.F90:54:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ B^x &=& \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, (j_y r_z - j_z r_y)/r^3,\\
src/casing.F90:55:!>                    B^y &=& \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, (j_z r_x - j_x r_z)/r^3,\\
src/casing.F90:56:!>                    B^z &=& \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, (j_x r_y - j_y r_x)/r^3
src/casing.F90:57:!>      \f}
src/casing.F90:58:!>      up to a scaling factor \c virtualcasingfactor  \f$=-1/4\pi\f$ that is taken into account at the end. </li>
src/casing.F90:59:!> <li> When all is said and done, this routine calculates
src/casing.F90:60:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ \int_0^{2\pi} \int_0^{2\pi} \verb+vcintegrand+ \;\; d\theta d\zeta
src/casing.F90:61:!>      \f}
src/casing.F90:62:!>      for a given \f$(X,Y,Z)\f$, where \c vcintegrand  is given in Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:integrand_casing})\f$. </li>
src/casing.F90:63:!> <li> The surface integral is performed using \c DCUHRE , which
src/casing.F90:64:!>      uses an adaptive subdivision strategy and also computes absolute error estimates.
src/casing.F90:65:!>      The absolute and relative accuracy required are provided by the \c inputvar \c vcasingtol .
src/casing.F90:66:!>      The minimum number of function evaluations is provided by the \c inputvar \c vcasingits. </li>
src/casing.F90:67:!> </ul>
src/casing.F90:68:!>
src/casing.F90:69:!> **Calculation of integrand**
src/casing.F90:70:!>
src/casing.F90:71:!> <ul>
src/casing.F90:72:!> <li> An adaptive integration is used to compute the integrals.
src/casing.F90:73:!>      Consequently, the magnetic field tangential to the plasma boundary is required at an arbitrary point.
src/casing.F90:74:!>      This is computed, as always, from \f${\bf B} = \nabla \times {\bf A}\f$, and this provides \f${\bf B} = B^\theta {\bf e}_\theta + B^\zeta {\bf e}_\zeta\f$.
src/casing.F90:75:!>      Recall that \f$B^s=0\f$ by construction on the plasma boundary.
src/casing.F90:76:!>      \todo It would be MUCH faster to only require the tangential field on a regular grid!!!
src/casing.F90:77:!>
src/casing.F90:78:!> </li>
src/casing.F90:79:!> <li> Then, the metric elements \f$g_{\theta\theta}\f$, \f$g_{\theta\zeta}\f$ and \f$g_{\zeta\zeta}\f$ are computed.
src/casing.F90:80:!>      These are used to "lower" the components of the magnetic field, \f${\bf B} = B_\theta \nabla \theta + B_\zeta \nabla \zeta\f$.
src/casing.F90:81:!>      \todo Please check why \f$B_s\f$ is not computed. Is it because \f$B_s \nabla s \times {\bf n} = 0\f$ ?
src/casing.F90:82:!>
src/casing.F90:83:!> </li>
src/casing.F90:84:!> <li> The distance between the "evaluate" point, \f$(X,Y,Z)\f$, and the given point on the surface, \f$(x,y,z)\f$ is computed. </li>
src/casing.F90:85:!> <li> If the computational boundary becomes too close to the plasma boundary, the distance is small and this causes problems for the numerics.
src/casing.F90:86:!>      I have tried to regularize this problem by introducing \f$\epsilon \equiv \f$\c inputvar \c vcasingeps.
src/casing.F90:87:!>      Let the "distance" be
src/casing.F90:88:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ D \equiv \sqrt{(X-x)^2 + (Y-y)^2 + (Z-Z)^2} + \epsilon^2.
src/casing.F90:89:!>      \f} </li>
src/casing.F90:90:!> <li> On taking the limit that \f$\epsilon \rightarrow 0\f$, the virtual casing integrand is
src/casing.F90:91:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \verb+vcintegrand+ \equiv ( B_x n_x + B_y n_y + B_z n_z ) ( 1 + 3 \epsilon^2 / D^2 ) / D^3, \label{eq:integrand_casing}
src/casing.F90:92:!>       \f}
src/casing.F90:93:!>      where the normal vector is \f${\bf n} \equiv n_x {\bf i} + n_y {\bf j} + n_z {\bf k}\f$.
src/casing.F90:94:!>      The normal vector, \c Nxyz , to the computational boundary (which does not change) is computed in preset().
src/casing.F90:95:!>      \todo This needs to be revised.
src/casing.F90:96:!>
src/casing.F90:97:!> </li>
src/casing.F90:98:!> </ul>
src/casing.F90:99:!>
src/casing.F90:100:!> @param[in] teta \f$\theta\f$
src/casing.F90:101:!> @param[in] zeta \f$\zeta\f$
src/casing.F90:102:!> @param[out] gBn \f$ \sqrt g {\bf B} \cdot {\bf n}\f$
src/casing.F90:103:!> @param[out] icasing return flag from dcuhre()
src/casing.F90:256:!> @brief Differential virtual casing integrand
src/casing.F90:257:!> \ingroup grp_free-boundary
src/casing.F90:258:!>
src/casing.F90:259:!> Differential virtual casing integrand
src/casing.F90:260:!>
src/casing.F90:261:!> @param[in] Ndim number of parameters (==2)
src/casing.F90:262:!> @param[in] tz \f$\theta\f$ and \f$\zeta\f$
src/casing.F90:263:!> @param[in] Nfun number of function values (==3)
src/casing.F90:264:!> @param[out] vcintegrand cartesian components of magnetic field
src/constants_mod.F90:1:!> \ingroup grp_global
src/constants_mod.F90:2:!> \brief some constants used throughout the code
src/coords.F90:1:!> \defgroup grp_geometry Geometry
src/coords.F90:2:!>
src/coords.F90:3:!> \file
src/coords.F90:4:!> \brief Calculates coordinates, \f${\bf x}(s,\theta,\zeta) \equiv R \, {\bf e}_R + Z \, {\bf e}_Z\f$, and metrics, using FFTs.
src/coords.F90:6:!> \brief Calculates coordinates, \f${\bf x}(s,\theta,\zeta) \equiv R \, {\bf e}_R + Z \, {\bf e}_Z\f$, and metrics, using FFTs.
src/coords.F90:7:!> \ingroup grp_geometry
src/coords.F90:8:!>
src/coords.F90:9:!> **Coordinates**
src/coords.F90:10:!> <ul>
src/coords.F90:11:!> <li>  We work in coordinates, \f$(s,\theta,\zeta)\f$, which are be defined _inversely_ via a transformation _to_ Cartesian coordinates, \f$(x,y,z)\f$. </li>
src/coords.F90:12:!> <li>  The toroidal angle, \f$\zeta\f$, is identical to the cylindrical angle, \f$\zeta\equiv\phi\f$. </li>
src/coords.F90:13:!> <li>  The radial coordinate, \f$s\f$, is _not_ a global variable: it only needs to be defined in each volume, and in each volume \f$s \in [-1,1]\f$. </li>
src/coords.F90:14:!> <li>  The choice of poloidal angle, \f$\theta\f$, does not affect the following. </li>
src/coords.F90:15:!> </ul>
src/coords.F90:16:!>
src/coords.F90:17:!> **Geometry**
src/coords.F90:18:!> <ul>
src/coords.F90:19:!> <li>  The geometry of the "ideal"-interfaces, \f${\bf x}_v(\theta,\zeta)\f$, is given by \f$R(\theta,\zeta)\f$ and \f$Z(\theta,\zeta)\f$ as follows:
src/coords.F90:20:!>       <ul>
src/coords.F90:21:!>       <li>  \c Igeometry=1 : Cartesian
src/coords.F90:22:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf x} & \equiv & r_{pol}\theta \; {\bf \hat i} + r_{tor}\zeta \; {\bf \hat j}+ R \; {\bf \hat k}
src/coords.F90:23:!>       \f}
src/coords.F90:24:!>       where \f$r_{pol}\f$ and \f$r_{tor}\f$ are inputs and \f$r_{pol}=r_{tor}=1\f$ by default. </li>
src/coords.F90:25:!>       <li>  \c Igeometry=2 : Cylindrical
src/coords.F90:26:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf x} & = & R \; \cos\theta \; {\bf \hat i} + R \; \sin\theta \; {\bf \hat j} + \zeta \; {\bf \hat k}
src/coords.F90:27:!>       \f} </li>
src/coords.F90:28:!>       <li>  \c Igeometry=3 : Toroidal
src/coords.F90:29:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf x} & \equiv & R \; {\bf \hat r} + Z \; {\bf \hat k}
src/coords.F90:30:!>       \f}
src/coords.F90:31:!>       where \f${\bf \hat    r} \equiv   \cos \phi \; {\bf \hat i} + \sin \phi \; {\bf \hat j}\f$ and
src/coords.F90:32:!>             \f${\bf \hat \phi} \equiv - \sin \phi \; {\bf \hat i} + \cos \phi \; {\bf \hat j}\f$. </li>
src/coords.F90:33:!>       </ul> </li>
src/coords.F90:34:!>
src/coords.F90:35:!> <li>  The geometry of the ideal interfaces is given as Fourier summation: e.g., for stellarator-symmetry
src/coords.F90:36:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ R_v(\theta,\zeta) & \equiv & \sum_j R_{j,v} \cos\alpha_j, \\
src/coords.F90:37:!>           Z_v(\theta,\zeta) & \equiv & \sum_j Z_{j,v} \sin\alpha_j,
src/coords.F90:38:!>       \f}
src/coords.F90:39:!>       where \f$\alpha_j \equiv m_j \theta - n_j \zeta\f$. </li>
src/coords.F90:40:!>
src/coords.F90:41:!> </ul>
src/coords.F90:42:!>       **interpolation between interfaces**
src/coords.F90:43:!> <ul>
src/coords.F90:44:!> <li>  The "coordinate" functions, \f$R(s,\theta,\zeta)\f$ and \f$Z(s,\theta,\zeta)\f$, are constructed by radially interpolating the Fourier representations of the
src/coords.F90:45:!>       ideal-interfaces. </li>
src/coords.F90:46:!> <li>  The \f$v\f$-th volume is bounded by \f${\bf x}_{v-1}\f$ and \f${\bf x}_{v}\f$. </li>
src/coords.F90:47:!> <li>  In each _annular_ volume, the coordinates are constructed by linear interpolation:
src/coords.F90:48:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \begin{array}{cccccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/coords.F90:49:!>           R(s,\theta,\zeta) & \equiv & \displaystyle \sum_j & \displaystyle \left[ \; \frac{(1-s)}{2} \; R_{j,v-1} + \frac{(1+s)}{2} \; R_{j,v}\; \right] \; \cos\alpha_j,\\
src/coords.F90:50:!>           Z(s,\theta,\zeta) & \equiv & \displaystyle \sum_j & \displaystyle \left[ \; \frac{(1-s)}{2} \; Z_{j,v-1} + \frac{(1+s)}{2} \; Z_{j,v}\; \right] \; \sin\alpha_j,
src/coords.F90:51:!>           \end{array}
src/coords.F90:52:!>       \f} </li>
src/coords.F90:53:!> </ul>
src/coords.F90:54:!>       **coordinate singularity: regularized extrapolation**
src/coords.F90:55:!> <ul>
src/coords.F90:56:!>
src/coords.F90:57:!> <li>  For cylindrical or toroidal geometry, in the innermost, "simple-torus" volume, the coordinates are constructed by an interpolation that
src/coords.F90:58:!>       "encourages" the interpolated coordinate surfaces to not intersect. </li>
src/coords.F90:59:!> <li>  Introduce \f$\bar s \equiv (s+1)/2\f$, so that in each volume \f$\bar s \in [0,1]\f$, then
src/coords.F90:60:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ R_{j}(s) & = & R_{j,0} + (R_{j,1} - R_{j,0} ) f_j, \\
src/coords.F90:61:!>           Z_{j}(s) & = & Z_{j,0} + (Z_{j,1} - Z_{j,0} ) f_j,
src/coords.F90:62:!>       \f}
src/coords.F90:63:!>       where, in toroidal geometry,
src/coords.F90:64:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/coords.F90:65:!>       f_j \equiv \left\{
src/coords.F90:66:!>       \begin{array}{llcccccccccccccc} \bar s       & , & \mbox{ for } m_j=0, \\
src/coords.F90:67:!>                                       \bar s^{m_j} & , & \mbox{ otherwise.}
src/coords.F90:68:!>       \end{array}\right\}.
src/coords.F90:69:!>       \f} </li>
src/coords.F90:70:!> <li>  Note: The location of the coordinate axis, i.e. the \f$R_{j,0}\f$ and \f$Z_{j,0}\f$,
src/coords.F90:71:!>       is set in the coordinate "packing" and "unpacking" routine, packxi(). </li>
src/coords.F90:72:!> </ul>
src/coords.F90:73:!>       **Jacobian**
src/coords.F90:74:!> <ul>
src/coords.F90:75:!> <li>  The coordinate Jacobian (and some other metric information) is given by
src/coords.F90:76:!>       <ul>
src/coords.F90:77:!>       <li> \c Igeometry=1 : Cartesian
src/coords.F90:78:!>       \f{eqnarray}{     {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta & = & -r_{tor}R_\theta \; \hat {\bf i} -  r_{pol}R_\zeta \; \hat {\bf j} + r_{pol}r_{tor}\hat {\bf k} \\
src/coords.F90:79:!>           \boldsymbol{\xi} \cdot {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta & = &  \delta R \\
src/coords.F90:80:!>           \sqrt g                                           & = &         R_s \ r_{pol} \ r_{tor}
src/coords.F90:81:!>       \f} </li>
src/coords.F90:82:!>       <li>  \c Igeometry=2 : Cylindrical
src/coords.F90:83:!>       \f{eqnarray}{    {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta & = & (R_\theta \sin \theta + R \cos\theta ) \; {\bf \hat i} + (R    \sin \theta - R_\theta \cos\theta ) \; {\bf \hat j} - R R_\zeta \; {\bf \hat k} \\
src/coords.F90:84:!>           \boldsymbol{\xi}\cdot {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta & = & \delta R \; R \\
src/coords.F90:85:!>           \sqrt g                                          & = & R_s \; R
src/coords.F90:86:!>       \f} </li>
src/coords.F90:87:!>       <li>  \c Igeometry=3 : Toroidal
src/coords.F90:88:!>       \f{eqnarray}{    {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta & = & - R \, Z_\theta \, \hat r + (Z_\theta \,R_\zeta - R_\theta \,Z_\zeta) \hat \phi + R \,R_\theta \,\hat z\\
src/coords.F90:89:!>           \boldsymbol{\xi}\cdot {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta & = & R ( \delta Z \; R_\theta - \delta R \; Z_\theta ) \\
src/coords.F90:90:!>            \sqrt g                                         & = & R ( Z_s      \; R_\theta - R_s      \; Z_\theta )
src/coords.F90:91:!>       \f} </li>
src/coords.F90:92:!>       </ul>
src/coords.F90:93:!>       </li>
src/coords.F90:94:!>
src/coords.F90:95:!> </ul>
src/coords.F90:96:!>       **cartesian metrics**
src/coords.F90:97:!> <ul>
src/coords.F90:98:!> <li>  The cartesian metrics are
src/coords.F90:99:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \begin{array}{cccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/coords.F90:100:!>           g_{s     s     } =  R_s      R_s                  ,&
src/coords.F90:101:!>           g_{s     \theta} =  R_s      R_\theta             ,&
src/coords.F90:102:!>           g_{s     \zeta } =  R_s      R_\zeta              ,&
src/coords.F90:103:!>           g_{\theta\theta} =  R_\theta R_\theta + r_{pol}^2 ,&
src/coords.F90:104:!>           g_{\theta\zeta } =  R_\theta R_\zeta              ,&
src/coords.F90:105:!>           g_{\zeta \zeta } =  R_\zeta  R_\zeta  + r_{tor}^2
src/coords.F90:106:!>           \end{array}
src/coords.F90:107:!>       \f} </li>
src/coords.F90:108:!>
src/coords.F90:109:!> </ul>
src/coords.F90:110:!>       **cylindrical metrics**
src/coords.F90:111:!> <ul>
src/coords.F90:112:!> <li>  The cylindrical metrics are
src/coords.F90:113:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \begin{array}{cccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/coords.F90:114:!>           g_{s     s     }  =  R_s      R_s            ,&
src/coords.F90:115:!>           g_{s     \theta}  =  R_s      R_\theta       ,&
src/coords.F90:116:!>           g_{s     \zeta }  =  R_s      R_\zeta        ,&
src/coords.F90:117:!>           g_{\theta\theta}  =  R_\theta R_\theta + R^2 ,&
src/coords.F90:118:!>           g_{\theta\zeta }  =  R_\theta R_\zeta        ,&
src/coords.F90:119:!>           g_{\zeta \zeta }  =  R_\zeta  R_\zeta  + 1
src/coords.F90:120:!>           \end{array}
src/coords.F90:121:!>       \f} </li>
src/coords.F90:122:!>
src/coords.F90:123:!> </ul>
src/coords.F90:124:!>       **logical control**
src/coords.F90:125:!> <ul>
src/coords.F90:126:!> <li>  The logical control is provided by \c Lcurvature as follows:
src/coords.F90:127:!>       <ul>
src/coords.F90:128:!>       <li> \c Lcurvature=0 : only the coordinate transformation is computed, i.e. only \f$R\f$ and \f$Z\f$ are calculated,          e.g. global() </li>
src/coords.F90:129:!>       <li> \c Lcurvature=1 : the Jacobian, \f$\sqrt g \f$, and "lower" metrics, \f$g_{\mu,\nu}\f$, are calculated ,                 e.g. bnorml(), lforce(), curent(), metrix(), sc00aa() </li>
src/coords.F90:130:!>       <li> \c Lcurvature=2 : the "curvature" terms are calculated, by which I mean the second derivatives of the position vector;
src/coords.F90:131:!>                              this information is required for computing the current, \f${\bf j}=\nabla\times\nabla\times{\bf A}\f$, e.g. jo00aa() </li>
src/coords.F90:132:!>       <li> \c Lcurvature=3 : the derivative of the \f$g_{\mu,\nu}/\sqrt g\f$ w.r.t. the interface boundary geometry is calculated,  e.g. metrix(), curent() </li>
src/coords.F90:133:!>       <li> \c Lcurvature=4 : the derivative of the \f$g_{\mu,\nu}\f$ w.r.t. the interface boundary geometry is calculated,          e.g. dforce() </li>
src/coords.F90:134:!>       <li> \c Lcurvature=5 : the derivative of \f$\sqrt{g}\f$ w.r.t. the interface boundary geometry is calculated,                 e.g. rzaxis() </li>
src/coords.F90:135:!>       </ul> </li>
src/coords.F90:136:!> </ul>
src/coords.F90:137:!>
src/coords.F90:138:!> @param[in] lvol specified in which volume to compute coordinates
src/coords.F90:139:!> @param[in] lss radial coordinate \f$s\f$
src/coords.F90:140:!> @param[in] Lcurvature logical control flag
src/coords.F90:141:!> @param[in] Ntz number of points in \f$\theta\f$ and \f$\zeta\f$
src/coords.F90:142:!> @param[in] mn number of Fourier harmonics
src/cputiming_mod.F90:1:!> \brief timing variables
src/cputiming_mod.F90:2:!> \ingroup grp_global
src/curent.F90:1:!> \defgroup grp_currents Plasma Currents
src/curent.F90:2:!>
src/curent.F90:3:!> \file
src/curent.F90:4:!> \brief Computes the plasma current, \f$I \equiv \int B_\theta \, d\theta\f$, and the "linking" current, \f$G \equiv \int B_\zeta \, d\zeta\f$.
src/curent.F90:6:!> \brief Computes the plasma current, \f$I \equiv \int B_\theta \, d\theta\f$, and the "linking" current, \f$G \equiv \int B_\zeta \, d\zeta\f$.
src/curent.F90:7:!> \ingroup grp_currents
src/curent.F90:8:!>
src/curent.F90:9:!> **enclosed currents**
src/curent.F90:10:!>
src/curent.F90:11:!> <ul>
src/curent.F90:12:!> <li>  In the vacuum region, the enclosed currents are given by either surface integrals of the current density or line integrals of the magnetic field,
src/curent.F90:13:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \int_{\cal S} {\bf j}\cdot d{\bf s} = \int_{\partial \cal S} {\bf B}\cdot d{\bf l},
src/curent.F90:14:!>       \f}
src/curent.F90:15:!>       and line integrals are usually easier to compute than surface integrals. </li>
src/curent.F90:16:!> <li>  The magnetic field is given by the curl of the magnetic vector potential, as described in e.g. bfield() . </li>
src/curent.F90:17:!> <li>  The toroidal, plasma current is obtained by taking a "poloidal" loop, \f$d{\bf l}={\bf e}_\theta \, d\theta\f$,
src/curent.F90:18:!>       on the plasma boundary, where \f$B^s=0\f$, to obtain
src/curent.F90:19:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ I   \equiv   \int_{0}^{2\pi} \! {\bf B} \cdot {\bf e}_\theta \, d\theta
src/curent.F90:20:!>                            =     \int_{0}^{2\pi} \! \left( - \partial_s A_\zeta \; \bar g_{\theta\theta} + \partial_s A_\theta \; \bar g_{\theta\zeta} \right) \, d\theta,
src/curent.F90:21:!>       \f}
src/curent.F90:22:!>       where \f$\bar g_{\mu\nu} \equiv g_{\mu\nu} / \sqrt g\f$. </li>
src/curent.F90:23:!> <li>  The poloidal, "linking" current through the torus is obtained by taking a "toroidal" loop, \f$d{\bf l}={\bf e}_\zeta \, d\zeta\f$,
src/curent.F90:24:!>       on the plasma boundary to obtain
src/curent.F90:25:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ G \equiv \int_{0}^{2\pi} \! {\bf B}\cdot {\bf e}_\zeta \, d\zeta
src/curent.F90:26:!>                        =     \int_{0}^{2\pi} \! \left( - \partial_s A_\zeta \; \bar g_{\theta\zeta} + \partial_s A_\theta \; \bar g_{\zeta\zeta} \right) \, d\zeta.
src/curent.F90:27:!>       \f} </li>
src/curent.F90:28:!> </ul>
src/curent.F90:29:!>
src/curent.F90:30:!> **Fourier integration**
src/curent.F90:31:!>
src/curent.F90:32:!> <ul>
src/curent.F90:33:!> <li>  Using \f$f \equiv - \partial_s A_\zeta \; \bar g_{\theta\theta} + \partial_s A_\theta \; \bar g_{\theta\zeta}\f$,
src/curent.F90:34:!>       the integral for the plasma current is
src/curent.F90:35:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ I = {\sum_i}^\prime f_i \cos(n_i \zeta) 2\pi, \label{eq:plasmacurrent_curent}
src/curent.F90:36:!>       \f}
src/curent.F90:37:!>       where \f$\sum^\prime\f$ includes only the \f$m_i=0\f$ harmonics. </li>
src/curent.F90:38:!> <li>  Using \f$g \equiv - \partial_s A_\zeta \; \bar g_{\theta\zeta} + \partial_s A_\theta \; \bar g_{\zeta\zeta}\f$,
src/curent.F90:39:!>       the integral for the linking current is
src/curent.F90:40:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ G = {\sum_i}^\prime g_i \cos(m_i \zeta) 2\pi, \label{eq:linkingcurrent_curent}
src/curent.F90:41:!>       \f}
src/curent.F90:42:!>       where \f$\sum^\prime\f$ includes only the \f$n_i=0\f$ harmonics. </li>
src/curent.F90:43:!> <li>  The plasma  current, Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:plasmacurrent_curent})\f$, should be independent of \f$\zeta\f$,
src/curent.F90:44:!>       and the linking current, Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:linkingcurrent_curent})\f$, should be independent of \f$\theta\f$.
src/curent.F90:45:!>       \todo Perhaps this can be proved analytically; in any case it should be confirmed numerically.
src/curent.F90:46:!>
src/curent.F90:47:!> </li>
src/curent.F90:48:!> </ul>
src/curent.F90:49:!>
src/curent.F90:50:!> @param[in] lvol index of volume
src/curent.F90:51:!> @param[in] mn number of Fourier harmonics

src/dforce.F90:4:!> \file
src/dforce.F90:5:!> \brief Calculates \f${\bf F}({\bf x})\f$, where \f${\bf x} \equiv \{\mbox{geometry}\} \equiv \{ R_{i,v}, Z_{i,v}\}\f$
src/dforce.F90:6:!>        and \f${\bf F}\equiv[[p+B^2/2]] + \{\mbox{spectral constraints}\} \f$, and \f$\nabla {\bf F}\f$.
src/dforce.F90:8:!> \brief Calculates \f${\bf F}({\bf x})\f$, where \f${\bf x} \equiv \{\mbox{geometry}\} \equiv \{ R_{i,v}, Z_{i,v}\}\f$
src/dforce.F90:9:!>        and \f${\bf F}\equiv[[p+B^2/2]] + \{\mbox{spectral constraints}\} \f$, and \f$\nabla {\bf F}\f$.
src/dforce.F90:10:!> \ingroup grp_global_force
src/dforce.F90:11:!>
src/dforce.F90:12:!> **unpacking**
src/dforce.F90:13:!>
src/dforce.F90:14:!> <ul>
src/dforce.F90:15:!> <li> The geometrical degrees of freedom are represented as a vector, \f${\bf x} \equiv \{ R_{i,v}, Z_{i,v}\}\f$,
src/dforce.F90:16:!>       where \f$i=1,\f$ \c mn labels the Fourier harmonic and \f$v=1,\f$ \c Mvol\f$-1\f$ is the interface label.
src/dforce.F90:17:!>       This vector is "unpacked" using packxi().
src/dforce.F90:18:!>       (Note that packxi() also sets the coordinate axis, i.e. the \f$R_{i,0}\f$ and \f$Z_{i,0}\f$.) </li>
src/dforce.F90:19:!> </ul>
src/dforce.F90:20:!>
src/dforce.F90:21:!> **Matrices computation**
src/dforce.F90:22:!>
src/dforce.F90:23:!> <ul>
src/dforce.F90:24:!> <li> the volume-integrated metric arrays, \c DToocc, etc. are evaluated in each volume by calling ma00aa() </li>
src/dforce.F90:25:!> <li> the energy and helicity matrices, \c dMA(0:NN,0:NN), \c dMB(0:NN,0:2), etc. are evaluated in each volume by calling matrix() </li>
src/dforce.F90:26:!> </ul>
src/dforce.F90:27:!>
src/dforce.F90:28:!> **parallelization over volumes**
src/dforce.F90:29:!>
src/dforce.F90:30:!> Two different cases emerge: either a local constraint or a global constraint is considered.
src/dforce.F90:31:!> This condition is determined by the flag \c LocalConstraint.
src/dforce.F90:32:!>
src/dforce.F90:33:!> <ul>
src/dforce.F90:34:!> <li> Local constraint
src/dforce.F90:35:!>      <ul>
src/dforce.F90:36:!>      <li> In each volume, \c vvol=1,Mvol ,
src/dforce.F90:37:!>            <ul>
src/dforce.F90:38:!>            <li> the logical array \c ImagneticOK(vvol) is set to \c .false. </li>
src/dforce.F90:39:!>            <li> The MPI node associated to the volume calls dfp100().
src/dforce.F90:40:!>                 This routine calls ma02aa() (and might iterate on mp00ac()) and computes the
src/dforce.F90:41:!>                 field solution in each volume consistent with the constraint. </li>
src/dforce.F90:42:!>            <li> The MPI node associated to the volume calls dfp200().
src/dforce.F90:43:!>                 This computes \f$p+B^2/2\f$ (and the spectral constraints if required) at the interfaces in
src/dforce.F90:44:!>                 each volumes, as well as the derivatives of the force-balance if \c LComputeDerivatives=1. </li>
src/dforce.F90:45:!>            </ul> </li>
src/dforce.F90:46:!>      <li> After the parallelization loop over the volumes, brcast() is called to broadcast the required information. </li>
src/dforce.F90:47:!>      </ul> </li>
src/dforce.F90:48:!> <li> Global constraint <br/>
src/dforce.F90:49:!>      The MPI node \f$0\f$ minimizes the constraint with HYBRID1() by iterating on dfp100() until the field matches the constraint.
src/dforce.F90:50:!>      Other MPI nodes enter the subroutine loop_dfp100(). In loop_dfp100(), each MPI node
src/dforce.F90:51:!>      <ul>
src/dforce.F90:52:!>      <li> calls dfp100(), </li>
src/dforce.F90:53:!>      <li> solves the field in its associated volumes, </li>
src/dforce.F90:54:!>      <li> communicates the field to the node \f$0\f$ and </li>
src/dforce.F90:55:!>      <li> repeats this loop until the node \f$0\f$ sends a flag \c iflag=5. </li>
src/dforce.F90:56:!>      </ul> </li>
src/dforce.F90:57:!> </ul>
src/dforce.F90:58:!>
src/dforce.F90:59:!> **broadcasting**
src/dforce.F90:60:!>
src/dforce.F90:61:!> <ul>
src/dforce.F90:62:!> <li> The required quantities are broadcast by brcast(). </li>
src/dforce.F90:63:!> </ul>
src/dforce.F90:64:!>
src/dforce.F90:65:!> **construction of force**
src/dforce.F90:66:!>
src/dforce.F90:67:!> <ul>
src/dforce.F90:68:!> <li> The force vector, \f${\bf F}({\bf x})\f$, is a combination of the pressure-imbalance Fourier harmonics, \f$[[p+B^2/2]]_{i,v}\f$,
src/dforce.F90:69:!>       where \f$i\f$ labels Fourier harmonic and \f$v\f$ is the interface label:
src/dforce.F90:70:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ F_{i,v} \equiv \left[ ( p_{v+1}+B^2_{i,v+1}/2 ) - ( p_v + B^2_{i,v}/2 ) \right] \times \texttt{BBweight}_i,
src/dforce.F90:71:!>       \f}
src/dforce.F90:72:!>       where \c BBweight_i is defined in preset() ;
src/dforce.F90:73:!>       and the spectral condensation constraints,
src/dforce.F90:74:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ F_{i,v} \equiv I_{i,v} \times \texttt{epsilon} + S_{i,v,1} \times \texttt{sweight}_v - S_{i,v+1,0} \times \texttt{sweight}_{v+1},
src/dforce.F90:75:!>       \f}
src/dforce.F90:76:!>       where the spectral condensation constraints, \f$I_{i,v}\f$, and the "star-like" poloidal angle constraints, \f$S_{i,v,\pm 1}\f$,
src/dforce.F90:77:!>       are calculated and defined in lforce() ;
src/dforce.F90:78:!>       and the \c sweight\f$_v\f$ are defined in preset(). All quantities local to a volume are computed in dfp200(),
src/dforce.F90:79:!>       information is then broadcasted to the MPI node \f$0\f$ in dforce() and the global force is evaluated. </li>
src/dforce.F90:80:!> </ul>
src/dforce.F90:81:!>
src/dforce.F90:82:!> **prints out the force gradient matrix .hessian**
src/dforce.F90:83:!>
src/dforce.F90:84:!> <ul>

src/dforce.F90:86:!> </ul>
src/dforce.F90:87:!>

src/global.F90:14:!> <ul>
src/global.F90:15:!> <li> The geometry of the \f$l\f$-th interface, for \f$l=0,N\f$ where \f$N\equiv\f$ Nvol, is described by a set of Fourier harmonics,
src/global.F90:16:!>      using an arbitrary poloidal angle,
src/global.F90:17:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ R_l(\theta,\zeta)&=&\sum_{j}R_{j,l}\cos(m_j\theta-n_j\zeta), \\
src/global.F90:18:!>                    Z_l(\theta,\zeta)&=&\sum_{j}Z_{j,l}\sin(m_j\theta-n_j\zeta). \f}
src/global.F90:19:!> <li> These harmonics are read from the \c ext.sp file and come directly after the namelists described above.
src/global.F90:20:!>      The required format is as follows:
src/global.F90:21:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ \begin{array}{ccccccccc}
src/global.F90:22:!>      m_1 & n_1 & R_{1,0} & Z_{1,0} & R_{1,1} & Z_{1,1} & ... & R_{1,N} & Z_{1,N} \\
src/global.F90:23:!>      m_2 & n_2 & R_{2,0} & Z_{2,0} & R_{2,1} & Z_{2,1} & ... & R_{2,N} & Z_{2,N} \\
src/global.F90:24:!>      ... \\
src/global.F90:25:!>      m_j & n_j & R_{j,0} & Z_{j,0} & R_{j,1} & Z_{j,1} & ... & R_{j,N} & Z_{j,N} \\
src/global.F90:26:!>      ...
src/global.F90:27:!>      \end{array}
src/global.F90:28:!>      \f}
src/global.F90:29:!> <li> The coordinate axis corresponds to \f$j=0\f$ and the outermost boundary corresponds to \f$j=\f$ Nvol.
src/global.F90:30:!> <li> An arbitrary selection of harmonics may be inluded in any order, but only those within the range specified by Mpol and Ntor will be used.
src/global.F90:31:!> <li> The geometry of *all* the interfaces, i.e. \f$l=0,N\f$, including the degenerate "coordinate-axis" interface, must be given.

src/hesian.F90:134:!> **construction of Hessian matrix**
src/hesian.F90:135:!> <ul>
src/hesian.F90:136:!> <li> The routine dforce() is used to compute the derivatives, with respect to interface geometry,
src/hesian.F90:137:!>      of the force imbalance harmonics, \f$[[p+B^2/2]]_{j}\f$, which may be considered to be the "physical" constraints,
src/hesian.F90:138:!>      and if \c Igeometry==3 then also the derivatives of the "artificial" spectral constraints, \f$I_j \equiv (R_\theta X + Z_\theta Y)_j\f$. </li>
src/hesian.F90:139:!> <li> The input variable \c Lconstraint determines how the enclosed fluxes, \f$\Delta \psi_t\f$ and \f$\Delta \psi_p\f$,
src/hesian.F90:140:!>      and the helicity multiplier, \f$\mu\f$, vary as the geometry is varied;
src/hesian.F90:141:!>      see global.f90 and mp00ac() for more details. </li>
src/hesian.F90:142:!> </ul>
src/hesian.F90:273:!> **construction of eigenvalues and eigenvectors**
src/hesian.F90:274:!> <ul>
src/hesian.F90:275:!> <li> If \c LHevalues==T then the eigenvalues of the Hessian are computed using the NAG routine \c F02EBF. </li>
src/hesian.F90:276:!> <li> If \c LHevectors==T then the eigenvalues *and* the eigenvectors of the Hessian are computed. </li>
src/hesian.F90:277:!> <li> Note that if \c Igeometry==3, then the derivative-matrix also contains information regarding how the "artificial" spectral constraints
src/hesian.F90:278:!>      vary with geometry; so, the eigenvalues and eigenvectors are not purely "physical". </li>
src/hesian.F90:279:!> <li> The eigenvalues and eigenvectors (if required) are written to the file \c .ext.GF.ev as follows:
src/hesian.F90:280:!>
src/hesian.F90:281:!> ```
src/hesian.F90:282:!> open(hunit,file=trim(get_hidden(ext))//".GF.ev",status="unknown",form="unformatted")
src/hesian.F90:283:!> write(hunit)NGdof,Ldvr,Ldvi        ! integers; if only the eigenvalues were computed then Ldvr=Ldvi=1;
src/hesian.F90:284:!> write(hunit)evalr(1:NGdof)         ! reals   ; real      part of eigenvalues;
src/hesian.F90:285:!> write(hunit)evali(1:NGdof)         ! reals   ; imaginary part of eigenvalues;
src/hesian.F90:286:!> write(hunit)evecr(1:NGdof,1:NGdof) ! reals   ; real      part of eigenvalues; only if Ldvr=NGdof;
src/hesian.F90:287:!> write(hunit)eveci(1:NGdof,1:NGdof) ! reals   ; imaginary part of eigenvalues; only if Ldvi=NGdof;
src/hesian.F90:288:!> close(hunit)
src/hesian.F90:289:!> ```
src/hesian.F90:290:!> </li>
src/hesian.F90:291:!> <li> The eigenvectors are saved in columns of \c evecr, \c eveci, as described by the NAG documentation for \c F02EBF. </li>
src/hesian.F90:292:!> </ul>
