src/jo00aa.F90:6:!>
src/jo00aa.F90:7:!> This routine is called by xspech() as a post diagnostic and only if \c Lcheck==1.
src/jo00aa.F90:8:!>
src/jo00aa.F90:9:!> **construction of current,** \f${\bf j} \equiv \nabla \times \nabla \times {\bf A}\f$
src/jo00aa.F90:10:!> <ul>
src/jo00aa.F90:11:!> <li> The components of the vector potential, \f${\bf A}=A_\theta \nabla + A_\zeta \nabla \zeta\f$, are
src/jo00aa.F90:12:!>      \f{eqnarray}{
src/jo00aa.F90:13:!>        A_\theta(s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{red}  A_{\theta,e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \label{eq:At_jo00aa} \\
src/jo00aa.F90:14:!>        A_\zeta (s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{blue} A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \label{eq:Az_jo00aa}
src/jo00aa.F90:15:!>      \f}
src/jo00aa.F90:16:!>      where \f${\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \equiv \bar s^{m_i/2} \, T_l(s)\f$, \f$T_l(s)\f$ is the Chebyshev polynomial, and \f$\alpha_j \equiv m_j\theta-n_j\zeta\f$.
src/jo00aa.F90:17:!>      The regularity factor, \f$\bar s^{m_i/2}\f$, where \f$\bar s \equiv (1+s)/2\f$, is only included if there is a coordinate singularity in the domain
src/jo00aa.F90:18:!>      (i.e. only in the innermost volume, and only in cylindrical and toroidal geometry.) </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:19:!> <li> The magnetic field, \f$\sqrt g \, {\bf B} = \sqrt g B^s {\bf e}_s + \sqrt g B^\theta {\bf e}_\theta + \sqrt g B^\zeta {\bf e}_\zeta\f$, is
src/jo00aa.F90:20:!>      \f{eqnarray}{
src/jo00aa.F90:21:!>        \begin{array}{ccccrcrcrcrcccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/jo00aa.F90:22:!>        \sqrt g \, {\bf B} & = & {\bf e}_s      & \sum_{i,l} [ ( & - m_i {\color{blue} A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} & - & n_i {\color{red}  A_{\theta,e,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}        \sin\alpha_i + ( & + m_i {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} & + & n_i {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}        \cos\alpha_i ] \\
src/jo00aa.F90:23:!>                           & + & {\bf e}_\theta & \sum_{i,l} [ ( &                                       & - &     {\color{blue} A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \cos\alpha_i + ( &                                          & - &     {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \sin\alpha_i ] \\
src/jo00aa.F90:24:!>                           & + & {\bf e}_\zeta  & \sum_{i,l} [ ( &       {\color{red}  A_{\theta,e,i,l}} &   &                                     & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \cos\alpha_i + ( &       {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} &   &                                        & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \sin\alpha_i ]
src/jo00aa.F90:25:!>        \end{array}
src/jo00aa.F90:26:!>      \f} </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:27:!> <li> The current is
src/jo00aa.F90:28:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ \sqrt g \, {\bf j} = ( \partial_\theta B_\zeta  - \partial_\zeta  B_\theta) \; {\bf e}_s
src/jo00aa.F90:29:!>                                       + ( \partial_\zeta  B_s      - \partial_s      B_\zeta ) \; {\bf e}_\theta
src/jo00aa.F90:30:!>                                       + ( \partial_s      B_\theta - \partial_\theta B_s     ) \; {\bf e}_\zeta ,
src/jo00aa.F90:31:!>      \f}
src/jo00aa.F90:32:!>      where (for computational convenience) the covariant components of \f${\bf B}\f$ are computed as
src/jo00aa.F90:33:!>      \f{eqnarray}{
src/jo00aa.F90:34:!>        B_s      & = & (\sqrt g B^s) \, g_{ss     } / \sqrt g + (\sqrt g B^\theta) \, g_{s     \theta} / \sqrt g + (\sqrt g B^\zeta) \, g_{s     \zeta} / \sqrt g, \\
src/jo00aa.F90:35:!>        B_\theta & = & (\sqrt g B^s) \, g_{s\theta} / \sqrt g + (\sqrt g B^\theta) \, g_{\theta\theta} / \sqrt g + (\sqrt g B^\zeta) \, g_{\theta\zeta} / \sqrt g, \\
src/jo00aa.F90:36:!>        B_\zeta  & = & (\sqrt g B^s) \, g_{s\zeta } / \sqrt g + (\sqrt g B^\theta) \, g_{\theta\zeta } / \sqrt g + (\sqrt g B^\zeta) \, g_{\zeta \zeta} / \sqrt g.
src/jo00aa.F90:37:!>      \f} </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:38:!> </ul>
src/jo00aa.F90:39:!>
src/jo00aa.F90:40:!> **quantification of the error**
src/jo00aa.F90:41:!> <ul>
src/jo00aa.F90:42:!> <li> The measures of the error are
src/jo00aa.F90:43:!>      \f{eqnarray}{
src/jo00aa.F90:44:!>          ||\left( {\bf j}-\mu {\bf B}\right)\cdot\nabla s      || &  \equiv  &
src/jo00aa.F90:45:!>        \int \!\! ds \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \left| \sqrt g \, {\bf j}\cdot\nabla s     -\mu \; \sqrt g \, {\bf B} \cdot\nabla s      \right|, \label{eq:Es_jo00aa} \\
src/jo00aa.F90:46:!>          ||\left( {\bf j}-\mu {\bf B}\right)\cdot\nabla \theta || &  \equiv  &
src/jo00aa.F90:47:!>        \int \!\! ds \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \left| \sqrt g \, {\bf j}\cdot\nabla \theta-\mu \; \sqrt g \, {\bf B} \cdot\nabla \theta \right|, \label{eq:Et_jo00aa} \\
src/jo00aa.F90:48:!>          ||\left( {\bf j}-\mu {\bf B}\right)\cdot\nabla \zeta  || &  \equiv  &
src/jo00aa.F90:49:!>        \int \!\! ds \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \left| \sqrt g \, {\bf j}\cdot\nabla \zeta -\mu \; \sqrt g \, {\bf B} \cdot\nabla \zeta  \right|. \label{eq:Ez_jo00aa}
src/jo00aa.F90:50:!>      \f} </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:51:!> </ul>
src/jo00aa.F90:52:!>
src/jo00aa.F90:53:!> **comments**
src/jo00aa.F90:54:!> <ul>
src/jo00aa.F90:55:!> <li>  Is there a better definition and quantification of the error? For example, should we employ an error measure that is dimensionless? </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:56:!> <li>  If the coordinate singularity is in the domain, then \f$|\nabla\theta|\rightarrow\infty\f$ at the coordinate origin.
src/jo00aa.F90:57:!>       What then happens to \f$||\left( {\bf j}-\mu {\bf B}\right)\cdot\nabla \theta ||\f$ as defined in Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:Et_jo00aa})\f$? </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:58:!> <li>  What is the predicted scaling of the error in the Chebyshev-Fourier representation scale with numerical resolution?
src/jo00aa.F90:59:!>       Note that the predicted error scaling for \f$E^s\f$, \f$E^\theta\f$ and \f$E^\zeta\f$ may not be standard,
src/jo00aa.F90:60:!>       as various radial derivatives are taken to compute the components of \f${\bf j}\f$.
src/jo00aa.F90:61:!>       (See for example the discussion in Sec.IV.C in Hudson et al. (2011) \cite y2011_hudson ,
src/jo00aa.F90:62:!>       where the expected scaling of the error for a finite-element implementation is confirmed numerically.) </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:63:!> <li>  Instead of using Gaussian integration to compute the integral over \f$s\f$, an adaptive quadrature algorithm may be preferable. </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:64:!> </ul>
src/jo00aa.F90:65:!>
src/jo00aa.F90:66:!> @param[in] lvol  in which volume should the Beltrami error be computed
src/jo00aa.F90:67:!> @param[in] Ntz   number of grid points in \f$\theta\f$ and \f$\zeta\f$
src/jo00aa.F90:68:!> @param[in] lquad degree of Gaussian quadrature
src/jo00aa.F90:69:!> @param[in] mn    number of Fourier harmonics
src/jo00aa.F90:119:!> **details of the numerics**
src/jo00aa.F90:120:!> <ul>
src/jo00aa.F90:121:!> <li> The integration over \f$s\f$ is performed using Gaussian integration, e.g., \f$\displaystyle \int \!\! f(s) ds \approx \sum_k \omega_k f(s_k)\f$;
src/jo00aa.F90:122:!>      with the abscissae, \f$s_k\f$, and the weights, \f$\omega_k\f$, for \f$k=1,\f$ \c Iquad\f$_v\f$, determined by \c CDGQF.
src/jo00aa.F90:123:!>      The resolution, \c N \f$ \equiv \f$ \c Iquad\f$_v\f$, is determined by \c Nquad  (see global.f90 and preset() ).
src/jo00aa.F90:124:!>      A fatal error is enforced by jo00aa() if \c CDGQF returns an \c ifail \f$\ne 0\f$. </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:159:!> <li>  Inside the Gaussian quadrature loop, i.e. for each \f$s_k\f$,
src/jo00aa.F90:160:!>       <ul>
src/jo00aa.F90:170:!>       <li>  The metric elements, \f$g_{\mu,\nu} \equiv \f$ \c gij(1:6,0,1:Ntz), and the Jacobian, \f$\sqrt g \equiv \f$ \c sg(0,1:Ntz),
src/jo00aa.F90:171:!>             are calculated on a regular angular grid, \f$(\theta_i,\zeta_j)\f$, in coords().
src/jo00aa.F90:172:!>             The derivatives \f$\partial_i g_{\mu,\nu} \equiv\f$ \c gij(1:6,i,1:Ntz) and \f$\partial_i \sqrt g \equiv \f$ \c sg(i,1:Ntz),
src/jo00aa.F90:173:!>             with respect to \f$ i \in \{ s,\theta,\zeta \}\f$ are also returned. </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:195:!>       <li>  The Fourier components of the vector potential given in Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:At_jo00aa})\f$ and Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:Az_jo00aa})\f$, and their first and second radial derivatives, are summed. </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:233:!>       <li>  The quantities \f$\sqrt g B^s\f$, \f$\sqrt g B^\theta\f$ and \f$\sqrt g B^\zeta\f$, and their first and second derivatives with respect to \f$(s,\theta,\zeta)\f$,
src/jo00aa.F90:234:!>             are computed on the regular angular grid (using FFTs). </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:305:!>       <li>  The following quantities are then computed on the regular angular grid
src/jo00aa.F90:306:!>             \f{eqnarray}{ \sqrt g j^s      & = & \sum_u \left[
src/jo00aa.F90:307:!>                           \partial_\theta (\sqrt g B^u) \; g_{u,\zeta } + (\sqrt g B^u) \; \partial_\theta g_{u,\zeta } - (\sqrt g B^u) g_{u,\zeta } \; \partial_\theta \sqrt g / \sqrt g
src/jo00aa.F90:308:!>                                                    \right] / \sqrt g \nonumber \\
src/jo00aa.F90:309:!>                                       & - & \sum_u \left[
src/jo00aa.F90:310:!>                           \partial_\zeta  (\sqrt g B^u) \; g_{u,\theta} + (\sqrt g B^u) \; \partial_\zeta  g_{u,\theta} - (\sqrt g B^u) g_{u,\theta} \; \partial_\zeta  \sqrt g / \sqrt g
src/jo00aa.F90:311:!>                                                    \right] / \sqrt g, \\
src/jo00aa.F90:312:!>                           \sqrt g j^\theta & = & \sum_u \left[
src/jo00aa.F90:313:!>                           \partial_\zeta  (\sqrt g B^u) \; g_{u,s     } + (\sqrt g B^u) \; \partial_\zeta  g_{u,s     } - (\sqrt g B^u) g_{u,s     } \; \partial_\zeta  \sqrt g / \sqrt g
src/jo00aa.F90:314:!>                                                    \right] / \sqrt g \nonumber \\
src/jo00aa.F90:315:!>                                       & - & \sum_u \left[
src/jo00aa.F90:316:!>                           \partial_s      (\sqrt g B^u) \; g_{u,\zeta } + (\sqrt g B^u) \; \partial_s      g_{u,\zeta } - (\sqrt g B^u) g_{u,\zeta } \; \partial_s      \sqrt g / \sqrt g
src/jo00aa.F90:317:!>                                                    \right] / \sqrt g, \\
src/jo00aa.F90:318:!>                           \sqrt g j^\zeta  & = & \sum_u \left[
src/jo00aa.F90:319:!>                           \partial_s      (\sqrt g B^u) \; g_{u,\theta} + (\sqrt g B^u) \; \partial_s      g_{u,\theta} - (\sqrt g B^u) g_{u,\theta} \; \partial_s      \sqrt g / \sqrt g
src/jo00aa.F90:320:!>                                                    \right] / \sqrt g \nonumber \\
src/jo00aa.F90:321:!>                                       & - & \sum_u \left[
src/jo00aa.F90:322:!>                           \partial_\theta (\sqrt g B^u) \; g_{u,s     } + (\sqrt g B^u) \; \partial_\theta g_{u,s     } - (\sqrt g B^u) g_{u,s     } \; \partial_\theta \sqrt g / \sqrt g
src/jo00aa.F90:323:!>                                                    \right] / \sqrt g.
src/jo00aa.F90:324:!>             \f} </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:325:!>       </ul>
src/jo00aa.F90:394:!> <li>  The error is stored into an array called \c beltramierror which is then written to the HDF5 file in hdfint(). </li>
src/jo00aa.F90:457:!> </ul>
src/lforce.F90:1:!> \defgroup grp_local_force "local" force
src/lforce.F90:2:!>
src/lforce.F90:3:!> \file
src/lforce.F90:4:!> \brief Computes \f$B^2\f$, and the spectral condensation constraints if required, on the interfaces, \f${\cal I}_i\f$.
src/lforce.F90:6:!> \brief Computes \f$B^2\f$, and the spectral condensation constraints if required, on the interfaces, \f${\cal I}_i\f$.
src/lforce.F90:7:!> \ingroup grp_local_force
src/lforce.F90:8:!>
src/lforce.F90:9:!> **field strength**
src/lforce.F90:10:!>
src/lforce.F90:11:!> <ul>
src/lforce.F90:12:!> <li> The field strength is given by \f$B^2 = B^s B_s + B^\theta B_\theta + B^\zeta B_\zeta\f$, and on the interfaces \f$B^s=0\f$ by construction. </li>
src/lforce.F90:13:!> <li> The magnetic field is
src/lforce.F90:14:!>       \f$\sqrt g \; {\bf B} = (\partial_\theta A_\zeta - \partial_\zeta A_\theta ) {\bf e}_s - \partial_s A_\zeta {\bf e}_\theta + \partial_s A_\theta {\bf e}_\zeta\f$. </li>
src/lforce.F90:15:!> <li> The covariant components of the field are computed via \f$B_\theta = B^\theta g_{\theta\theta} + B^\zeta g_{\theta\zeta}\f$ and \f$B_\zeta = B^\theta g_{\theta\zeta} + B^\zeta g_{\zeta\zeta}\f$. </li>
src/lforce.F90:16:!> <li> The expression for \f$B^2\f$ is
src/lforce.F90:17:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/lforce.F90:18:!>       (\sqrt g)^2 B^2 = A_\zeta^\prime \; A_\zeta^\prime \; g_{\theta\theta} - 2 \; A_\zeta^\prime \; A_\theta^\prime \; g_{\theta\zeta} + A_\theta^\prime \; A_\theta^\prime \; g_{\zeta\zeta},
src/lforce.F90:19:!>       \f}
src/lforce.F90:20:!>       where the \f$"\prime"\f$ denotes derivative with respect to \f$s\f$. </li>
src/lforce.F90:21:!> <li> The quantity returned is
src/lforce.F90:22:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ F \equiv \texttt{pscale} \times \frac{P}{V^\gamma} + \frac{B^2}{2},
src/lforce.F90:23:!>       \f}
src/lforce.F90:24:!>       where \f$P\equiv\f$ \c adiabatic  and \f$V\equiv\f$ volume. </li>
src/lforce.F90:25:!> </ul>
src/lforce.F90:26:!>
src/lforce.F90:27:!> **spectral constraints**
src/lforce.F90:28:!>
src/lforce.F90:29:!> <ul>
src/lforce.F90:30:!> <li> In addition to the physical-force-balance constraints, namely that \f$[[p+B^2/2]]=0\f$ across the interfaces,
src/lforce.F90:31:!>       additional angle constraints are required to obtain a unique Fourier representation of the interface geometry. </li>
src/lforce.F90:32:!> <li> Introducing the angle functional: a weighted combination of the "polar" constraint;
src/lforce.F90:33:!>       the normalized, poloidal, spectral width (Hirshman & Meier (1985) \cite y1985_hirshman, Hirshman & Breslau (1998) \cite y1998_hirshman)
src/lforce.F90:34:!>       the poloidal-angle origin constraint; and the "length" of the angle curves
src/lforce.F90:35:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ F \!\equiv\! \sum_{i=1}^{N-1} \alpha_i \underbrace{\oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \!\! \frac{1}{\Theta_{i,\theta}}}_{polar-angle}
src/lforce.F90:36:!>                            +     \sum_{i=1}^{N-1} \beta_i \, \underbrace{\;\; M_i \;\;}_{spectral-width}
src/lforce.F90:37:!>                            +     \sum_{i=1}^{N-1} \gamma_i \int_{0}^{2\pi} \! \frac{1}{2}\left[Z_i(0,\zeta)-Z_{i,0}\right]^2 d\zeta
src/lforce.F90:38:!>                            +     {\color{red}{\oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \!\! \sum_{i=1}^{N} \delta_i \, \underbrace{\;\;\;\; L_i \;\;\;\;}_{poloidal-length}}}
src/lforce.F90:39:!>       \f}
src/lforce.F90:40:!>       where \f$i\f$ labels the interfaces, and
src/lforce.F90:41:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \Theta_{i,\theta} & \equiv & \frac{x \, y_\theta - x_\theta \, y}{x^2+y^2}, \\
src/lforce.F90:42:!>       M_i          & \equiv & \frac{\sum_j m_j^p ( R_{j,i}^2 + Z_{j,i}^2 )}{\sum_j       ( R_{j,i}^2 + Z_{j,i}^2 )} ,\\
src/lforce.F90:43:!>       {\color{red}{L_i}}          & \equiv & {\color{red}{\sqrt{      [R_{i}(\theta,\zeta)-R_{i-1}(\theta,\zeta)]^2+[Z_{i}(\theta,\zeta)-Z_{i-1}(\theta,\zeta)]^2}}},
src/lforce.F90:44:!>       \f}
src/lforce.F90:45:!>       and where \f$j\f$ labels the Fourier harmonics.
src/lforce.F90:46:!>       The \f$\alpha_i\f$, \f$\beta_i\f$, \f$\gamma_i\f$ and \f$\delta_i \equiv \f$ \c sweight are user-supplied weight factors. </li>
src/lforce.F90:47:!> <li> The polar constraint is derived from defining \f$\tan \Theta \equiv y/x\f$, where
src/lforce.F90:48:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ x(\theta,\zeta) &  \equiv &  R_{i}(\theta,\zeta)-R_{i,0}(\zeta), \\
src/lforce.F90:49:!>           y(\theta,\zeta) &  \equiv &  Z_{i}(\theta,\zeta)-Z_{i,0}(\zeta),
src/lforce.F90:50:!>       \f}
src/lforce.F90:51:!>       and where the geometric center of each interface is given by the arc-length weighted integrals, see rzaxis(),
src/lforce.F90:52:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/lforce.F90:53:!>           R_{i,0} & \equiv & \int_0^{2\pi} \!\!\!\! d\theta \; R_i(\theta,\zeta) \sqrt{R_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta)^2+Z_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta)^2}, \\
src/lforce.F90:54:!>           Z_{i,0} & \equiv & \int_0^{2\pi} \!\!\!\! d\theta \; Z_i(\theta,\zeta) \sqrt{R_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta)^2+Z_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta)^2},
src/lforce.F90:55:!>       \f}
src/lforce.F90:56:!>       and \f$\cos \Theta = x / \sqrt{x^2+y^2}\f$ has been used to simplify the expressions and to avoid divide-by-zero. </li>
src/lforce.F90:57:!> <li> Only "poloidal tangential" variations will be allowed to find the extremum of \f$F\f$, which are described by
src/lforce.F90:58:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/lforce.F90:59:!>           \delta R_i(\theta,\zeta) & \equiv & R_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta) \, \delta u_i(\theta,\zeta),\\
src/lforce.F90:60:!>           \delta Z_i(\theta,\zeta) & \equiv & Z_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta) \, \delta u_i(\theta,\zeta),
src/lforce.F90:61:!>       \f}
src/lforce.F90:62:!>       from which it follows that the variation in each Fourier harmonic is
src/lforce.F90:63:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/lforce.F90:64:!>           \delta R_{j,i} = \displaystyle \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, R_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta) \, \delta u_i(\theta,\zeta) \, \cos(m_j\theta-n_j\zeta), \\
src/lforce.F90:65:!>           \delta Z_{j,i} = \displaystyle \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, Z_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta) \, \delta u_i(\theta,\zeta) \, \sin(m_j\theta-n_j\zeta),
src/lforce.F90:66:!>       \f}
src/lforce.F90:67:!>       and
src/lforce.F90:68:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/lforce.F90:69:!>           \delta R_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta) & \equiv & R_{i,\theta\theta}(\theta,\zeta) \, \delta u_i(\theta,\zeta) + R_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta) \, \delta u_{i,\theta} (\theta,\zeta) \\
src/lforce.F90:70:!>           \delta Z_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta) & \equiv & Z_{i,\theta\theta}(\theta,\zeta) \, \delta u_i(\theta,\zeta) + Z_{i,\theta}(\theta,\zeta) \, \delta u_{i,\theta} (\theta,\zeta)
src/lforce.F90:71:!>       \f} </li>
src/lforce.F90:72:!> <li> The variation in \f$F\f$ is
src/lforce.F90:73:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \delta F
src/lforce.F90:74:!>       & = & \sum_{i=1}^{N-1} \alpha_i \;\;\;\oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \!\! \left( \frac{-2\Theta_{i,\theta\theta}}{\Theta_{i,\theta}^2} \right) \delta u_i \nonumber \\
src/lforce.F90:75:!>       & + & \sum_{i=1}^{N-1} \beta_i  \;\;\;\oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \!\! \left( R_{i,\theta} X_i + Z_{i,\theta} Y_i \right) \delta u_i \nonumber \\
src/lforce.F90:76:!>       & + & \sum_{i=1}^{N-1} \gamma_i  \;\;\; \int \! d\zeta \;   \left( Z_{i}(0,\zeta)-Z_{i,0} \right) Z_{i,\theta} \; \delta u_i \nonumber \\
src/lforce.F90:77:!>       & + & {\color{red}{\sum_{i=1}^{N-1} \delta_i \;\;\;\oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \!\! \left( \frac{\Delta R_{i  } R_{i,\theta} + \Delta Z_{i  } Z_{i,\theta}}{L_{i  }} \right)
src/lforce.F90:78:!>             \delta u_i}}
src/lforce.F90:79:!>       \nonumber \\
src/lforce.F90:80:!>       & - & {\color{red}{\sum_{i=1}^{N-1} \delta_{i+1}   \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \!\! \left( \frac{\Delta R_{i+1} R_{i,\theta} + \Delta Z_{i+1} Z_{i,\theta}}{L_{i+1}} \right)
src/lforce.F90:81:!>             \delta u_i}}
src/lforce.F90:82:!>       \label{eq:firstvariation_lforce}
src/lforce.F90:83:!>       \f}
src/lforce.F90:84:!>       where, for the stellarator symmetric case,
src/lforce.F90:85:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/lforce.F90:86:!>           X_i & \equiv & \displaystyle \sum\nolimits_j ( m_j^p - M_i ) \, R_{j,i} \cos(m_j\theta-n_j\zeta), \\
src/lforce.F90:87:!>           Y_i & \equiv & \displaystyle \sum\nolimits_j ( m_j^p - M_i ) \, Z_{j,i} \sin(m_j\theta-n_j\zeta),
src/lforce.F90:88:!>       \f}
src/lforce.F90:89:!>       and
src/lforce.F90:90:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ {\color{red}{\Delta R_{i}}} & \equiv & {\color{red}{R_{i}(\theta,\zeta)-R_{i-1}(\theta,\zeta)}},\\
src/lforce.F90:91:!>           {\color{red}{\Delta Z_{i}}} & \equiv & {\color{red}{Z_{i}(\theta,\zeta)-Z_{i-1}(\theta,\zeta)}},
src/lforce.F90:92:!>       \f} </li>
src/lforce.F90:93:!> <li> The spectral constraints derived from Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:firstvariation_lforce})\f$ are
src/lforce.F90:94:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ I_i(\theta,\zeta) & \equiv & - 2 \alpha_i \frac{\Theta_{i,\theta\theta}}{\Theta_{i,\theta}^{2}}
src/lforce.F90:95:!>               + \beta_i \left( R_{i,\theta} X_i + Z_{i,\theta} Y_i \right)
src/lforce.F90:96:!>               + \gamma_i \left( Z_{i}(0,\zeta) - Z_{i,0} \right) Z_{i,\theta}(0,\zeta) \nonumber \\
src/lforce.F90:97:!>             & + & {\color{red}{\delta_{i  } \frac{\Delta R_{i  } R_{i,\theta} + \Delta Z_{i  } Z_{i,\theta}}{L_i}}}
src/lforce.F90:98:!>               -   {\color{red}{\delta_{i+1} \frac{\Delta R_{i+1} R_{i,\theta} + \Delta Z_{i+1} Z_{i,\theta}}{L_{i+1} } } }
src/lforce.F90:99:!>       \label{eq:spectralconstraints_lforce}
src/lforce.F90:100:!>       \f} </li>
src/lforce.F90:101:!> <li> Note that choosing \f$p=2\f$ gives \f$X=-R_{\theta\theta}\f$ and \f$Y=-Z_{\theta\theta}\f$, and the spectrally condensed angle constraint, \f$R_\theta X + Z_\theta Y=0\f$,
src/lforce.F90:102:!>       becomes \f$\partial_\theta (R_\theta^2+Z_\theta^2)=0\f$,
src/lforce.F90:103:!>       which defines the equal arc length angle. </li>
src/lforce.F90:104:!> <li> The poloidal-angle origin term, namely \f$\gamma_i \left( Z_{i}(0,\zeta) - Z_{i,0} \right) Z_{i,\theta}(0,\zeta)\f$
src/lforce.F90:105:!>       is only used to constrain the \f$m_j=0\f$ harmonics. </li>
src/lforce.F90:106:!> <li> The construction of the angle functional was influenced by the following considerations: </li>
src/lforce.F90:107:!>       <ul>
src/lforce.F90:108:!>       <li>The minimal spectral width constraint is very desirable as it reduces the required Fourier resolution,
src/lforce.F90:109:!>             but it does not constrain the \f$m=0\f$ harmonics
src/lforce.F90:110:!>             and the minimizing spectral-width poloidal-angle may not be consistent with the poloidal angle used on adjacent interfaces. </li>
src/lforce.F90:111:!>       <li>  The regularization of the vector potential and the coordinate interpolation near the coordinate origin (see elsewhere)
src/lforce.F90:112:!>             assumes that the poloidal angle is the polar angle. </li>
src/lforce.F90:113:!>       <li> The user will provide the Fourier harmonics of the boundary, and thus the user will implicitly define the poloidal angle used on the boundary. </li>
src/lforce.F90:114:!>       <li>  Minimizing the length term will ensure that the poloidal angle used on each interface
src/lforce.F90:115:!>             is smoothly connected to the poloidal angle used on adjacent interfaces. </li>
src/lforce.F90:116:!>       </ul> </li>
src/lforce.F90:117:!> <li> A suitable choice of the weight factors, \f$\alpha_i\f$, \f$\beta_i\f$, \f$\gamma_i\f$ and \f$\delta_i\f$, will ensure
src/lforce.F90:118:!>       that the polar constraint dominates for the innermost surfaces and that this constraint rapidly becomes insignificant away from the origin;
src/lforce.F90:119:!>       that the minimal spectral constraint dominates in the "middle";
src/lforce.F90:120:!>       and that the minimizing length constraint will be significant near the origin and dominant near the edge,
src/lforce.F90:121:!>       so that the minimizing spectral width angle will be continuously connected to the polar angle on the innermost surfaces
src/lforce.F90:122:!>       and the user-implied angle at the plasma boundary.
src/lforce.F90:123:!>       The length constraint should not be insignificant where the spectral constraint is dominant (so that the \f$m=0\f$ harmonics are constrained). </li>
src/lforce.F90:124:!> <li> The polar constraint does not need normalization.
src/lforce.F90:125:!>       The spectral width constraint has already been normalized.
src/lforce.F90:126:!>       The length constraint is not yet normalized, but perhaps it should be. </li>
src/lforce.F90:127:!> <li> The spectral constraints given in Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:spectralconstraints_lforce})\f$ need to be differentiated
src/lforce.F90:128:!>       with respect to the interface Fourier harmonics, \f$R_{j,i}\f$ and \f$Z_{j,i}\f$.
src/lforce.F90:129:!>       The first and second terms lead to a block diagonal hessian, and the length term leads to a block tri-diagonal hessian. </li>
src/lforce.F90:130:!> <li> Including the poloidal-angle origin constraint means that the polar angle constraint can probably be ignored, i.e. \f$\alpha_i=0\f$. </li>
src/lforce.F90:131:!> </ul>
src/lforce.F90:132:!>
src/lforce.F90:133:!> @param[in] lvol
src/lforce.F90:134:!> @param[in] iocons
src/lforce.F90:135:!> @param[in] ideriv
src/lforce.F90:136:!> @param[in] Ntz
src/lforce.F90:137:!> @param dBB
src/lforce.F90:138:!> @param XX
src/lforce.F90:139:!> @param YY
src/lforce.F90:140:!> @param length
src/lforce.F90:141:!> @param DDl
src/lforce.F90:142:!> @param MMl
src/lforce.F90:143:!> @param[in] iflag

src/ma02aa.F90:1:!> \defgroup grp_solver_driver Solver/Driver
src/ma02aa.F90:2:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:3:!> \file
src/ma02aa.F90:4:!> \brief Constructs Beltrami field in given volume consistent with flux, helicity, rotational-transform and/or parallel-current constraints.
src/ma02aa.F90:6:!> \brief Constructs Beltrami field in given volume consistent with flux, helicity, rotational-transform and/or parallel-current constraints.
src/ma02aa.F90:7:!> \ingroup grp_solver_driver
src/ma02aa.F90:8:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:9:!> @param[in] lvol index of nested volume for which to run this
src/ma02aa.F90:10:!> @param[in] NN   number of degrees of freedom in the (packed format) vector potential;
src/ma02aa.F90:49:!> **linear method**
src/ma02aa.F90:51:!> <ul>
src/ma02aa.F90:52:!> <li> The quantity \f$\mu\f$ is *not* not treated as a "magnetic" degree-of-freedom
src/ma02aa.F90:53:!>       equivalent to in the degrees-of-freedom in the magnetic vector potential
src/ma02aa.F90:54:!>       (as it strictly should be, because it is a Lagrange multiplier introduced to enforce the helicity constraint). </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:55:!> <li> In this case, the Beltrami equation, \f$\nabla \times {\bf B} = \mu {\bf B}\f$, is *linear* in the magnetic degrees-of-freedom. </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:56:!> <li> The algorithm proceeds as follows:
src/ma02aa.F90:57:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:58:!> **plasma volumes**
src/ma02aa.F90:59:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:60:!> <ul>
src/ma02aa.F90:61:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:62:!> <li> In addition to the enclosed toroidal flux, \f$\Delta \psi_t\f$, which is held constant in the plasma volumes,
src/ma02aa.F90:63:!>       the Beltrami field in a given volume is assumed to be parameterized by \f$\mu\f$ and \f$\Delta \psi_p\f$.
src/ma02aa.F90:64:!>       (Note that \f$\Delta \psi_p\f$ is not defined in a torus.) </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:65:!> <li> These are "packed" into an array, e.g. \f$\boldsymbol{\mu} \equiv (\mu, \Delta\psi_p)^T\f$, so that standard library routines ,
src/ma02aa.F90:66:!>       e.g. \c C05PCF, can be used to (iteratively) find the appropriately-constrained Beltrami solution, i.e. \f${\bf f}(\boldsymbol{\mu})=0\f$. </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:67:!> <li> The function \f${\bf f}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\f$, which is computed by mp00ac(), is defined by the input parameter \c Lconstraint:
src/ma02aa.F90:68:!> <ul>
src/ma02aa.F90:69:!> <li> If \c Lconstraint = -1, 0, then \f$\boldsymbol{\mu}\f$       is *not* varied and \c Nxdof=0. </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:70:!> <li> If \c Lconstraint =  1,    then \f$\boldsymbol{\mu}\f$       is       varied to satisfy the transform constraints;
src/ma02aa.F90:71:!>      and \c Nxdof=1 in the simple torus and \c Nxdof=2 in the annular regions.
src/ma02aa.F90:72:!>      (Note that in the "simple-torus" region, the enclosed poloidal flux \f$\Delta\psi_p\f$ is not well-defined,
src/ma02aa.F90:73:!>      and only \f$\mu=\boldsymbol{\mu}_1\f$ is varied in order to satisfy the transform constraint on the "outer" interface of that volume.) </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:74:!> <li> \todo If \c Lconstraint =  2,    then \f$\mu=\boldsymbol{\mu}_1\f$ is       varied in order to satisfy the helicity constraint,
src/ma02aa.F90:75:!>      and \f$\Delta\psi_p=\boldsymbol{\mu}_2\f$ is *not* varied, and \c Nxdof=1.
src/ma02aa.F90:76:!>      (under re-construction)
src/ma02aa.F90:77:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:78:!> </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:79:!> </ul> </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:80:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:81:!> </ul>
src/ma02aa.F90:82:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:83:!> **vacuum volume**
src/ma02aa.F90:84:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:85:!> <ul>
src/ma02aa.F90:86:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:87:!> <li> In the vacuum, \f$\mu=0\f$, and the enclosed fluxes, \f$\Delta \psi_t\f$ and \f$\Delta \psi_p\f$, are considered to parameterize the family of solutions.
src/ma02aa.F90:88:!>      (These quantities may not be well-defined if \f${\bf B}\cdot{\bf n}\ne 0\f$ on the computational boundary.) </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:89:!> <li> These are "packed" into an array, \f$\boldsymbol{\mu} \equiv (\Delta\psi_t,\Delta\psi_p)^T\f$, so that, as above, standard routines can be used
src/ma02aa.F90:90:!>      to iteratively find the appropriately constrained solution, i.e. \f${\bf f}(\boldsymbol{\mu})=0\f$. </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:91:!> <li> The function \f${\bf f}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\f$, which is computed by mp00ac(), is defined by the input parameter \c Lconstraint:
src/ma02aa.F90:92:!> <ul>
src/ma02aa.F90:93:!> <li> If \c Lconstraint = -1,   then \f$\boldsymbol{\mu}\f$ is *not* varied and \c Nxdof=0. </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:94:!> <li> If \c Lconstraint =  0,2, then \f$\boldsymbol{\mu}\f$ is varied to satisfy the enclosed current constraints, and \c Nxdof=2. </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:95:!> <li> If \c Lconstraint =  1,   then \f$\boldsymbol{\mu}\f$ is varied to satisfy
src/ma02aa.F90:96:!>      the constraint on the transform on the inner boundary \f$\equiv\f$ plasma boundary and the "linking" current, and \c Nxdof=2.  </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:97:!> </ul> </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:98:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:99:!> </ul>  </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:100:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:101:!> <li> The Beltrami fields, and the rotational-transform and helicity etc. as required to determine the function \f${\bf f}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\f$
src/ma02aa.F90:102:!>      are calculated in mp00ac(). </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:103:!> <li> This routine, mp00ac(), is called iteratively if \c Nxdof>1 via
src/ma02aa.F90:104:!>      \c C05PCF to determine the appropriately constrained Beltrami field, \f${\bf B}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\f$, so that \f${\bf f}(\boldsymbol{\mu})=0\f$. </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:105:!> <li> The input variables \c mupftol and \c mupfits control the required accuracy and maximum number of iterations. </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:106:!> <li> If \c Nxdof=1, then mp00ac() is called only once to provide the Beltrami fields with the given value of \f$\boldsymbol{\mu}\f$. </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:107:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:108:!> </ul>
src/ma02aa.F90:235:!> **debugging: finite-difference confirmation of the derivatives of the rotational-transform**
src/ma02aa.F90:236:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:237:!> <ul>
src/ma02aa.F90:238:!>
src/ma02aa.F90:239:!> <li> Note that the rotational-transform (if required) is calculated by tr00ab(), which is called by mp00ac(). </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:240:!> <li> If \c Lconstraint=1, then mp00ac() will ask tr00ab() to compute the derivatives of the transform
src/ma02aa.F90:241:!>      with respect to variations in the helicity-multiplier, \f$\mu\f$, and the enclosed poloidal-flux, \f$\Delta\psi_p\f$, so that
src/ma02aa.F90:242:!>      \c C05PCF may more efficiently find the solution. </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:243:!> <li> The required derivatives are
src/ma02aa.F90:244:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}}{\partial \mu}\\
src/ma02aa.F90:245:!>                    \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}}{\partial \Delta \psi_p}
src/ma02aa.F90:246:!>      \f}
src/ma02aa.F90:247:!>      to improve the efficiency of the iterative search.
src/ma02aa.F90:248:!>      A finite difference estimate of these derivatives is available; need \c DEBUG, \c Lcheck=2 and \c Lconstraint=1. </li>
src/ma02aa.F90:249:!> </ul>
src/matrix.F90:2:!> \defgroup grp_build_matrices Build matrices
src/matrix.F90:3:!>
src/matrix.F90:4:!> \latexonly
src/matrix.F90:5:!> \definecolor{Orange}{rgb}{1.0,0.5,0.0}
src/matrix.F90:6:!> \definecolor{Cerulean}{rgb}{0.0,0.5,1.0}
src/matrix.F90:7:!> \endlatexonly
src/matrix.F90:8:!>
src/matrix.F90:9:!> \file
src/matrix.F90:10:!> \brief Constructs energy and helicity matrices that represent the Beltrami linear system.
src/matrix.F90:12:!> \brief Constructs energy and helicity matrices that represent the Beltrami linear system.
src/matrix.F90:13:!> \ingroup grp_build_matrices
src/matrix.F90:14:!> **gauge conditions**
src/matrix.F90:15:!>
src/matrix.F90:16:!> <ul>
src/matrix.F90:17:!> <li> In the \f$v\f$-th annulus, bounded by the \f$(v-1)\f$-th and \f$v\f$-th interfaces,
src/matrix.F90:18:!>       a general covariant representation of the magnetic vector-potential is written
src/matrix.F90:19:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf \bar A}=\bar A_{s} \nabla s + \bar A_{\theta} \nabla \theta + \bar A_{\zeta} \nabla \zeta. \f} </li>
src/matrix.F90:20:!> <li> To this add \f$\nabla g(s,\theta,\zeta)\f$, where \f$g\f$ satisfies
src/matrix.F90:21:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \begin{array}{cccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/matrix.F90:22:!>           \partial_s      g( s,\theta,\zeta) & = & - & \bar A_{s     }( s,\theta,\zeta) \\
src/matrix.F90:23:!>           \partial_\theta g(-1,\theta,\zeta) & = & - & \bar A_{\theta}(-1,\theta,\zeta) \\
src/matrix.F90:24:!>           \partial_\zeta  g(-1,     0,\zeta) & = & - & \bar A_{\zeta }(-1,     0,\zeta).
src/matrix.F90:25:!>       \end{array}
src/matrix.F90:26:!>      \f} </li>
src/matrix.F90:27:!> <li> Then \f${\bf A}={\bf \bar A}+\nabla g\f$ is given by \f${\bf A}=A_{\theta}\nabla\theta+A_{\zeta}\nabla\zeta\f$ with
src/matrix.F90:28:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/matrix.F90:29:!>        A_{\theta}(-1,\theta,\zeta) &=& 0 \label{eq:At_matrixgauge_matrix} \\
src/matrix.F90:30:!>        A_{\zeta }(-1,     0,\zeta) &=& 0 \label{eq:Az_matrixgauge_matrix}
src/matrix.F90:31:!>       \f} </li>
src/matrix.F90:32:!> <li> This specifies the gauge: to see this, notice that no gauge term can be added without violating the conditions in Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:At_matrixgauge_matrix})\f$ or Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:Az_matrixgauge_matrix})\f$. </li>
src/matrix.F90:33:!> <li> Note that the gauge employed in each volume is distinct. </li>
src/matrix.F90:34:!> </ul>
src/matrix.F90:35:!>
src/matrix.F90:36:!> **boundary conditions**
src/matrix.F90:37:!>
src/matrix.F90:38:!> <ul>
src/matrix.F90:39:!> <li> The magnetic field is
src/matrix.F90:40:!>       \f$\sqrt g \, {\bf B} = (\partial_\theta A_\zeta - \partial_\zeta A_\theta)\;{\bf e}_s - \partial_s A_\zeta \;{\bf e}_\theta + \partial_s A_\theta \;{\bf e}_\zeta\f$. </li>
src/matrix.F90:41:!> <li> In the annular volumes, the condition that the field is tangential to the inner interface, \f$\sqrt g {\bf B}\cdot\nabla s=0\f$ at \f$s=-1\f$,
src/matrix.F90:42:!>       gives \f$\partial_\theta A_\zeta - \partial_\zeta A_\theta = 0\f$.
src/matrix.F90:43:!>       With the above condition on \f$A_\theta\f$ given in Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:At_matrixgauge_matrix})\f$, this gives \f$\partial_\theta A_\zeta=0\f$, which with Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:Az_matrixgauge_matrix})\f$ gives
src/matrix.F90:44:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ A_\zeta(-1,\theta,\zeta)=0.
src/matrix.F90:45:!>       \f} </li>
src/matrix.F90:46:!> <li> The condition at the outer interface, \f$s=+1\f$, is that the field is \f$\sqrt g \, {\bf B}\cdot\nabla s = \partial_\theta A_\zeta - \partial_\zeta A_\theta = b\f$,
src/matrix.F90:47:!>       where \f$b\f$ is supplied by the user.
src/matrix.F90:48:!>       For each of the plasma regions, \f$b=0\f$.
src/matrix.F90:49:!>       For the vacuum region, generally \f$b\ne0\f$. </li>
src/matrix.F90:50:!> </ul>
src/matrix.F90:51:!>
src/matrix.F90:52:!> **enclosed fluxes**
src/matrix.F90:53:!>
src/matrix.F90:54:!> <ul>
src/matrix.F90:55:!> <li> In the plasma regions, the enclosed fluxes must be constrained. </li>
src/matrix.F90:56:!> <li> The toroidal and poloidal fluxes enclosed in each volume are determined using
src/matrix.F90:57:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \int_S {\bf B}\cdot{\bf ds}=\int_{\partial S}{\bf A}\cdot {\bf dl}.
src/matrix.F90:58:!>       \f} </li>
src/matrix.F90:59:!> </ul>
src/matrix.F90:60:!>
src/matrix.F90:61:!> **Fourier-Chebyshev representation**
src/matrix.F90:62:!>
src/matrix.F90:63:!> <ul>
src/matrix.F90:64:!> <li> The components of the vector potential, \f${\bf A}=A_\theta \nabla + A_\zeta \nabla \zeta \f$, are
src/matrix.F90:65:!>      \f{eqnarray}{
src/matrix.F90:66:!>        A_\theta(s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \label{eq:At_matrix} \\
src/matrix.F90:67:!>        A_\zeta( s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \label{eq:Az_matrix}
src/matrix.F90:68:!>      \f}
src/matrix.F90:69:!>      where \f${\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \f$ is the __recombined__ Chebyshev polynomial in a volume without an axis, or __modified__ Zernike polynomial in a volume with an axis
src/matrix.F90:70:!>      (i.e. only in the innermost volume, and only in cylindrical and toroidal geometry.)
src/matrix.F90:71:!>      , and \f$\alpha_j \equiv m_j\theta-n_j\zeta\f$.</li>
src/matrix.F90:72:!> <li> The magnetic field, \f$\sqrt g \, {\bf B} = \sqrt g B^s {\bf e}_s + \sqrt g B^\theta {\bf e}_\theta + \sqrt g B^\zeta {\bf e}_\zeta\f$, is
src/matrix.F90:73:!>      \f{eqnarray}{
src/matrix.F90:74:!>        \begin{array}{ccccrcrcrcrcccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/matrix.F90:75:!>        \sqrt g \, {\bf B} & = & {\bf e}_s      & \sum_{i,l} [ ( & - m_i {\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} & - & n_i {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}        \sin\alpha_i + ( & + m_i {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} & + & n_i {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}        \cos\alpha_i ] \\
src/matrix.F90:76:!>                           & + & {\bf e}_\theta & \sum_{i,l} [ ( &                                      & - &     {\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \cos\alpha_i + ( &                                          & - &     {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \sin\alpha_i ] \\
src/matrix.F90:77:!>                           & + & {\bf e}_\zeta  & \sum_{i,l} [ ( &       {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}} &   &                                    & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \cos\alpha_i + ( &       {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} &   &                                        & ) {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \sin\alpha_i ]
src/matrix.F90:78:!>        \end{array}
src/matrix.F90:79:!>      \f}
src/matrix.F90:80:!> </li>
src/matrix.F90:81:!> <li> The components of the velocity, \f${\bf v} \equiv v_s \nabla s + v_\theta \nabla \theta + v_\zeta \nabla \zeta eta\f$, are
src/matrix.F90:82:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ v_s     (s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{red}  v_{     s,e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Orange}   v_{     s,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \\
src/matrix.F90:83:!>                     v_\theta(s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{red}  v_{\theta,e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Orange}   v_{\theta,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \\
src/matrix.F90:84:!>                     v_\zeta (s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{blue} v_{\zeta ,e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Cerulean} v_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i.
src/matrix.F90:85:!>       \f} </li>
src/matrix.F90:86:!> </ul>
src/matrix.F90:87:!>
src/matrix.F90:88:!> **constrained energy functional**
src/matrix.F90:89:!>
src/matrix.F90:90:!> <ul>
src/matrix.F90:91:!>
src/matrix.F90:92:!> <li> The constrained energy functional in each volume depends on the vector potential and the Lagrange multipliers,
src/matrix.F90:93:!> \f{eqnarray}{ {\cal F} \equiv
src/matrix.F90:94:!> {\cal F}[{\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}},{\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}},{\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}},{\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}},
src/matrix.F90:95:!>  {\color{red} v_{s,e,i,l}},{\color{Orange} v_{s,o,i,l}},{\color{red} v_{\theta,e,i,l}},{\color{Orange} v_{\theta,o,i,l}},{\color{blue} v_{\zeta,e,i,l}},{\color{Cerulean} v_{\zeta,o,i,l}},\mu,a_i,b_i,c_i,d_i,e_i,f_i,g_1,h_1],
src/matrix.F90:96:!> \f}
src/matrix.F90:97:!>       and is given by:
src/matrix.F90:98:!> \f{eqnarray}{ \begin{array}{cclcclcccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/matrix.F90:99:!>     {\cal F}
src/matrix.F90:100:!>     & \equiv & \displaystyle                        \int {\bf B} \cdot {\bf B} \, dv
src/matrix.F90:101:!>        +                                            \int {\bf v} \cdot {\bf v} \, dv
src/matrix.F90:102:!>        -       \displaystyle       \mu       \left[ \int {\bf A} \cdot {\bf B} \, dv - K \right]                                                                                                              \\
src/matrix.F90:103:!>     &  + &   & \displaystyle \sum_{i=1} & a_i       & \displaystyle \left[  \sum_l {\color{red}     A_{\theta,e,i,l}} T_l(-1) - 0                                                                     \right] \\
src/matrix.F90:104:!>     &  + &   & \displaystyle \sum_{i=1} & b_i       & \displaystyle \left[  \sum_l {\color{blue}    A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} T_l(-1) - 0                                                                     \right] \\
src/matrix.F90:105:!>     &  + &   & \displaystyle \sum_{i=2} & c_i       & \displaystyle \left[  \sum_l {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} T_l(-1) - 0                                                                     \right] \\
src/matrix.F90:106:!>     &  + &   & \displaystyle \sum_{i=2} & d_i       & \displaystyle \left[  \sum_l {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} T_l(-1) - 0                                                                     \right] \\
src/matrix.F90:107:!>     &  + &   & \displaystyle \sum_{i=2} & e_i       & \displaystyle \left[  \sum_l \left( - m_i {\color{blue}    A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} - n_i {\color{red}     A_{\theta,e,i,l}} \right) T_l(+1) - b_{s,i} \right] \\
src/matrix.F90:108:!>     &  + &   & \displaystyle \sum_{i=2} & f_i       & \displaystyle \left[  \sum_l \left( + m_i {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} + n_i {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} \right) T_l(+1) - b_{c,i} \right] \\
src/matrix.F90:109:!>     &  + &   & \displaystyle            & g_1       & \displaystyle \left[  \sum_l {\color{red}     A_{\theta,e,1,l}} T_l(+1) - \Delta \psi_t \right]                                \\
src/matrix.F90:110:!>     &  + &   & \displaystyle            & h_1       & \displaystyle \left[  \sum_l {\color{blue}    A_{\zeta, e,1,l}} T_l(+1) + \Delta \psi_p \right]
src/matrix.F90:111:!>     \end{array}
src/matrix.F90:112:!> \f}
src/matrix.F90:113:!>       where
src/matrix.F90:114:!>       <ul>
src/matrix.F90:115:!>       <li>\f$a_i\f$, \f$b_i\f$, \f$c_i\f$ and \f$d_i\f$ are Lagrange multipliers used to enforce the combined gauge and interface boundary condition
src/matrix.F90:116:!>            on the inner interface, </li>
src/matrix.F90:117:!>       <li>\f$e_i\f$ and \f$f_i\f$                       are Lagrange multipliers used to enforce the interface boundary condition on the outer interface,
src/matrix.F90:118:!>            namely \f$\sqrt g \, {\bf B}\cdot\nabla s = b\f$; and </li>
src/matrix.F90:119:!>       <li>\f$g_1\f$ and \f$h_1\f$                       are Lagrange multipliers used to enforce the constraints on the enclosed fluxes. </li>
src/matrix.F90:120:!>       </ul>
src/matrix.F90:121:!> <li> In each plasma volume the boundary condition on the outer interface is \f$b=0\f$.
src/matrix.F90:122:!> <li> In the vacuum volume (only for free-boundary), we may set \f$\mu=0\f$.
src/matrix.F90:123:!> <li> __Note:__ in SPEC version >3.00, the basis recombination method is used to ensure the boundary condition on the inner side of an interface.
src/matrix.F90:124:!>      The lagrange multipliers \f$a_i, b_i, c_i, d_i\f$ are no longer used in volumes without a coordinate singularity.
src/matrix.F90:125:!>      In a volume with a coordinate singularity, they are used only\f$a_i, c_i\f$ with $m=0,1$ are excluded also due to Zernike basis recombination.
src/matrix.F90:126:!>
src/matrix.F90:127:!> </ul>
src/matrix.F90:128:!>
src/matrix.F90:129:!> **derivatives of magnetic energy integrals**
src/matrix.F90:130:!>
src/matrix.F90:131:!> <ul>
src/matrix.F90:132:!>
src/matrix.F90:133:!> <li> The first  derivatives of \f$\int \! dv \; {\bf B} \! \cdot \! {\bf B}\f$ with respect to
src/matrix.F90:134:!> \f${\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}\f$, \f${\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}}\f$, \f${\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}}\f$ and \f${\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}}\f$ are
src/matrix.F90:135:!> \f{eqnarray}{
src/matrix.F90:136:!> \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot {\bf B} & = &
src/matrix.F90:137:!> 2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}}{\partial {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}} =
src/matrix.F90:138:!>      2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot \left[ - n_i {\overline T}_{l,i}  \sin\alpha_i \, {\bf e}_s + {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \cos\alpha_i \, {\bf e}_\zeta \right] / \sqrt g \\
src/matrix.F90:139:!> \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot {\bf B} & = &
src/matrix.F90:140:!> 2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}}{\partial {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}}} =
src/matrix.F90:141:!>      2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot \left[ + n_i {\overline T}_{l,i}  \cos\alpha_i \, {\bf e}_s + {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \sin\alpha_i \, {\bf e}_\zeta \right] / \sqrt g \\
src/matrix.F90:142:!> \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot {\bf B} & = &
src/matrix.F90:143:!> 2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}}{\partial {\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}}} =
src/matrix.F90:144:!>      2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot \left[ - m_i {\overline T}_{l,i}  \sin\alpha_i \, {\bf e}_s - {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \cos\alpha_i \, {\bf e}_\theta \right] / \sqrt g \\
src/matrix.F90:145:!> \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot {\bf B} & = &
src/matrix.F90:146:!> 2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}}{\partial {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}}} =
src/matrix.F90:147:!>      2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \cdot \left[ + m_i {\overline T}_{l,i}  \cos\alpha_i \, {\bf e}_s - {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \sin\alpha_i \, {\bf e}_\theta \right] / \sqrt g
src/matrix.F90:148:!> \f} </li>
src/matrix.F90:149:!> <li> The second derivatives of \f$\int \! dv \; {\bf B} \! \cdot \! {\bf B}\f$ with respect to
src/matrix.F90:150:!>  \f${\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}\f$, \f${\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}}\f$, \f${\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}}\f$ and \f${\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}}\f$ are
src/matrix.F90:151:!> \f{eqnarray}{
src/matrix.F90:152:!> \!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\! \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{red}     A_{\theta,e,j,p}}} \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}} \! \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \! \cdot \! {\bf B}
src/matrix.F90:153:!> \!\!&\!\!=\!\!&\!\!2 \int \!\! dv \;
src/matrix.F90:154:!>  ( + n_j n_i {\overline T}_{p,j}  {\overline T}_{l,i}  s_j s_i g_{ss} - n_j {\overline T}_{p,j}  {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime s_j c_i g_{s\zeta}
src/matrix.F90:155:!>    -     n_i {\overline T}_{l,i}  {\overline T}_{p,j}^\prime s_i c_j g_{s\zeta} +     {\overline T}_{p,j}^\prime {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime c_j c_i g_{\zeta\zeta}) / \sqrt g^2\nonumber \\
src/matrix.F90:156:!> \!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\! \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,j,p}}} \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}} \! \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \! \cdot \! {\bf B}
src/matrix.F90:157:!> \!\!&\!\!=\!\!&\!\!2 \int \!\! dv \;
src/matrix.F90:158:!>  ( - n_j n_i {\overline T}_{p,j}  {\overline T}_{l,i}  c_j s_i g_{ss} + n_j {\overline T}_{p,j}  {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime c_j c_i g_{s\zeta}
src/matrix.F90:159:!>    -     n_i {\overline T}_{l,i}  {\overline T}_{p,j}^\prime s_i s_j g_{s\zeta} +     {\overline T}_{p,j}^\prime {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime s_j c_i g_{\zeta\zeta}) / \sqrt g^2\nonumber \\
src/matrix.F90:160:!> \!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\! \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{blue}    A_{\zeta, e,j,p}}} \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}} \! \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \! \cdot \! {\bf B}
src/matrix.F90:161:!> \!\!&\!\!=\!\!&\!\!2 \int \!\! dv \;
src/matrix.F90:162:!>  ( + m_j n_i {\overline T}_{p,j}  {\overline T}_{l,i}  s_j s_i g_{ss} - m_j {\overline T}_{p,j}  {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime s_j c_i g_{s\zeta}
src/matrix.F90:163:!>    +     n_i {\overline T}_{l,i}  {\overline T}_{p,j}^\prime s_i c_j g_{s\theta} -     {\overline T}_{p,j}^\prime {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime c_j c_i g_{\theta\zeta}) / \sqrt g^2\nonumber \\
src/matrix.F90:164:!> \!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\! \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,j,p}}} \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}} \! \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \! \cdot \! {\bf B}
src/matrix.F90:165:!> \!\!&\!\!=\!\!&\!\!2 \int \!\! dv \;
src/matrix.F90:166:!>  ( - m_j n_i {\overline T}_{p,j}  {\overline T}_{l,i}  c_j s_i g_{ss} + m_j {\overline T}_{p,j}  {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime c_j c_i g_{s\zeta}
src/matrix.F90:167:!>    +     n_i {\overline T}_{l,i}  {\overline T}_{p,j}^\prime s_i s_j g_{s\theta} -     {\overline T}_{p,j}^\prime {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime s_j c_i g_{\theta\zeta}) / \sqrt g^2\nonumber
src/matrix.F90:168:!> \f}
src/matrix.F90:169:!> \f{eqnarray}{
src/matrix.F90:170:!> \!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\! \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{red}     A_{\theta,e,j,p}}} \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}}} \! \int \!\! dv \; {\bf B} \! \cdot \! {\bf B}
src/matrix.F90:171:!> \!\!&\!\!=\!\!&\!\!2 \int \!\! dv \;

src/matrix.F90:232:!> <li> The first  derivatives of \f$\int \! dv \; {\bf A} \cdot{\bf B}\f$ with respect to
src/matrix.F90:233:!> \f${\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}\f$, \f${\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}}\f$, \f${\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}}\f$ and \f${\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}}\f$ are
src/matrix.F90:234:!> \f{eqnarray}{
src/matrix.F90:235:!> \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf A} \cdot {\bf B} & = &
src/matrix.F90:236:!> \int \!\! dv \; \left( \frac{\partial {\bf A}}{\partial {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}} \cdot {\bf B} + {\bf A} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}}{\partial {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}} \right) =
src/matrix.F90:237:!> \int \!\! dv \; ( {\overline T}_{l,i} \cos\alpha_i \nabla \theta \cdot {\bf B} + {\bf A} \cdot {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \cos\alpha_i \, {\bf e}_\zeta / \sqrt g ) \\
src/matrix.F90:238:!> \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf A} \cdot {\bf B} & = &
src/matrix.F90:239:!> \int \!\! dv \; \left( \frac{\partial {\bf A}}{\partial {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}}} \cdot {\bf B} + {\bf A} \cdot \frac{\partial {\bf B}}{\partial {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}}} \right) =
src/matrix.F90:240:!> \int \!\! dv \; ( {\overline T}_{l,i} \sin\alpha_i \nabla \theta \cdot {\bf B} + {\bf A} \cdot {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \sin\alpha_i \, {\bf e}_\zeta / \sqrt g ) \\
src/matrix.F90:241:!> \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf A} \cdot {\bf B} & = &

src/matrix.F90:313:!> \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,j,p}}} \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf A} \cdot {\bf B} & = &   \int \!\! dv \;  \left[
src/matrix.F90:314:!> \cancel{
src/matrix.F90:315:!> - {\overline T}_{l,i} \sin\alpha_i \nabla \zeta  \cdot {\overline T}_{p,j}^\prime \sin\alpha_j \, {\bf e}_\theta}
src/matrix.F90:316:!> \cancel{
src/matrix.F90:317:!> - {\overline T}_{p,j} \sin\alpha_j \nabla \zeta  \cdot {\overline T}_{l,i}^\prime \sin\alpha_i \, {\bf e}_\theta}
src/matrix.F90:318:!> \right] / \sqrt g
src/matrix.F90:319:!> \f} </li>
src/matrix.F90:320:!> <li> In these expressions the terms  \f$\nabla \theta \cdot {\bf e}_\theta =        \nabla \zeta \cdot {\bf e}_\zeta =1 \f$,
src/matrix.F90:321:!>                                  and \f$\cancel{\nabla \theta \cdot {\bf e}_\zeta} =\cancel{\nabla \zeta \cdot {\bf e}_\theta}=0\f$
src/matrix.F90:322:!>       have been included to show the structure of the derivation. </li>
src/matrix.F90:323:!> </ul>
src/matrix.F90:324:!>
src/matrix.F90:325:!> **derivatives of kinetic energy integrals**
src/matrix.F90:326:!>
src/matrix.F90:327:!> <ul>
src/matrix.F90:328:!> <li> The first derivatives of \f$\int \! dv \; v^2\f$ with respect to \f${\color{red}  v_{     s,e,i,l}}\f$ etc. are
src/matrix.F90:329:!> \f{eqnarray}{ \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{red}      v_{     s,e,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\bf v} & = & 2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\overline T}_{l,i} \cos\alpha_i \nabla s \\
src/matrix.F90:330:!>               \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{Orange}   v_{     s,o,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\bf v} & = & 2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\overline T}_{l,i} \sin\alpha_i \nabla s \\
src/matrix.F90:331:!>               \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{red}      v_{\theta,e,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\bf v} & = & 2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\overline T}_{l,i} \cos\alpha_i \nabla \theta \\
src/matrix.F90:332:!>               \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{Orange}   v_{\theta,o,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\bf v} & = & 2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\overline T}_{l,i} \sin\alpha_i \nabla \theta \\
src/matrix.F90:333:!>               \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{blue}     v_{\zeta ,e,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\bf v} & = & 2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\overline T}_{l,i} \cos\alpha_i \nabla \zeta  \\
src/matrix.F90:334:!>               \frac{\partial}{\partial {\color{Cerulean} v_{\zeta ,o,i,l}}} \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\bf v} & = & 2 \int \!\! dv \; {\bf v} \cdot {\overline T}_{l,i} \sin\alpha_i \nabla \zeta  \\
src/matrix.F90:335:!> \f} </li>

src/metrix.F90:1:!> \defgroup grp_metrics Metric quantities
src/metrix.F90:2:!>
src/metrix.F90:3:!> \file
src/metrix.F90:4:!> \brief Calculates the metric quantities, \f$\sqrt g \, g^{\mu\nu}\f$, which are required for the energy and helicity integrals.
src/metrix.F90:6:!> \brief Calculates the metric quantities, \f$\sqrt g \, g^{\mu\nu}\f$, which are required for the energy and helicity integrals.
src/metrix.F90:7:!> \ingroup grp_metrics
src/metrix.F90:8:!>
src/metrix.F90:9:!> **metrics**
src/metrix.F90:10:!>
src/metrix.F90:11:!> <ul>
src/metrix.F90:12:!> <li> The Jacobian, \f$\sqrt g\f$, and the "lower" metric elements, \f$g_{\mu\nu}\f$, are calculated by coords(),
src/metrix.F90:13:!>      and are provided on a regular grid in "real-space", i.e. \f$(\theta,\zeta)\f$, at a given radial location, i.e. where \f$s\f$ is input. </li>
src/metrix.F90:14:!> </ul>
src/metrix.F90:15:!>
src/metrix.F90:16:!> **plasma region**
src/metrix.F90:17:!>
src/metrix.F90:18:!> <ul>
src/metrix.F90:19:!> <li>  In the plasma region, the required terms are \f$\bar g_{\mu\nu} \equiv g_{\mu\nu}/\sqrt g\f$.
src/metrix.F90:20:!>
src/metrix.F90:21:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \begin{array}{ccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/metrix.F90:22:!>       \sqrt g \; g^{s     s     } & = & \left( g_{\theta\theta} g_{\zeta \zeta } - g_{\theta\zeta } g_{\theta\zeta } \right) / \sqrt g \\
src/metrix.F90:23:!>       \sqrt g \; g^{s     \theta} & = & \left( g_{\theta\zeta } g_{s     \zeta } - g_{s     \theta} g_{\zeta \zeta } \right) / \sqrt g \\
src/metrix.F90:24:!>       \sqrt g \; g^{s     \zeta } & = & \left( g_{s     \theta} g_{\theta\zeta } - g_{\theta\theta} g_{s     \zeta } \right) / \sqrt g \\
src/metrix.F90:25:!>       \sqrt g \; g^{\theta\theta} & = & \left( g_{\zeta \zeta } g_{s     s     } - g_{s     \zeta } g_{s     \zeta } \right) / \sqrt g \\
src/metrix.F90:26:!>       \sqrt g \; g^{\theta\zeta } & = & \left( g_{s     \zeta } g_{s     \theta} - g_{\theta\zeta } g_{s     s     } \right) / \sqrt g \\
src/metrix.F90:27:!>       \sqrt g \; g^{\zeta \zeta } & = & \left( g_{s     s     } g_{\theta\theta} - g_{s     \theta} g_{s     \theta} \right) / \sqrt g
src/metrix.F90:28:!>       \end{array}
src/metrix.F90:29:!>       \f} </li>
src/metrix.F90:30:!> </ul>
src/metrix.F90:31:!>
src/metrix.F90:32:!> **FFTs**
src/metrix.F90:33:!>
src/metrix.F90:34:!> <ul>
src/metrix.F90:35:!> <li> After constructing the required quantities in real space, FFTs provided the required Fourier harmonics, which are returned through global.f90 .
src/metrix.F90:36:!>      (The "extended" Fourier resolution is used.) </li>
src/metrix.F90:37:!> </ul>
src/metrix.F90:115:!> \brief compute guvijsave
src/metrix.F90:116:!>
src/metrix.F90:117:!> @param lquad
src/metrix.F90:118:!> @param vvol
src/metrix.F90:119:!> @param ideriv
src/metrix.F90:120:!> @param Lcurvature
src/mp00ac.F90:2:!> \defgroup grp_solver Solver for Beltrami (linear) system
src/mp00ac.F90:3:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:4:!> \file
src/mp00ac.F90:5:!> \brief Solves Beltrami/vacuum (linear) system, given matrices.
src/mp00ac.F90:7:!> \brief Solves Beltrami/vacuum (linear) system, given matrices.
src/mp00ac.F90:8:!> \ingroup grp_solver
src/mp00ac.F90:9:!> **unpacking fluxes, helicity multiplier**
src/mp00ac.F90:10:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:11:!> <ul>
src/mp00ac.F90:12:!> <li> The vector of "parameters", \f$\boldsymbol{\mu}\f$, is unpacked. (Recall that \f$\boldsymbol{\mu}\f$ was "packed" in ma02aa() .)
src/mp00ac.F90:13:!>      In the following, \f$\boldsymbol{\psi} \equiv (\Delta \psi_t, \Delta \psi_p)^T\f$. </li>
src/mp00ac.F90:14:!> </ul>
src/mp00ac.F90:15:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:16:!> **construction of linear system**
src/mp00ac.F90:17:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:18:!> <ul>
src/mp00ac.F90:19:!> <li> The equation \f$\nabla \times {\bf B} = \mu {\bf B}\f$ is cast as a matrix equation, \f{eqnarray}{ {\cal M} \cdot {\bf a} = {\cal R},\f}
src/mp00ac.F90:20:!>       where \f${\bf a}\f$ represents the degrees-of-freedom in the magnetic vector potential, \f${\bf a} \equiv \{ {\color{red} {A_{\theta,e,i,l}}}, {\color{blue}{A_{\zeta, e,i,l}}}, \dots \}\f$. </li>
src/mp00ac.F90:21:!> <li> The matrix \f${\cal M}\f$ is constructed from \f${\cal A}\equiv\,\f$\c dMA and \f${\cal D}\equiv\,\f$\c dMD,
src/mp00ac.F90:22:!>       which were constructed in matrix() , according to
src/mp00ac.F90:23:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ {\cal M} \equiv {\cal A} - \mu {\cal D}.
src/mp00ac.F90:24:!>       \f}
src/mp00ac.F90:25:!>       Note that in the vacuum region, \f$\mu=0\f$, so \f${\cal M}\f$ reduces to \f${\cal M} \equiv {\cal A}\f$. </li>
src/mp00ac.F90:26:!> <li> The construction of the vector \f${\cal R}\f$ is as follows:
src/mp00ac.F90:27:!>       <ul> <li> if \c Lcoordinatesingularity=T , then
src/mp00ac.F90:28:!>            \f{eqnarray}{ {\cal R} \equiv - \left( {\cal B} - \mu {\cal E} \right) \cdot \boldsymbol{\psi}
src/mp00ac.F90:29:!>            \f} </li>
src/mp00ac.F90:30:!>            <li> if \c Lcoordinatesingularity=F and \c Lplasmaregion=T , then
src/mp00ac.F90:31:!>            \f{eqnarray}{ {\cal R} \equiv - {\cal B} \cdot \boldsymbol{\psi}
src/mp00ac.F90:32:!>            \f} </li>
src/mp00ac.F90:33:!>            <li> if \c Lcoordinatesingularity=F and \c Lvacuumregion=T , then
src/mp00ac.F90:34:!>            \f{eqnarray}{ {\cal R} \equiv - {\cal G} - {\cal B} \cdot \boldsymbol{\psi}
src/mp00ac.F90:35:!>            \f} </li>
src/mp00ac.F90:36:!>       </ul>
src/mp00ac.F90:37:!>       The quantities \f${\cal B}\equiv\,\f$\c dMB, \f${\cal E}\equiv\,\f$\c dME and \f${\cal G}\equiv\,\f$\c dMG are constructed in matrix() . </li>
src/mp00ac.F90:38:!> </ul>
src/mp00ac.F90:39:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:40:!> **solving linear system**
src/mp00ac.F90:41:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:42:!> It is _not_ assumed that the linear system is positive definite.
src/mp00ac.F90:43:!> The \c LAPACK routine \c DSYSVX is used to solve the linear system.
src/mp00ac.F90:44:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:45:!> **unpacking, ...**
src/mp00ac.F90:46:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:47:!> <ul>
src/mp00ac.F90:48:!> <li> The magnetic degrees-of-freedom are unpacked by packab() . </li>
src/mp00ac.F90:49:!> <li> The error flag, \c ImagneticOK , is set that indicates if the Beltrami fields were successfully constructed. </li>
src/mp00ac.F90:50:!> </ul>
src/mp00ac.F90:51:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:52:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:53:!> **construction of "constraint" function**
src/mp00ac.F90:54:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:55:!> <ul>
src/mp00ac.F90:56:!> <li> The construction of the function \f${\bf f}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\f$ is required so that iterative methods can be used to construct the Beltrami field
src/mp00ac.F90:57:!>       consistent with the required constraints (e.g. on the enclosed fluxes, helicity, rotational-transform, ...).
src/mp00ac.F90:58:!>       \see ma02aa() for additional details.
src/mp00ac.F90:59:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:60:!> **plasma region**
src/mp00ac.F90:61:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:62:!> <ul>
src/mp00ac.F90:63:!> <li> For \c Lcoordinatesingularity=T , the returned function is:
src/mp00ac.F90:64:!> \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf f}(\mu,\Delta\psi_p) \equiv
src/mp00ac.F90:65:!> \left\{ \begin{array}{cccccccr}
src/mp00ac.F90:66:!> (&                                            0&,&                                 0&)^T, & \textrm{if }\texttt{Lconstraint} &=& -1 \\
src/mp00ac.F90:67:!> (&                                            0&,&                                 0&)^T, & \textrm{if }\texttt{Lconstraint} &=&  0 \\
src/mp00ac.F90:68:!> (&{{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}(+1)-\texttt{iota (lvol  )}&,&                                 0&)^T, & \textrm{if }\texttt{Lconstraint} &=&  1 \\
src/mp00ac.F90:69:!> (&                                            ?&,&                                 ?&)^T, & \textrm{if }\texttt{Lconstraint} &=&  2
src/mp00ac.F90:70:!>           \end{array}\right.
src/mp00ac.F90:71:!> \f} </li>
src/mp00ac.F90:72:!> <li> For \c Lcoordinatesingularity=F , the returned function is:
src/mp00ac.F90:73:!> \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf f}(\mu,\Delta\psi_p) \equiv
src/mp00ac.F90:74:!> \left\{ \begin{array}{cccccccr}
src/mp00ac.F90:75:!> (&                                          0&,&                                         0&)^T, & \textrm{if }\texttt{Lconstraint} &=& -1 \\
src/mp00ac.F90:76:!> (&                                          0&,&                                         0&)^T, & \textrm{if }\texttt{Lconstraint} &=&  0 \\
src/mp00ac.F90:77:!> (&{{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}(-1)-\texttt{oita(lvol-1)}&,&{{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}(+1)-\texttt{iota(lvol)}&)^T, & \textrm{if }\texttt{Lconstraint} &=&  1 \\
src/mp00ac.F90:78:!> (&                                          ?&,&                                         ?&)^T, & \textrm{if }\texttt{Lconstraint} &=&  2
src/mp00ac.F90:79:!>           \end{array}\right.
src/mp00ac.F90:80:!> \f} </li>
src/mp00ac.F90:81:!> </ul>
src/mp00ac.F90:82:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:83:!> **vacuum region**
src/mp00ac.F90:84:!>
src/mp00ac.F90:85:!> <ul>
src/mp00ac.F90:86:!> <li> For the vacuum region, the returned function is:

src/packab.F90:8:!> \file
src/packab.F90:9:!> \brief Packs, and unpacks, Beltrami field solution vector; \f${\bf a}\equiv\{A_{\theta,e,i,l}, A_{\zeta,e,i,l}, \mathrm{etc.}\}\f$.
src/packab.F90:11:!> \brief Packs and unpacks Beltrami field solution vector.
src/packab.F90:12:!> \ingroup grp_packing
src/packab.F90:13:!>
src/packab.F90:14:!> **construction of "vector" of independent degrees of freedom**
src/packab.F90:15:!>
src/packab.F90:16:!> <ul>
src/packab.F90:17:!> <li> Numerical routines for solving linear equations typically require the unknown, independent degrees of freedom
src/packab.F90:18:!>      to be "packed" into a vector, \f${\bf x}\f$. </li>
src/packab.F90:19:!> <li> The magnetic field is defined by the independent degrees of freedom in
src/packab.F90:20:!>      the Chebyshev-Fourier representation of the vector potential, \f${\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}}\f$ and \f${\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}}\f$;
src/packab.F90:21:!>      and the non-stellarator-symmetric terms if relevant, \f${\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}}\f$ and \f${\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}}\f$;
src/packab.F90:22:!>      and the Lagrange multipliers, \f$a_i\f$, \f$b_i\f$, \f$c_i\f$, \f$d_i\f$, \f$e_i\f$, etc. as required to enforce the constraints:
src/packab.F90:23:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf x} \equiv \{ {\color{red} A_{\theta,e,i,l}},{\color{blue}A_{\zeta, e,i,l}},{\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}},{\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}},a_i,b_i,c_i,d_i,e_i,f_i,g_1,h_1\}.
src/packab.F90:24:!>      \f} </li>
src/packab.F90:25:!> <li> The "packing" index is assigned in preset() . </li>
src/packab.F90:26:!> </ul>
src/packab.F90:27:!>
src/packab.F90:28:!> @param packorunpack
src/packab.F90:29:!> @param lvol
src/packab.F90:30:!> @param NN
src/packab.F90:31:!> @param solution
src/packab.F90:32:!> @param ideriv
src/packxi.F90:1:!> \file
src/packxi.F90:2:!> \brief Packs, and unpacks, geometrical degrees of freedom; and sets coordinate axis.
src/packxi.F90:4:!> \ingroup grp_packing
src/packxi.F90:5:!> \brief Packs, and unpacks, geometrical degrees of freedom; and sets coordinate axis.
src/packxi.F90:6:!>
src/packxi.F90:7:!> **geometrical degrees of freedom**
src/packxi.F90:8:!> <ul>
src/packxi.F90:9:!> <li> The geometrical degrees-of-freedom,
src/packxi.F90:10:!>      namely the \f$R_{j,v}\f$ and \f$Z_{j,v}\f$ where \f$v\f$ labels the interface and \f$j\f$ labels the Fourier harmonic,
src/packxi.F90:11:!>      must be "packxi", and "unpackxi", into a single vector, \f$\boldsymbol{\xi}\f$, so that standard numerical routines can be
src/packxi.F90:12:!>      called to find solutions to force-balance, i.e. \f${\bf F}[\boldsymbol{\xi}]=0\f$. </li>
src/packxi.F90:13:!> <li> A coordinate "pre-conditioning" factor is included:
src/packxi.F90:14:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ \boldsymbol{\xi}_k \equiv \frac{R_{j,v}}{\Psi_{j,v}},
src/packxi.F90:15:!>      \f}
src/packxi.F90:16:!>      where \f$\Psi_{j,v} \equiv\,\f$\c psifactor(j,v) , which is defined in global.f90 . </li>
src/packxi.F90:17:!> </ul>
src/packxi.F90:18:!>
src/packxi.F90:19:!> **coordinate axis**
src/packxi.F90:20:!> <ul>
src/packxi.F90:21:!> <li> The coordinate axis is not an independent degree-of-freedom of the geometry.
src/packxi.F90:22:!>      It is constructed by extrapolating the geometry of the innermost interface down to a line. </li>
src/packxi.F90:23:!> <li> Note that if the coordinate axis depends only on the geometry of the innermost interface
src/packxi.F90:24:!>      then the block tridiagonal structure of the the force-derivative matrix is preserved. </li>
src/packxi.F90:25:!> <li> Define the arc-length weighted averages,
src/packxi.F90:26:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ R_0(\zeta) \equiv \frac{\int_{0}^{2\pi} R_1(\theta,\zeta) dl}{L(\zeta)}, \qquad
src/packxi.F90:27:!>                    Z_0(\zeta) \equiv \frac{\int_{0}^{2\pi} Z_1(\theta,\zeta) dl}{L(\zeta)},
src/packxi.F90:28:!>      \f}
src/packxi.F90:29:!>      where \f$L(\zeta)\equiv \int_{0}^{2\pi} dl\f$ and \f$dl \equiv \sqrt{ \partial_\theta R_1(\theta,\zeta)^2 + \partial_\theta Z_1(\theta,\zeta)^2 } \, d\theta\f$. </li>
src/packxi.F90:30:!> <li> Note that if \f$dl\f$ does not depend on \f$\theta\f$, i.e. if \f$\theta\f$ is the equal arc-length angle, then the expressions simplify. </li>
src/packxi.F90:31:!> <li> Note that the geometry of the coordinate axis thus constructed only depends on the geometry of the innermost interface, by which I
src/packxi.F90:32:!>      mean that the geometry of the coordinate axis is independent of the angle parameterization. </li>
src/packxi.F90:33:!> </ul>
src/packxi.F90:34:!>
src/packxi.F90:35:!> **some numerical comments**
src/packxi.F90:36:!> <ul>
src/packxi.F90:37:!> <li> First, the differential poloidal length, \f$dl \equiv \sqrt{ R_\theta^2 + Z_\theta^2 }\f$, is computed in real space using
src/packxi.F90:38:!>      an inverse FFT from the Fourier harmonics of \f$R\f$ and \f$Z\f$. </li>
src/packxi.F90:39:!> <li> Second, the Fourier harmonics of the \f$dl\f$ are computed using an FFT.
src/packxi.F90:40:!>      The integration over \f$\theta\f$ to construct \f$L\equiv \int dl\f$ is now trivial: just multiply the \f$m=0\f$ harmonics of \f$dl\f$ by \f$2\pi\f$.
src/packxi.F90:41:!>      The \c ajk(1:mn) variable is used. </li>
src/packxi.F90:42:!> <li> Next, the weighted \f$R\,dl\f$ and \f$Z\,dl\f$ are computed in real space, and the poloidal integral is similarly taken. </li>
src/packxi.F90:43:!> <li> Lastly, the Fourier harmonics are constructed using an FFT after dividing in real space. </li>
src/packxi.F90:44:!> </ul>
src/packxi.F90:45:!>
src/packxi.F90:46:!> @param[in] NGdof
src/packxi.F90:47:!> @param position
src/packxi.F90:48:!> @param[in] Mvol
src/packxi.F90:49:!> @param[in] mn
src/packxi.F90:50:!> @param iRbc
src/packxi.F90:51:!> @param iZbs
src/packxi.F90:52:!> @param iRbs
src/packxi.F90:53:!> @param iZbc
src/packxi.F90:54:!> @param packorunpack
src/packxi.F90:55:!> @param[in] LComputeDerivatives
src/packxi.F90:56:!> @param[in] LComputeAxis
