src/pc00aa.F90:13:!> **relevant input variables**
src/pc00aa.F90:14:!>
src/pc00aa.F90:15:!> <ul>
src/pc00aa.F90:16:!> <li> The following input variables control the operation of \c E04DGF :
src/pc00aa.F90:17:!>      <ul>
src/pc00aa.F90:18:!>      <li> \c epsilon :
src/pc00aa.F90:19:!>           weighting of "spectral energy"; see pc00ab() </li>
src/pc00aa.F90:20:!>      <li> \c maxstep :
src/pc00aa.F90:21:!>           this is given to \c E04DGF for the \f$\texttt{Maximum Step Length}\f$ </li>
src/pc00aa.F90:22:!>      <li> \c maxiter :
src/pc00aa.F90:23:!>           upper limit on derivative calculations used in the conjugate gradient iterations </li>
src/pc00aa.F90:24:!>      <li> \c verify :
src/pc00aa.F90:25:!>           if \c verify=1, then \c E04DGF
src/pc00aa.F90:26:!>           will confirm user supplied gradients (provided by pc00ab() ) are correct; </li>
src/pc00aa.F90:27:!>      </ul> </li>
src/pc00aa.F90:28:!> <li> \todo Unfortunately, \c E04DGF
src/pc00aa.F90:29:!>       seems to require approximately \f$3 N\f$ function evaluations before proceeding to minimize the energy functional,
src/pc00aa.F90:30:!>       where there are \f$N\f$ degrees of freedom.
src/pc00aa.F90:31:!>       I don't know how to turn this off!
src/pc00aa.F90:32:!>
src/pc00aa.F90:33:!> </li>
src/pc00aa.F90:34:!> </ul>
src/pc00aa.F90:35:!>
src/pc00aa.F90:36:!> @param[in]    NGdof
src/pc00aa.F90:37:!> @param[inout] position
src/pc00aa.F90:38:!> @param[in]    Nvol
src/pc00aa.F90:39:!> @param[in]    mn
src/pc00aa.F90:40:!> @param        ie04dgf
src/pc00ab.F90:1:!> \file
src/pc00ab.F90:2:!> \brief Returns the energy functional and it's derivatives with respect to geometry.
src/pc00ab.F90:4:!> \brief Returns the energy functional and it's derivatives with respect to geometry.
src/pc00ab.F90:5:!> \ingroup grp_conjugate_gradient
src/pc00ab.F90:6:!>
src/pc00ab.F90:7:!> **Energy functional**
src/pc00ab.F90:8:!>
src/pc00ab.F90:9:!> <ul>
src/pc00ab.F90:10:!> <li> The energy functional is
src/pc00ab.F90:11:!>      \f{eqnarray}{ F \equiv \sum_{l=1}^{N} \int_{\cal V} \left( \frac{p}{\gamma-1} + \frac{B^2}{2} \right) dv,
src/pc00ab.F90:12:!>      \label{eq:energyfunctional_pc00ab}
src/pc00ab.F90:13:!>      \f}
src/pc00ab.F90:14:!>      where \f$N \equiv\,\f$\c Nvol is the number of interfaces. </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:15:!> <li> Assuming that the toroidal and poloidal fluxes, \f$\psi_t\f$ and \f$\psi_p\f$, the helicity, \f${\cal K}\f$, the helicity multiplier, \f$\mu\f$,
src/pc00ab.F90:16:!>      and/or the interface rotational-transforms, \f${{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}\f$, are appropriately constrained,
src/pc00ab.F90:17:!>      the Beltrami fields in each volume depend only the geometry of the adjacent interfaces.
src/pc00ab.F90:18:!>      So, the energy functional is assumed to be a function of "position", i.e. \f$F = F(R_{l,j},Z_{l,j})\f$. </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:19:!> <li> Introducing a ficitious time, \f$t\f$, the position may be advanced according to
src/pc00ab.F90:20:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \begin{array}{cccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/pc00ab.F90:21:!>                     \displaystyle \frac{\partial R_j}{\partial t} & \equiv & \displaystyle
src/pc00ab.F90:22:!>                     - \frac{\partial }{\partial R_j} \sum_{l=1}^{N} \int \left( \frac{p}{\gamma-1} + \frac{B^2}{2} \right) dv,\\
src/pc00ab.F90:23:!>                     \displaystyle \frac{\partial Z_j}{\partial t} & \equiv & \displaystyle
src/pc00ab.F90:24:!>                     - \frac{\partial }{\partial Z_j} \sum_{l=1}^{N} \int \left( \frac{p}{\gamma-1} + \frac{B^2}{2} \right) dv.
src/pc00ab.F90:25:!>       \end{array} \label{eq:descent_pc00ab} \f} </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:26:!> <li> There remain degrees of freedom in the angle representation of the interfaces. </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:27:!> </ul>
src/pc00ab.F90:28:!>
src/pc00ab.F90:29:!> **Spectral energy minimization**
src/pc00ab.F90:30:!>
src/pc00ab.F90:31:!> <ul>
src/pc00ab.F90:32:!> <li> Consider variations which do not affect the geometry of the surfaces,
src/pc00ab.F90:33:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \delta R &=& R_\theta \; u,\\
src/pc00ab.F90:34:!>                     \delta Z &=& Z_\theta \; u,
src/pc00ab.F90:35:!>       \f}
src/pc00ab.F90:36:!>       where \f$u\f$ is a angle variation. </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:37:!> <li> The corresponding variation in each of the Fourier harmonics is
src/pc00ab.F90:38:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \delta R_j &\equiv& \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, R_\theta \; u \; \cos \alpha_j,\\
src/pc00ab.F90:39:!>                     \delta Z_j &\equiv& \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, Z_\theta \; u \; \sin \alpha_j,
src/pc00ab.F90:40:!>       \f} </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:41:!> <li> Following Hirshman et al., introducing the normalized spectral width
src/pc00ab.F90:42:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ M \equiv \frac{\sum_j ( m_j^p + n_j^q ) ( R_{l,j}^2+Z_{l,j}^2 )}{\sum_j ( R_{l,j}^2+Z_{l,j}^2 )},
src/pc00ab.F90:43:!>       \f} </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:44:!> <li> Using the notation
src/pc00ab.F90:45:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ N &\equiv& \sum_j \lambda_j ( R_{l,j}^2+Z_{l,j}^2 ), \\
src/pc00ab.F90:46:!>                     D &\equiv& \sum_j           ( R_{l,j}^2+Z_{l,j}^2 ),
src/pc00ab.F90:47:!>       \f}
src/pc00ab.F90:48:!>       where \f$\lambda_j \equiv m_j^p + n_j^q\f$,
src/pc00ab.F90:49:!>       the variation in the normalized spectral width is
src/pc00ab.F90:50:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \delta M = (\delta N - M \delta D)/D.
src/pc00ab.F90:51:!>       \f} </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:52:!> <li> For tangential variations,
src/pc00ab.F90:53:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \delta N &=& 2 \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \; u \left(R_\theta \sum_j \lambda_j R_j \cos \alpha_j + Z_\theta \sum_j \lambda_j Z_j \sin \alpha_j \right),\\
src/pc00ab.F90:54:!>                     \delta D &=& 2 \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \; u \left(R_\theta \sum_j           R_j \cos \alpha_j + Z_\theta \sum_j           Z_j \sin \alpha_j \right).
src/pc00ab.F90:55:!>       \f} </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:56:!> <li> The "tangential spectral-width descent direction" is thus
src/pc00ab.F90:57:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \frac{\partial u}{\partial t} &=& -\left[ R_\theta \sum_j(\lambda_j - M) R_j \cos \alpha_j / D + Z_\theta \sum_j(\lambda_j - M)Z_j \sin \alpha_j / D \right].
src/pc00ab.F90:58:!>       \f} </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:59:!> <li> This suggests that position should be advanced according to
src/pc00ab.F90:60:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \frac{\partial R_j}{\partial t} & \equiv &
src/pc00ab.F90:61:!>       - \frac{\partial }{\partial R_j} \sum_{l=1}^{N} \int \left( \frac{p}{\gamma-1} + \frac{B^2}{2} \right) dv - [R_\theta (R_\theta X + Z_\theta Y)]_j,\\
src/pc00ab.F90:62:!>           \frac{\partial Z_j}{\partial t} & \equiv &
src/pc00ab.F90:63:!>       - \frac{\partial }{\partial Z_j} \sum_{l=1}^{N} \int \left( \frac{p}{\gamma-1} + \frac{B^2}{2} \right) dv - [Z_\theta (R_\theta X + Z_\theta Y)]_j,
src/pc00ab.F90:64:!>       \f}
src/pc00ab.F90:65:!>       where \f$X \equiv \sum_j (\lambda_j - M)R_j \cos\alpha_j / D\f$ and \f$Y \equiv \sum_j (\lambda_j - M)Z_j \sin\alpha_j / D\f$. </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:66:!> </ul>
src/pc00ab.F90:67:!>
src/pc00ab.F90:68:!> **numerical implementation**
src/pc00ab.F90:69:!>
src/pc00ab.F90:70:!> <ul>
src/pc00ab.F90:71:!> <li> The spectral condensation terms,
src/pc00ab.F90:72:!> \f{eqnarray}{ R_\theta (R_\theta X + Z_\theta Y) &=& \sum_{j,k,l} m_j m_k (\lambda_l-M) R_j ( + R_k R_l \sin\alpha_j\sin\alpha_k\cos\alpha_l - Z_k Z_l \sin\alpha_j\cos\alpha_k\sin\alpha_l)/D,\\
src/pc00ab.F90:73:!>               Z_\theta (R_\theta X + Z_\theta Y) &=& \sum_{j,k,l} m_j m_k (\lambda_l-M) Z_j ( - R_k R_l \cos\alpha_j\sin\alpha_k\cos\alpha_l + Z_k Z_l \cos\alpha_j\cos\alpha_k\sin\alpha_l)/D,
src/pc00ab.F90:74:!> \f}
src/pc00ab.F90:75:!> are calculated using triple angle expressions...
src/pc00ab.F90:76:!> \todo IT IS VERY LIKELY THAT FFTs WOULD BE FASTER!!!
src/pc00ab.F90:77:!>
src/pc00ab.F90:78:!> </li>
src/pc00ab.F90:79:!> </ul>
src/pc00ab.F90:80:!>
src/pp00aa.F90:2:!> \file
src/pp00aa.F90:3:!> \brief Constructs Poincaré plot and "approximate" rotational-transform (driver).
src/pp00aa.F90:5:!> \brief Constructs Poincaré plot and "approximate" rotational-transform (driver).
src/pp00aa.F90:6:!> \ingroup grp_diagnostics
src/pp00aa.F90:7:!>
src/pp00aa.F90:8:!> **relevant input variables**
src/pp00aa.F90:9:!>
src/pp00aa.F90:10:!> <ul>
src/pp00aa.F90:11:!> <li> The resolution of Poincaré plot is controlled by
src/pp00aa.F90:12:!>       <ul>
src/pp00aa.F90:13:!>       <li> \c nPtraj trajectories will be located in each volume;
src/pp00aa.F90:14:!>       <li> \c nPpts  iterations per trajectory;
src/pp00aa.F90:15:!>       <li> \c odetol o.d.e. integration tolerance;
src/pp00aa.F90:16:!>       </ul> </li>
src/pp00aa.F90:17:!> <li> The magnetic field is given by bfield() . </li>
src/pp00aa.F90:18:!> <li> The approximate rotational transform is determined, in pp00ab() , by fieldline integration. </li>
src/pp00aa.F90:19:!> </ul>
src/pp00aa.F90:20:!>
src/pp00aa.F90:21:!> **format of output: Poincaré**
src/pp00aa.F90:22:!>
src/pp00aa.F90:23:!> <ul>
src/pp00aa.F90:24:!> <li> The Poincaré data is written to \c .ext.poincare:xxxx , where \c xxxx is an integer indicating the volume.
src/pp00aa.F90:25:!>       The format of this file is as follows:
src/pp00aa.F90:26:!>
src/pp00aa.F90:27:!>~~~~~~~~~~~~
src/pp00aa.F90:28:!> write(svol,'(i4.4)')lvol ! lvol labels volume;
src/pp00aa.F90:29:!> open(lunit+myid,file=trim(get_hidden(ext))//".poincare."//svol,status="unknown",form="unformatted")
src/pp00aa.F90:30:!> do until end of file
src/pp00aa.F90:31:!>   write(lunit+myid) Nz, nPpts                ! integers
src/pp00aa.F90:32:!>   write(lunit+myid) data(1:4,0:Nz-1,1:nPpts) ! doubles
src/pp00aa.F90:33:!> enddo
src/pp00aa.F90:34:!> close(lunit+myid)
src/pp00aa.F90:35:!>~~~~~~~~~~~~
src/pp00aa.F90:36:!> `where`
src/pp00aa.F90:37:!>       <ul>
src/pp00aa.F90:38:!>       <li> \f$\theta \equiv\,\f$\c data(1,k,j) is the poloidal angle,      </li>
src/pp00aa.F90:39:!>       <li> \f$ s     \equiv\,\f$\c data(2,k,j) is the radial coordinate,   </li>
src/pp00aa.F90:40:!>       <li> \f$ R     \equiv\,\f$\c data(3,k,j) is the cylindrical \f$R\f$, </li>
src/pp00aa.F90:41:!>       <li> \f$ Z     \equiv\,\f$\c data(4,k,j) is the cylindrical \f$Z\f$, </li>
src/pp00aa.F90:42:!>       </ul>
src/pp00aa.F90:43:!> <li> The integer \c k=0,Nz-1 labels toroidal planes, so that \f$\phi = ( 2 \pi / \texttt{Nfp}) ( k / \texttt{Nz})\f$,
src/pp00aa.F90:44:!> <li> The integer \c j=1,nPpts labels toroidal iterations.
src/pp00aa.F90:45:!> <li> Usually (if no fieldline integration errors are encountered) the number of fieldlines followed in volume \c lvol
src/pp00aa.F90:46:!>       is given by \f$N+1\f$, where the radial resolution, \f$N\equiv\,\f$\c Ni(lvol) , is given on input.
src/pp00aa.F90:47:!>       This will be over-ruled by if \c nPtrj(lvol) , given on input, is non-negative.
src/pp00aa.F90:48:!> <li> The starting location for the fieldline integrations are equally spaced in the radial coordinate \f$s_i=s_{l-1}+ i (s_{l}-s_{l-1})/N\f$ for \f$i=0,N\f$,
src/pp00aa.F90:49:!>       along the line \f$\theta=0\f$, \f$\zeta=0\f$.
src/pp00aa.F90:50:!> </ul>
src/pp00aa.F90:51:!>
src/pp00aa.F90:52:!> **format of output: rotational-transform**
src/pp00aa.F90:53:!>
src/pp00aa.F90:54:!> <ul>
src/pp00aa.F90:55:!>
src/pp00aa.F90:56:!> <li> The rotational-transform data is written to \c .ext.transform:xxxx , where \c xxxx is an integer indicating the volume.
src/pp00aa.F90:57:!>       The format of this file is as follows:
src/pp00aa.F90:58:!> ```
src/pp00aa.F90:59:!>  open(lunit+myid,file=trim(get_hidden(ext))//".sp.t."//svol,status="unknown",form="unformatted")
src/pp00aa.F90:60:!>  write(lunit+myid) lnPtrj-ioff+1                                      ! integer
src/pp00aa.F90:61:!>  write(lunit+myid) diotadxup(0:1,0,lvol)                              ! doubles
src/pp00aa.F90:62:!>  write(lunit+myid) ( fiota(itrj,1:2), itrj = ioff, lnPtrj ) ! doubles
src/pp00aa.F90:63:!>  close(lunit+myid)
src/pp00aa.F90:64:!> ```
src/pp00aa.F90:65:!> </ul>
src/pp00aa.F90:66:!>
src/pp00ab.F90:1:!> \file
src/pp00ab.F90:2:!> \brief Follows magnetic fieldline using ode-integration routine from rksuite.f .
src/pp00ab.F90:4:!> \brief Constructs Poincaré plot and "approximate" rotational-transform (for single field line).
src/pp00ab.F90:5:!> \ingroup grp_diagnostics
src/pp00ab.F90:6:!>
src/pp00ab.F90:7:!> **relevant input variables**
src/pp00ab.F90:8:!>
src/pp00ab.F90:9:!> <ul>
src/pp00ab.F90:10:!> <li> The resolution of Poincaré plot is controlled by
src/pp00ab.F90:11:!>      <ul>
src/pp00ab.F90:12:!>      <li> \c nPpts iterations per trajectory; </li>
src/pp00ab.F90:13:!>      <li> \c odetol o.d.e. integration tolerance; </li>
src/pp00ab.F90:14:!>      </ul>
src/pp00ab.F90:15:!>      The magnetic field is given by bfield() . </li>
src/pp00ab.F90:16:!> </ul>
src/pp00ab.F90:17:!>
src/pp00ab.F90:18:!> **rotational-transform**
src/pp00ab.F90:19:!>
src/pp00ab.F90:20:!> <ul>
src/pp00ab.F90:21:!> <li> The approximate rotational transform is determined by field line integration.
src/pp00ab.F90:22:!>      This is constructed by fitting a least squares fit to the field line trajectory. </li>
src/pp00ab.F90:23:!> </ul>
src/pp00ab.F90:24:!>
src/pp00ab.F90:25:!> @param[in]  lvol
src/pp00ab.F90:26:!> @param      sti
src/pp00ab.F90:27:!> @param[in]  Nz
src/pp00ab.F90:28:!> @param[in]  nPpts
src/pp00ab.F90:29:!> @param      poincaredata
src/pp00ab.F90:30:!> @param      fittedtransform
src/pp00ab.F90:31:!> @param[out] utflag
src/preset.F90:2:!> \defgroup grp_initialization Initialization of the code
src/preset.F90:3:!>
src/preset.F90:4:!> \latexonly
src/preset.F90:5:!> \definecolor{Orange}{rgb}{1.0,0.5,0.0}
src/preset.F90:6:!> \definecolor{Cerulean}{rgb}{0.0,0.5,1.0}
src/preset.F90:7:!> \endlatexonly
src/preset.F90:8:!>
src/preset.F90:9:!> \file
src/preset.F90:10:!> \brief Allocates and initializes internal arrays.
src/preset.F90:12:!> \brief Allocates and initializes internal arrays.
src/preset.F90:13:!> \ingroup grp_initialization
src/preset.F90:14:!>
src/preset.F90:429:!> **LGdof and NGdof : number of geometrical degrees-of-freedom**
src/preset.F90:430:!>
src/preset.F90:431:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:432:!> <li> \c LGdof \f$\equiv\f$ the number of degrees-of-freedom in the geometry (i.e. Fourier harmonics) of each interface </li>
src/preset.F90:433:!> <li> \c NGdof \f$\equiv\f$ total number of degrees-of-freedom in geometry, i.e. of all interfaces </li>
src/preset.F90:434:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:453:!> **iota and oita: rotational transform on interfaces**
src/preset.F90:454:!>
src/preset.F90:455:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:456:!> <li> The input variables \c iota and \c oita are the rotational transform
src/preset.F90:457:!>       on "inner-side" and on the "outer-side" of each interface. </li>
src/preset.F90:458:!> <li> These quantities are formally inputs. </li>
src/preset.F90:459:!> <li> Note that if \f$q_l+\gamma q_r \ne 0\f$, then \c iota is given by
src/preset.F90:460:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-} \equiv \frac{p_l + \gamma p_r}{q_l + \gamma q_r},
src/preset.F90:461:!>       \f}
src/preset.F90:462:!>       where \f$p_l \equiv\,\f$\c pl, \f$q_l \equiv\,\f$\c ql , etc.;
src/preset.F90:463:!>       and similarly for \c oita . </li>
src/preset.F90:464:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:485:!> **dtflux(1:Mvol) and dpflux(1:Mvol): enclosed fluxes**
src/preset.F90:486:!>
src/preset.F90:487:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:488:!> <li> \c dtflux \f$\equiv \Delta \psi_{tor} / 2\pi\f$ and
src/preset.F90:489:!>      \c dpflux \f$\equiv \Delta \psi_{pol} / 2\pi\f$ in each volume. </li>
src/preset.F90:490:!> <li> Note that the total toroidal flux enclosed by the plasma boundary is \f$\Phi_{edge} \equiv\,\f$\c phiedge . </li>
src/preset.F90:491:!> <li> \f$\psi_{tor} \equiv\,\f$\c tflux and \f$\psi_{pol} \equiv\,\f$\c pflux are immediately normalized (in readin() ) according to
src/preset.F90:492:!>      \f$\psi_{tor,i} \rightarrow \psi_{tor,i} / \psi_{0}\f$ and
src/preset.F90:493:!>      \f$\psi_{pol,i} \rightarrow \psi_{pol,i} / \psi_{0}\f$, where \f$\psi_{0} \equiv \psi_{tor,N}\f$ on input. </li>
src/preset.F90:494:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:538:!> **sweight(1:Mvol): star-like angle constraint weight**
src/preset.F90:539:!>
src/preset.F90:540:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:541:!> <li> the "star-like" poloidal angle constraint weights (only required for toroidal geometry, i.e. \c Igeometry=3) are given by
src/preset.F90:542:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \texttt{sweight}_v \equiv \texttt{upsilon} \times (l_v / N_{vol})^w,
src/preset.F90:543:!>       \f}
src/preset.F90:544:!>       where \f$l_v\f$ is the volume number,
src/preset.F90:545:!>       and \f$w \equiv\,\f$\c wpoloidal. </li>
src/preset.F90:546:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:554:!> **TT(0:Mrad,0:1,0:1): Chebyshev polynomials at inner/outer interface**
src/preset.F90:555:!>
src/preset.F90:556:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:557:!> <li> \c TT(0:Lrad,0:1,0:1) gives the Chebyshev polynomials, and their first derivative, evaluated at \f$s=-1\f$ and \f$s=+1\f$. </li>
src/preset.F90:558:!> <li> Precisely, \c TT(l,i,d) \f$\equiv T_l^{(d)}(s_i)\f$ for \f$s_0=-1\f$ and \f$s_1=+1\f$. </li>
src/preset.F90:559:!> <li> Note that \f$T_l^{(0)}(s)=s^l\f$ and \f$T_l^{(1)}(s)=s^{l+1} l^2\f$ for \f$s=\pm 1\f$. </li>
src/preset.F90:560:!> <li> Note that
src/preset.F90:561:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ T_l(-1)        = \left\{ \begin{array}{ccccccccccccccc}+1,& \textrm{ if $l$ is even,} \\
src/preset.F90:562:!>                                                                            -1,& \textrm{ if $l$ is odd;}
src/preset.F90:563:!>                                              \end{array} \right. & \; \;&
src/preset.F90:564:!>                     T_l(+1)        = \left\{ \begin{array}{ccccccccccccccc}+1,& \textrm{ if $l$ is even,} \\
src/preset.F90:565:!>                                                                            +1,& \textrm{ if $l$ is odd;}
src/preset.F90:566:!>                                              \end{array} \right. \\
src/preset.F90:567:!>                     T_l^\prime(-1) = \left\{ \begin{array}{ccccccccccccccc}-l^2,& \textrm{ if $l$ is even,} \\
src/preset.F90:568:!>                                                                            +l^2,& \textrm{ if $l$ is odd;}
src/preset.F90:569:!>                                              \end{array} \right. &\; \;&
src/preset.F90:570:!>                     T_l^\prime(+1) = \left\{ \begin{array}{ccccccccccccccc}+l^2,& \textrm{ if $l$ is even,} \\
src/preset.F90:571:!>                                                                            +l^2,& \textrm{ if $l$ is odd.}
src/preset.F90:572:!>                                               \end{array} \right.
src/preset.F90:573:!>       \f} </li>
src/preset.F90:574:!> <li> \c TT(0:Mrad,0:1,0:1) is used in routines that explicity require interface information, such as
src/preset.F90:575:!>       <ul>
src/preset.F90:576:!>         <li> the interface force-balance routine,                                lforce() </li>
src/preset.F90:577:!>         <li> the virtual casing routine,                                         casing() </li>
src/preset.F90:578:!>         <li> computing the rotational-transform on the interfaces,               tr00ab() </li>
src/preset.F90:579:!>         <li> computing the covariant components of the interface magnetic field, sc00aa() </li>
src/preset.F90:580:!>         <li> enforcing the constraints on the Beltrami fields,                   matrix() </li>
src/preset.F90:581:!>     and <li> computing the enclosed currents of the vacuum field,                curent(). </li>
src/preset.F90:582:!>       </ul> </li>
src/preset.F90:583:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:598:!> **ImagneticOK(1:Mvol): Beltrami/vacuum error flag**
src/preset.F90:599:!>
src/preset.F90:600:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:601:!> <li> error flags that indicate if the magnetic field in each volume has been successfully constructed </li>
src/preset.F90:602:!> <li> \c ImagneticOK is initialized to \c .false. in dforce() before the Beltrami solver routines are called.
src/preset.F90:603:!>       If the construction of the Beltrami field is successful
src/preset.F90:604:!>       (in either ma02aa() or mp00ac() )
src/preset.F90:605:!>       then \c ImagneticOK is set to \c .true. . </li>
src/preset.F90:606:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:621:!> **Lhessianallocated**
src/preset.F90:622:!>
src/preset.F90:623:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:624:!> <li> The internal logical variable, \c Lhessianallocated, indicates whether the ``Hessian'' matrix of second-partial derivatives
src/preset.F90:625:!>       (really, the first derivatives of the force-vector) has been allocated, or not! </li>
src/preset.F90:626:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:631:!> **ki(1:mn,0:1): Fourier identification**
src/preset.F90:632:!>
src/preset.F90:633:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:634:!> <li> Consider the "abbreviated" representation for a double Fourier series,
src/preset.F90:635:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \sum_i      f_i \cos(     m_i \theta -      n_i \zeta) \equiv                         \sum_{n=      0  }^{     N_0} f_{0,n} \cos(       -n\zeta)
src/preset.F90:636:!>                                                                                   + \sum_{m=1}^{     M_0} \sum_{n=-     N_0}^{     N_0} f_{m,n} \cos(m\theta-n\zeta),
src/preset.F90:637:!>       \f}
src/preset.F90:638:!>       and the same representation but with enhanced resolution,
src/preset.F90:639:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \sum_k \bar f_k \cos(\bar m_k \theta - \bar n_k \zeta) \equiv                         \sum_{n=      0  }^{     N_1} f_{0,n} \cos(       -n\zeta)
src/preset.F90:640:!>                                                                                   + \sum_{m=1}^{     M_1} \sum_{n=-     N_1}^{     N_1} f_{m,n} \cos(m\theta-n\zeta),
src/preset.F90:641:!>       \label{eq:enhancedFourierrepresentation_preset}
src/preset.F90:642:!>       \f}
src/preset.F90:643:!>       with \f$M_1 \ge M_0\f$ and \f$N_1 \ge N_0\f$;
src/preset.F90:644:!>       then \f$k_i\equiv\,\f$\c ki(i,0) is defined such that \f$\bar m_{k_i} = m_i\f$ and \f$\bar n_{k_i} = n_i\f$. </li>
src/preset.F90:645:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:649:!> **kija(1:mn,1:mn,0:1), kijs(1:mn,1:mn,0:1): Fourier identification**
src/preset.F90:650:!>
src/preset.F90:651:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:652:!> <li> Consider the following quantities, which are computed in ma00aa(),
src/preset.F90:653:!>       where \f$\bar g^{\mu\nu} = \sum_k \bar g^{\mu\nu}_k \cos \alpha_k\f$ for \f$\alpha_k \equiv m_k \theta - n_k \zeta\f$,
src/preset.F90:654:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/preset.F90:655:!>       \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \bar g^{\mu\nu} \cos\alpha_i \; \cos\alpha_j & = & \frac{1}{2} \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \bar g^{\mu\nu} ( + \cos\alpha_{k_{ij+}} + \cos\alpha_{k_{ij-}} ), \\
src/preset.F90:656:!>       \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \bar g^{\mu\nu} \cos\alpha_i \; \sin\alpha_j & = & \frac{1}{2} \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \bar g^{\mu\nu} ( + \sin\alpha_{k_{ij+}} - \sin\alpha_{k_{ij-}} ), \\
src/preset.F90:657:!>       \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \bar g^{\mu\nu} \sin\alpha_i \; \cos\alpha_j & = & \frac{1}{2} \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \bar g^{\mu\nu} ( + \sin\alpha_{k_{ij+}} + \sin\alpha_{k_{ij-}} ), \\
src/preset.F90:658:!>       \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \bar g^{\mu\nu} \sin\alpha_i \; \sin\alpha_j & = & \frac{1}{2} \oint\!\!\!\oint \!d\theta d\zeta \,\,\, \bar g^{\mu\nu} ( - \cos\alpha_{k_{ij+}} + \cos\alpha_{k_{ij-}} ),
src/preset.F90:659:!>       \f}
src/preset.F90:660:!>       where \f$(m_{k_{ij+}}, n_{k_{ij+}}) = (m_i + m_j, n_i + n_j)\f$ and \f$(m_{k_{ij-}}, n_{k_{ij-}}) = (m_i - m_j, n_i - n_j)\f$;
src/preset.F90:661:!>       then \c kija(i,j,0) \f$\equiv k_{ij+}\f$ and \c kijs(i,j,0) \f$\equiv k_{ij-}\f$. </li>
src/preset.F90:662:!> <li> Note that Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:enhancedFourierrepresentation_preset})\f$ does not include \f$m<0\f$; so,
src/preset.F90:663:!>       if \f$m_i - m_j < 0\f$ then \f$k_{ij-}\f$ is re-defined such that \f$(m_{k_{ij-}}, n_{k_{ij-}}) = (m_j - m_i, n_j - n_i)\f$; and
src/preset.F90:664:!>       similarly for the case \f$m=0\f$ and \f$n<0\f$.
src/preset.F90:665:!>       Also, take care that the sign of the sine harmonics in the above expressions will change for these cases. </li>
src/preset.F90:666:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:710:!> **djkp**
src/preset.F90:728:!> **iotakki**
src/preset.F90:772:!> **cheby(0:Lrad,0:2): Chebyshev polynomial workspace**
src/preset.F90:773:!>
src/preset.F90:774:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:775:!> <li> \c cheby(0:Lrad,0:2) is global workspace for computing the Chebyshev polynomials, and their derivatives,
src/preset.F90:776:!>       using the recurrence relations \f$T_0(s) = 1\f$, \f$T_1(s) = s\f$ and  \f$T_l(s) = 2 \, s \,T_{l-1}(s) - T_{l-2}(s)\f$. </li>
src/preset.F90:777:!> <li> These are computed as required, i.e. for arbitrary \f$s\f$, in bfield(), jo00aa() and ma00aa(). </li>
src/preset.F90:778:!> <li> Note that the quantities required for ma00aa() are for fixed \f$s\f$, and so these quantities should be precomputed. </li>
src/preset.F90:779:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:800:!> **Iquad, gaussianweight, gaussianabscissae: Gauss-Legendre quadrature**
src/preset.F90:801:!>
src/preset.F90:802:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:803:!> <li> The volume integrals are computed using a "Fourier" integration over the angles and by Gauss-Legendre quadrature over the radial,
src/preset.F90:804:!>       i.e. \f$\displaystyle \int \!\! f(s) ds = \sum_k \omega_k f(s_k)\f$. </li>
src/preset.F90:805:!> <li> The quadrature resolution in each volume is give by \c Iquad(1:Mvol) which is determined as follows:
src/preset.F90:806:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:807:!> <li> if \c Nquad.gt.0                                    , then \c Iquad(vvol)=Nquad                   </li>
src/preset.F90:808:!> <li> if \c Nquad.le.0 and \c .not.Lcoordinatesingularity , then \c Iquad(vvol)=2*Lrad(vvol)-Nquad      </li>
src/preset.F90:809:!> <li> if \c Nquad.le.0 and      \c Lcoordinatesingularity , then \c Iquad(vvol)=2*Lrad(vvol)-Nquad+Mpol </li>
src/preset.F90:810:!> </ul> </li>
src/preset.F90:811:!> <li> The Gaussian weights and abscissae are given by \c gaussianweight(1:maxIquad,1:Mvol) and \c gaussianabscissae(1:maxIquad,1:Mvol),
src/preset.F90:812:!>       which are computed using modified Numerical Recipes routine gauleg() . </li>
src/preset.F90:813:!> <li> \c Iquad\f$_v\f$ is passed through to ma00aa() to compute the volume integrals of the metric elements;
src/preset.F90:814:!>       also see jo00aa(), where \c Iquad\f$_v\f$ is used to compute the volume integrals of \f$||\nabla\times{\bf B} - \mu {\bf B}||\f$. </li>
src/preset.F90:815:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:871:!> **BBweight(1:mn): weighting of force-imbalance harmonics**
src/preset.F90:872:!>
src/preset.F90:873:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:874:!> <li> weight on force-imbalance harmonics;
src/preset.F90:875:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \texttt{BBweight}_i \equiv \texttt{opsilon} \times \exp\left[ - \texttt{escale} \times (m_i^2 + n_i^2) \right]
src/preset.F90:876:!>       \f} </li>
src/preset.F90:877:!> <li> this is only used in dforce() in constructing the force-imbalance vector </li>
src/preset.F90:878:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:890:!> **mmpp(1:mn): spectral condensation weight factors**
src/preset.F90:891:!>
src/preset.F90:892:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:893:!> <li> spectral condensation weight factors;
src/preset.F90:894:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \texttt{mmpp(i)} \equiv m_i^p,
src/preset.F90:895:!>       \f}
src/preset.F90:896:!>       where \f$p \equiv\,\f$\c pcondense . </li>
src/preset.F90:897:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:911:!> **NAdof, Ate, Aze, Ato and Azo: degrees-of-freedom in magnetic vector potential**
src/preset.F90:912:!>
src/preset.F90:913:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:914:!> <li> \c NAdof(1:Mvol) \f$\equiv\f$ total number of degrees-of-freedom in magnetic vector potential, including Lagrange multipliers, in each volume.
src/preset.F90:915:!>       This can de deduced from matrix(). </li>
src/preset.F90:916:!> <li> The components of the vector potential, \f${\bf A}=A_\theta \nabla + A_\zeta \nabla \zeta\f$, are
src/preset.F90:917:!>      \f{eqnarray}{
src/preset.F90:918:!>        A_\theta(s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{red}  A_{\theta,e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \label{eq:At_preset} \\
src/preset.F90:919:!>        A_\zeta( s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{blue} A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \label{eq:Az_preset}
src/preset.F90:920:!>      \f}
src/preset.F90:921:!>      where \f${\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \equiv \bar s^{m_i/2} \, T_l(s)\f$, \f$T_l(s)\f$ is the Chebyshev polynomial, and \f$\alpha_j \equiv m_j\theta-n_j\zeta\f$.
src/preset.F90:922:!>      The regularity factor, \f$\bar s^{m_i/2}\f$, where \f$\bar s \equiv (1+s)/2\f$, is only included if there is a coordinate singularity in the domain
src/preset.F90:923:!>      (i.e. only in the innermost volume, and only in cylindrical and toroidal geometry.) </li>
src/preset.F90:924:!> <li> The Chebyshev-Fourier harmonics of the covariant components of the magnetic vector potential are kept in
src/preset.F90:925:!>      \f{eqnarray}{
src/preset.F90:926:!>          {\color{red}     A_{\theta,e,i,l}} &\equiv& \texttt{Ate(v,0,j)}\%\texttt{s(l)} , \\
src/preset.F90:927:!>          {\color{blue}    A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} &\equiv& \texttt{Aze(v,0,j)}\%\texttt{s(l)} , \\
src/preset.F90:928:!>          {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} &\equiv& \texttt{Ato(v,0,j)}\%\texttt{s(l)} , \mathrm{and} \\
src/preset.F90:929:!>          {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} &\equiv& \texttt{Azo(v,0,j)}\%\texttt{s(l)} ;
src/preset.F90:930:!>      \f}
src/preset.F90:931:!>      where \f$v=1,\texttt{Mvol}\f$ labels volume, \f$j=1,\texttt{mn}\f$ labels Fourier harmonic, and \f$l=0,\,\f$\c Lrad \f$(v)\f$ labels Chebyshev polynomial.
src/preset.F90:932:!>      (These arrays also contains derivative information.) </li>
src/preset.F90:933:!> <li> If \c Linitguess=1 , a guess for the initial state for the Beltrami fields is constructed.
src/preset.F90:934:!>      An initial state is required for iterative solvers of the Beltrami fields. </li>
src/preset.F90:935:!> <li> If \c Linitguess=2 , the initial state for the Beltrami fields is read from file (see ra00aa() ).
src/preset.F90:936:!>      An initial state is required for iterative solvers of the Beltrami fields. </li>
src/preset.F90:937:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:1225:!> **workspace**
src/preset.F90:1269:!> **goomne, goomno: metric information**
src/preset.F90:1270:!> These are defined in metrix() , and used in ma00aa().
src/preset.F90:1271:!>
src/preset.F90:1272:!> **gssmne, gssmno: metric information**
src/preset.F90:1273:!> These are defined in metrix() , and used in ma00aa().
src/preset.F90:1274:!>
src/preset.F90:1275:!> **gstmne, gstmno: metric information**
src/preset.F90:1276:!> These are defined in metrix() , and used in ma00aa().
src/preset.F90:1277:!>
src/preset.F90:1278:!> **gszmne, gszmno: metric information**
src/preset.F90:1279:!> These are defined in metrix() , and used in ma00aa().
src/preset.F90:1280:!>
src/preset.F90:1281:!> **gttmne, gttmno: metric information**
src/preset.F90:1282:!> These are defined in metrix() , and used in ma00aa().
src/preset.F90:1283:!>
src/preset.F90:1284:!> **gtzmne, gtzmno: metric information**
src/preset.F90:1285:!> These are defined in metrix() , and used in ma00aa().
src/preset.F90:1286:!>
src/preset.F90:1287:!> **gzzmne, gzzmno: metric information**
src/preset.F90:1288:!> These are defined in metrix() , and used in ma00aa().
src/preset.F90:1289:!>
src/preset.F90:1335:!> **cosi(1:Ntz,1:mn) and sini(1:Ntz,1:mn)**
src/preset.F90:1336:!>
src/preset.F90:1337:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:1338:!> <li> Trigonometric factors used in various Fast Fourier transforms, where
src/preset.F90:1339:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \texttt{cosi}_{j,i} & = & \cos( m_i \theta_j - n_i \zeta_j ), \\
src/preset.F90:1340:!>                     \texttt{sini}_{j,i} & = & \sin( m_i \theta_j - n_i \zeta_j ).
src/preset.F90:1341:!>       \f} </li>
src/preset.F90:1342:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:1385:!> **psifactor(1:mn,1:Mvol): coordinate "pre-conditioning" factor**
src/preset.F90:1386:!>
src/preset.F90:1387:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:1388:!> <li> In toroidal geometry, the coordinate "pre-conditioning" factor is
src/preset.F90:1389:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ f_{j,v} \equiv \left\{
src/preset.F90:1390:!>       \begin{array}{lcccccc}\psi_{t,v}^{0    }&,&\mbox{for $m_j=0$}, \\
src/preset.F90:1391:!>                             \psi_{t,v}^{m_j/2}&,&\mbox{otherwise}.
src/preset.F90:1392:!>       \end{array}\right.
src/preset.F90:1393:!>       \f}
src/preset.F90:1394:!>       where \f$\psi_{t,v} \equiv\,\f$\c tflux is the (normalized?) toroidal flux enclosed by the \f$v\f$-th interface. </li>
src/preset.F90:1395:!> <li> \c psifactor is used in packxi(), dforce() and hesian(). </li>
src/preset.F90:1396:!> <li> \c inifactor is similarly constructed, with
src/preset.F90:1397:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ f_{j,v} \equiv \left\{
src/preset.F90:1398:!>       \begin{array}{lcccccc}\psi_{t,v}^{ 1 /2}&,&\mbox{for $m_j=0$}, \\
src/preset.F90:1399:!>                             \psi_{t,v}^{m_j/2}&,&\mbox{otherwise}.
src/preset.F90:1400:!>       \end{array}\right.
src/preset.F90:1401:!>       \f}
src/preset.F90:1402:!>       and used only for the initialization of the surfaces taking into account axis information if provided. </li>
src/preset.F90:1403:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:1518:!> **Bsupumn and Bsupvmn**
src/preset.F90:1525:!> **diotadxup and glambda: transformation to straight fieldline angle**
src/preset.F90:1526:!>
src/preset.F90:1527:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:1528:!> <li> Given the Beltrami fields in any volume, the rotational-transform on the adjacent interfaces
src/preset.F90:1529:!>       may be determined (in tr00ab()) by constructing the straight fieldline angle on the interfaces. </li>
src/preset.F90:1530:!> <li> The rotational transform on the inner or outer interface of a given volume depends on the magnetic field in that volume,
src/preset.F90:1531:!>       i.e. \f${{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_\pm = {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}({\bf B}_\pm)\f$,
src/preset.F90:1532:!>       so that
src/preset.F90:1533:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \delta {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_\pm = \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_\pm}{\partial {\bf B}_\pm} \cdot \delta {\bf B_\pm}.
src/preset.F90:1534:!>       \f} </li>
src/preset.F90:1535:!> <li> The magnetic field depends on the Fourier harmonics of both the inner and outer interface geometry (represented here as \f$x_j\f$),
src/preset.F90:1536:!>       the helicity multiplier, and the enclosed poloidal flux, i.e. \f${\bf B_\pm} = {\bf B_\pm}(x_j, \mu, \Delta \psi_p)\f$, so that
src/preset.F90:1537:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \delta {\bf B_\pm} = \frac{\partial {\bf B}_\pm}{\partial x_j          } \delta x_j
src/preset.F90:1538:!>                                        + \frac{\partial {\bf B}_\pm}{\partial \mu          } \delta \mu
src/preset.F90:1539:!>                                        + \frac{\partial {\bf B}_\pm}{\partial \Delta \psi_p} \delta \Delta \psi_p.
src/preset.F90:1540:!>       \f} </li>
src/preset.F90:1541:!> <li> The rotational-transforms, thus, can be considered to be functions of the geometry, the helicity-multiplier and the enclosed poloidal flux,
src/preset.F90:1542:!>       \f${{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_{\pm} = {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_{\pm}(x_j,\mu,\Delta\psi_p)\f$. </li>
src/preset.F90:1543:!> <li> The rotational-transform, and its derivatives, on the inner and outer interfaces of each volume is stored in
src/preset.F90:1544:!>       \c diotadxup(0:1,-1:2,1:Mvol) .
src/preset.F90:1545:!>       The indices label:
src/preset.F90:1546:!>       <ul>
src/preset.F90:1547:!>       <li> the first index labels the inner or outer interface, </li>
src/preset.F90:1548:!>       <li> the second one labels derivative, with  </li>
src/preset.F90:1549:!>       <ul> <li>\c -1 : indicating the derivative with respect to the interface geometry,
src/preset.F90:1550:!>                                 i.e. \f$\displaystyle \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_{\pm}}{\partial x_j}\f$, </li>
src/preset.F90:1551:!>            <li>\c 0 : the rotational-transform itself, </li>
src/preset.F90:1552:!>            <li>\c 1,2 : the derivatives with respec to \f$\mu\f$ and \f$\Delta \psi_p\f$,
src/preset.F90:1553:!>                    i.e. \f$\displaystyle \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_{\pm}}{\partial \mu}\f$ and
src/preset.F90:1554:!>                         \f$\displaystyle \frac{\partial {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}_{\pm}}{\partial \Delta \psi_p}\f$; </li>
src/preset.F90:1555:!>       </ul> </li>
src/preset.F90:1556:!>       <li>The 1.0_rkind / 3.0_rkind index labels volume. </li>
src/preset.F90:1557:!>       </ul> </li>
src/preset.F90:1558:!> <li> The values of \c diotadxup are assigned in mp00aa() after calling tr00ab(). </li>
src/preset.F90:1559:!> </ul>
src/preset.F90:1596:!> **vvolume, lBBintegral and lABintegral**
src/preset.F90:1597:!>
src/preset.F90:1598:!> <ul>
src/preset.F90:1599:!> <li> volume integrals
src/preset.F90:1600:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \texttt{vvolume(i)}     &=& \int_{{\cal V}_i}                       \, dv\\
src/preset.F90:1601:!>                     \texttt{lBBintegral(i)} &=& \int_{{\cal V}_i} {\bf B} \cdot {\bf B} \, dv\\
src/preset.F90:1602:!>                     \texttt{lABintegral(i)} &=& \int_{{\cal V}_i} {\bf A} \cdot {\bf B} \, dv
src/preset.F90:1603:!>       \f} </li>
src/preset.F90:1604:!> </ul>
src/ra00aa.F90:2:!> \defgroup grp_output Output file(s)
src/ra00aa.F90:3:!>
src/ra00aa.F90:4:!> \file
src/ra00aa.F90:5:!> \brief Writes vector potential to \c .ext.sp.A .
src/ra00aa.F90:7:!> \brief Writes vector potential to \c .ext.sp.A .
src/ra00aa.F90:8:!> \ingroup grp_output
src/ra00aa.F90:9:!>
src/ra00aa.F90:10:!> **representation of vector potential**
src/ra00aa.F90:11:!>
src/ra00aa.F90:12:!> <ul>
src/ra00aa.F90:13:!> <li> The components of the vector potential, \f${\bf A}=A_\theta \nabla + A_\zeta \nabla \zeta\f$, are
src/ra00aa.F90:14:!>      \f{eqnarray}{
src/ra00aa.F90:15:!>        A_\theta(s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{red}  A_{\theta,e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Orange}  A_{\theta,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \label{eq:At_ra00aa} \\
src/ra00aa.F90:16:!>        A_\zeta( s,\theta,\zeta) &=& \sum_{i,l} {\color{blue} A_{\zeta, e,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \cos\alpha_i + \sum_{i,l} {\color{Cerulean}A_{\zeta ,o,i,l}} \; {\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \sin\alpha_i, \label{eq:Az_ra00aa}
src/ra00aa.F90:17:!>      \f}
src/ra00aa.F90:18:!>      where \f${\overline T}_{l,i}(s) \equiv \bar s^{m_i/2} \, T_l(s)\f$, \f$T_l(s)\f$ is the Chebyshev polynomial, and \f$\alpha_j \equiv m_j\theta-n_j\zeta\f$.
src/ra00aa.F90:19:!>      The regularity factor, \f$\bar s^{m_i/2}\f$, where \f$\bar s \equiv (1+s)/2\f$, is only included if there is a coordinate singularity in the domain
src/ra00aa.F90:20:!>      (i.e. only in the innermost volume, and only in cylindrical and toroidal geometry.) </li>
src/ra00aa.F90:21:!> </ul>
src/ra00aa.F90:22:!>
src/ra00aa.F90:23:!> **file format**
src/ra00aa.F90:24:!>
src/ra00aa.F90:25:!> <ul>
src/ra00aa.F90:26:!> <li> The format of the files containing the vector potential is as follows:
src/ra00aa.F90:27:!> ```
src/ra00aa.F90:28:!> open(aunit, file=trim(get_hidden(ext))//".sp.A", status="replace", form="unformatted" )
src/ra00aa.F90:29:!> write(aunit) Mvol, Mpol, Ntor, mn, Nfp ! integers;
src/ra00aa.F90:30:!> write(aunit) im(1:mn) ! integers; poloidal modes;
src/ra00aa.F90:31:!> write(aunit) in(1:mn) ! integers; toroidal modes;
src/ra00aa.F90:32:!> do vvol = 1, Mvol ! integers; loop over volumes;
src/ra00aa.F90:33:!> write(aunit) Lrad(vvol) ! integers; the radial resolution in each volume may be different;
src/ra00aa.F90:34:!> do ii = 1, mn
src/ra00aa.F90:35:!> write(aunit) Ate(vvol,ii)%s(0:Lrad(vvol)) ! reals;
src/ra00aa.F90:36:!> write(aunit) Aze(vvol,ii)%s(0:Lrad(vvol)) ! reals;
src/ra00aa.F90:37:!> write(aunit) Ato(vvol,ii)%s(0:Lrad(vvol)) ! reals;
src/ra00aa.F90:38:!> write(aunit) Azo(vvol,ii)%s(0:Lrad(vvol)) ! reals;
src/ra00aa.F90:39:!> enddo ! end of do ii;
src/ra00aa.F90:40:!> enddo ! end of do vvol;
src/ra00aa.F90:41:!> close(aunit)
src/ra00aa.F90:42:!> ```
src/ra00aa.F90:43:!> </li>
src/ra00aa.F90:44:!> </ul>
src/ra00aa.F90:45:!>
src/ra00aa.F90:46:!> @param[in] writeorread 'W' to write the vector potential; 'R' to read it
src/rzaxis.F90:2:!> \defgroup grp_coord_axis Coordinate axis
src/rzaxis.F90:3:!>
src/rzaxis.F90:4:!> \file
src/rzaxis.F90:5:!> \brief The coordinate axis is assigned via a poloidal average over an arbitrary surface.
src/rzaxis.F90:7:!> \brief The coordinate axis is assigned via a poloidal average over an arbitrary surface.
src/rzaxis.F90:8:!> \ingroup grp_coord_axis
src/rzaxis.F90:9:!>
src/rzaxis.F90:10:!> Specifies position of coordinate axis; \f${\bf x}_a(\zeta) \equiv \int {\bf x}_1(\theta,\zeta) dl \, / \int dl\f$.
src/rzaxis.F90:11:!>
src/rzaxis.F90:12:!> **coordinate axis**
src/rzaxis.F90:13:!>
src/rzaxis.F90:14:!> <ul>
src/rzaxis.F90:15:!> <li> The coordinate axis is _not_ an independent degree-of-freedom of the geometry.
src/rzaxis.F90:16:!>       It is constructed by extrapolating the geometry of a given interface, as determined by \f$i \equiv\,\f$\c ivol which is given on input,
src/rzaxis.F90:17:!>       down to a line.
src/rzaxis.F90:18:!> <li> If the coordinate axis depends only on the _geometry_ of the interface and not the angle parameterization,
src/rzaxis.F90:19:!>       then the block tri-diagonal structure of the the force-derivative matrix is preserved.
src/rzaxis.F90:20:!> <li> Define the arc-length-weighted averages,
src/rzaxis.F90:21:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ R_0(\zeta) \equiv \frac{\displaystyle \int_{0}^{2\pi} R_i(\theta,\zeta) \, dl}{\displaystyle \int_{0}^{2\pi} \!\!\!\! dl}, \qquad
src/rzaxis.F90:22:!>                     Z_0(\zeta) \equiv \frac{\displaystyle \int_{0}^{2\pi} Z_i(\theta,\zeta) \, dl}{\displaystyle \int_{0}^{2\pi} \!\!\!\! dl},
src/rzaxis.F90:23:!>       \f}
src/rzaxis.F90:24:!>       where \f$dl \equiv \dot l \, d\theta = \sqrt{ \partial_\theta R_i(\theta,\zeta)^2 + \partial_\theta Z_i(\theta,\zeta)^2 } \, d\theta\f$.
src/rzaxis.F90:25:!> <li> (Note that if \f$\dot l\f$ does not depend on \f$\theta\f$, i.e. if \f$\theta\f$ is the equal arc-length angle, then the expressions simplify.
src/rzaxis.F90:26:!>        This constraint is not enforced.)
src/rzaxis.F90:27:!> <li> The geometry of the coordinate axis thus constructed only depends on the geometry of the interface, i.e.
src/rzaxis.F90:28:!>       the angular parameterization of the interface is irrelevant.
src/rzaxis.F90:29:!> </ul>
src/rzaxis.F90:30:!>
src/rzaxis.F90:31:!> **coordinate axis: derivatives**
src/rzaxis.F90:32:!>
src/rzaxis.F90:33:!> <ul>
src/rzaxis.F90:34:!> <li> The derivatives of the coordinate axis with respect to the Fourier harmonics of the given interface are given by
src/rzaxis.F90:35:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/rzaxis.F90:36:!>       \displaystyle \frac{\partial R_0}{\partial R_{i,j}^c} & = & \displaystyle \int \left( \cos\alpha_j \; \dot l
src/rzaxis.F90:37:!>                                                     -       \Delta R_i R_{i,\theta} \, m_j \sin\alpha_j / \; \dot l \right) d\theta / L \\
src/rzaxis.F90:38:!>       \displaystyle \frac{\partial R_0}{\partial R_{i,j}^s} & = & \displaystyle \int \left( \sin\alpha_j \; \dot l
src/rzaxis.F90:39:!>                                                     +       \Delta R_i R_{i,\theta} \, m_j \cos\alpha_j / \; \dot l \right) d\theta / L \\
src/rzaxis.F90:40:!>       \displaystyle \frac{\partial R_0}{\partial Z_{i,j}^c} & = & \displaystyle \int \left( \;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\,
src/rzaxis.F90:41:!>                                                     -       \Delta R_i Z_{i,\theta} \, m_j \sin\alpha_j / \; \dot l \right) d\theta / L \\
src/rzaxis.F90:42:!>       \displaystyle \frac{\partial R_0}{\partial Z_{i,j}^s} & = & \displaystyle \int \left( \;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\,
src/rzaxis.F90:43:!>                                                     +       \Delta R_i Z_{i,\theta} \, m_j \cos\alpha_j / \; \dot l \right) d\theta / L \\ \nonumber \\
src/rzaxis.F90:44:!>       \displaystyle \frac{\partial Z_0}{\partial R_{i,j}^c} & = & \displaystyle \int \left( \;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\,
src/rzaxis.F90:45:!>                                                     -       \Delta Z_i R_{i,\theta} \, m_j \sin\alpha_j / \; \dot l \right) d\theta / L \\
src/rzaxis.F90:46:!>       \displaystyle \frac{\partial Z_0}{\partial R_{i,j}^s} & = & \displaystyle \int \left( \;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;
src/rzaxis.F90:47:!>                                                     +       \Delta Z_i R_{i,\theta} \, m_j \cos\alpha_j / \; \dot l \right) d\theta / L \\
src/rzaxis.F90:48:!>       \displaystyle \frac{\partial Z_0}{\partial Z_{i,j}^c} & = & \displaystyle \int \left( \cos\alpha_j \; \dot l
src/rzaxis.F90:49:!>                                                     -       \Delta Z_i Z_{i,\theta} \, m_j \sin\alpha_j / \; \dot l \right) d\theta / L \\
src/rzaxis.F90:50:!>       \displaystyle \frac{\partial Z_0}{\partial Z_{i,j}^s} & = & \displaystyle \int \left( \sin\alpha_j \; \dot l
src/rzaxis.F90:51:!>                                                     +       \Delta Z_i Z_{i,\theta} \, m_j \cos\alpha_j / \; \dot l \right) d\theta / L
src/rzaxis.F90:52:!>       \f}
src/rzaxis.F90:53:!>       where \f$\displaystyle L(\zeta) \equiv \int_{0}^{2\pi} \!\!\!\! dl\f$.
src/rzaxis.F90:54:!> </ul>
src/rzaxis.F90:55:!>
src/rzaxis.F90:56:!> **some numerical comments**
src/rzaxis.F90:57:!>
src/rzaxis.F90:58:!> <ul>
src/rzaxis.F90:59:!> <li> First, the differential poloidal length, \f$\dot l \equiv \sqrt{ R_\theta^2 + Z_\theta^2 }\f$, is computed in real space using
src/rzaxis.F90:60:!>       an inverse FFT from the Fourier harmonics of \f$R\f$ and \f$Z\f$.
src/rzaxis.F90:61:!> <li> Second, the Fourier harmonics of \f$dl\f$ are computed using an FFT.
src/rzaxis.F90:62:!>       The integration over \f$\theta\f$ to construct \f$L\equiv \int dl\f$ is now trivial: just multiply the \f$m=0\f$ harmonics of \f$dl\f$ by \f$2\pi\f$.
src/rzaxis.F90:63:!>       The \c ajk(1:mn) variable is used, and this is assigned in readin() .
src/rzaxis.F90:64:!> <li> Next, the weighted \f$R \, dl\f$ and \f$Z \, dl\f$ are computed in real space, and the poloidal integral is similarly taken.
src/rzaxis.F90:65:!> <li> Last, the Fourier harmonics are constructed using an FFT after dividing in real space.
src/rzaxis.F90:66:!> </ul>
src/rzaxis.F90:67:!>
src/rzaxis.F90:68:!> @param[in]  Mvol
src/rzaxis.F90:69:!> @param[in]  mn
src/rzaxis.F90:70:!> @param      inRbc
src/rzaxis.F90:71:!> @param      inZbs
src/rzaxis.F90:72:!> @param      inRbs
src/rzaxis.F90:73:!> @param      inZbc
src/rzaxis.F90:74:!> @param[in]  ivol
src/rzaxis.F90:75:!> @param      LcomputeDerivatives

src/tr00ab.F90:2:!> \defgroup grp_transform Rotational Transform
src/tr00ab.F90:3:!>
src/tr00ab.F90:4:!> \file
src/tr00ab.F90:5:!> \brief Calculates rotational transform given an arbitrary tangential field.
src/tr00ab.F90:7:!> \brief Calculates rotational transform given an arbitrary tangential field.
src/tr00ab.F90:8:!> \ingroup grp_transform
src/tr00ab.F90:9:!>
src/tr00ab.F90:10:!> Calculates transform, \f$ \iota \hspace{-0.35em}\)-\( = \dot \theta ( 1 + \lambda_\theta) + \lambda_\zeta \f$, given \f${\bf B}|_{\cal I}\f$.
src/tr00ab.F90:11:!>
src/tr00ab.F90:12:!> **constructing straight field line angle on interfaces**
src/tr00ab.F90:13:!>
src/tr00ab.F90:14:!> <ul>
src/tr00ab.F90:15:!> <li> The algorithm stems from introducing a straight field line angle \f$\theta_s=\theta+\lambda(\theta,\zeta)\f$, where
src/tr00ab.F90:16:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \lambda=\sum_j \lambda_{o,j}\sin(m_j\theta-n_j\zeta) + \sum_j \lambda_{e,j}\cos(m_j\theta-n_j\zeta)
src/tr00ab.F90:17:!>       \f}
src/tr00ab.F90:18:!>       and insisting that
src/tr00ab.F90:19:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/tr00ab.F90:20:!>       \frac{{\bf B}\cdot \nabla \theta_s}{{\bf B}\cdot \nabla \zeta} = \dot \theta ( 1 + \lambda_\theta) + \lambda_\zeta = {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-},
src/tr00ab.F90:21:!>       \f}
src/tr00ab.F90:22:!>       where \f${{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}\f$ is a constant that is to be determined. </li>
src/tr00ab.F90:23:!> <li> Writing \f$\dot \theta = - \partial_s A_\zeta / \partial_s A_\theta\f$, we have
src/tr00ab.F90:24:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \label{eq:sfla_tr00ab}
src/tr00ab.F90:25:!>       \partial_s A_\theta \, {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-} + \partial_s A_\zeta \, \lambda_\theta - \partial_s A_\theta \, \lambda_\zeta = - \partial_s A_\zeta
src/tr00ab.F90:26:!>       \f} </li>
src/tr00ab.F90:27:!> <li> Expanding this equation we obtain
src/tr00ab.F90:28:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/tr00ab.F90:29:!>         &   & \left( A_{\theta,e,k}^\prime \cos\alpha_k + A_{\theta,o,k}^\prime \sin\alpha_k\right) \, {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-} \nonumber \\
src/tr00ab.F90:30:!>         & + & \left( A_{\zeta ,e,k}^\prime \cos\alpha_k + A_{\zeta ,o,k}^\prime \sin\alpha_k\right) \, \left( +m_j \lambda_{o,j} \cos\alpha_j - m_j \lambda_{e,j} \sin\alpha_j
src/tr00ab.F90:31:!>               \right) \nonumber \\
src/tr00ab.F90:32:!>         & - & \left( A_{\theta,e,k}^\prime \cos\alpha_k + A_{\theta,o,k}^\prime \sin\alpha_k\right) \, \left( -n_j \lambda_{o,j} \cos\alpha_j + n_j \lambda_{e,j} \sin\alpha_j
src/tr00ab.F90:33:!>               \right) \nonumber \\
src/tr00ab.F90:34:!>       = & - & \left( A_{\zeta ,e,k}^\prime \cos\alpha_k + A_{\zeta ,o,k}^\prime \sin\alpha_k\right),
src/tr00ab.F90:35:!>       \f}
src/tr00ab.F90:36:!>       where summation over \f$k=1,\,\f$\c mn and \f$j=2,\,\f$\c mns is implied </li>
src/tr00ab.F90:37:!> <li> After applying double angle formulae,
src/tr00ab.F90:38:!>       \f{eqnarray}{
src/tr00ab.F90:39:!>         &   & \left( A_{\theta,e,k}^\prime \cos\alpha_k + A_{\theta,o,k}^\prime \sin\alpha_k\right) \, {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-} \nonumber \\
src/tr00ab.F90:40:!>         & + & \lambda_{o,j} \left( + m_j A_{\zeta,e,k}^\prime + n_j A_{\theta,e,k}^\prime \right) \left[ +\cos(\alpha_k+\alpha_j)+\cos(\alpha_k-\alpha_j)\right]/2 \nonumber \\
src/tr00ab.F90:41:!>         & + & \lambda_{e,j} \left( - m_j A_{\zeta,e,k}^\prime - n_j A_{\theta,e,k}^\prime \right) \left[ +\sin(\alpha_k+\alpha_j)-\sin(\alpha_k-\alpha_j)\right]/2 \nonumber \\
src/tr00ab.F90:42:!>         & + & \lambda_{o,j} \left( + m_j A_{\zeta,o,k}^\prime + n_j A_{\theta,o,k}^\prime \right) \left[ +\sin(\alpha_k+\alpha_j)+\sin(\alpha_k-\alpha_j)\right]/2 \nonumber \\
src/tr00ab.F90:43:!>         & + & \lambda_{e,j} \left( - m_j A_{\zeta,o,k}^\prime - n_j A_{\theta,o,k}^\prime \right) \left[ -\cos(\alpha_k+\alpha_j)+\cos(\alpha_k-\alpha_j)\right]/2 \nonumber \\
src/tr00ab.F90:44:!>       = & - & \left( A_{\zeta,e,k}^\prime \cos\alpha_k + A_{\zeta,o,k}^\prime \sin\alpha_k\right),
src/tr00ab.F90:45:!>       \f}
src/tr00ab.F90:46:!>       and equating coefficients, an equation of the form \f${\bf A} \cdot {\bf x} = {\bf b}\f$ is obtained, where
src/tr00ab.F90:47:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ {\bf x} = ( \underbrace{{{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}}_{\verb!x[1]!} \; , \;
src/tr00ab.F90:48:!>                       \underbrace{\lambda_{o,2}, \lambda_{o,3},\dots}_{\verb!x[  2: N  ]!} \; , \;
src/tr00ab.F90:49:!>                       \underbrace{\lambda_{e,2}, \lambda_{e,3},\dots}_{\verb!x[N+1:2N-1]!} \;
src/tr00ab.F90:50:!>                      )^T.
src/tr00ab.F90:51:!>       \f} </li>
src/tr00ab.F90:52:!> </ul>
src/tr00ab.F90:53:!>
src/tr00ab.F90:54:!> **alternative iterative method**
src/tr00ab.F90:55:!>
src/tr00ab.F90:56:!> <ul>
src/tr00ab.F90:57:!> <li> Consider the equation \f$\dot \theta ( 1 + \lambda_\theta ) + \lambda_\zeta = {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-}\f$, where \f$\lambda = \sum_j \lambda_j \sin\alpha_j\f$, given on a grid
src/tr00ab.F90:58:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \dot \theta_i + \dot \theta_i \sum_j m_j \cos \alpha_{i,j} \lambda_j - \sum_j n_j \cos \alpha_{i,j} \lambda_j = {{\,\iota\!\!\!}-},
src/tr00ab.F90:59:!>       \f}
src/tr00ab.F90:60:!>       where \f$i\f$ labels the grid point. </li>
src/tr00ab.F90:61:!> <li> This is a matrix equation... </li>
src/tr00ab.F90:62:!> </ul>
src/tr00ab.F90:63:!>
src/tr00ab.F90:64:!> @param lvol
src/tr00ab.F90:65:!> @param mn
src/tr00ab.F90:66:!> @param NN
src/tr00ab.F90:67:!> @param Nt
src/tr00ab.F90:68:!> @param Nz
src/tr00ab.F90:69:!> @param iflag
src/tr00ab.F90:70:!> @param ldiota
src/utils/errors_mod.F90:14:    !> Abort run if condition is met
src/utils/errors_mod.F90:16:        !> Condition triggering the abort
src/utils/errors_mod.F90:18:        !> Filename where the error was detected
src/utils/errors_mod.F90:20:        !> Message to be displayed
src/utils/conversion_mod.F90:9:  !> Convert an integer to a string
src/utils/conversion_mod.F90:11:  !> Note that the max length is 20 digits.
src/volume_mod.F90:10:  !> \defgroup grp_volume Plasma volume
src/volume_mod.F90:11:!>
src/volume_mod.F90:12:!> \file
src/volume_mod.F90:13:!> \brief Computes volume of each region; and, if required, the derivatives of the volume with respect to the interface geometry.
src/volume_mod.F90:15:!> \brief Computes volume of each region; and, if required, the derivatives of the volume with respect to the interface geometry.
src/volume_mod.F90:16:!> \ingroup grp_volume
src/volume_mod.F90:17:!>
src/volume_mod.F90:18:!> Calculates volume of each region; \f${\cal V}_i \equiv \int dv\f$.
src/volume_mod.F90:19:!>
src/volume_mod.F90:20:!> **volume integral**
src/volume_mod.F90:21:!> <ul>
src/volume_mod.F90:22:!> <li> The volume enclosed by the \f$v\f$-th interface is given by the integral
src/volume_mod.F90:23:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ V = \int_{{\cal V}} \; dv
src/volume_mod.F90:24:!>            = \frac{1}{3}\int_{{\cal V}} \; \nabla \cdot {\bf x} \; dv
src/volume_mod.F90:25:!>            = \frac{1}{3}\int_{{\cal S}} \; {\bf x} \cdot d{\bf s}
src/volume_mod.F90:26:!>            = \frac{1}{3}        \int_{0}^{2\pi} \!\! d\theta \int_{0}^{2\pi/N} \!\! d\zeta \;\;\;
src/volume_mod.F90:27:!>                                       \left. {\bf x   } \cdot {\bf x_\theta} \times {\bf x_\zeta} \right|^s
src/volume_mod.F90:28:!>       \f}
src/volume_mod.F90:29:!>       where we have used \f$\nabla \cdot {\bf x} = 3\f$, and have assumed that the domain is periodic in the angles. </li>
src/volume_mod.F90:30:!> </ul>
src/volume_mod.F90:31:!>
src/volume_mod.F90:32:!> **representation of surfaces**
src/volume_mod.F90:33:!>
src/volume_mod.F90:34:!> <ul>
src/volume_mod.F90:35:!> <li> The coordinate functions are
src/volume_mod.F90:36:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ R(\theta,\zeta) & = & \sum_i R_{e,i} \; \cos\alpha_i + \sum_i R_{o,i} \; \sin\alpha_i \\
src/volume_mod.F90:37:!>                     Z(\theta,\zeta) & = & \sum_i Z_{e,i} \; \cos\alpha_i + \sum_i Z_{o,i} \; \sin\alpha_i,
src/volume_mod.F90:38:!>       \f}
src/volume_mod.F90:39:!>       where \f$\alpha_i \equiv m_i \theta - n_i \zeta\f$. </li>
src/volume_mod.F90:40:!> </ul>
src/volume_mod.F90:41:!>
src/volume_mod.F90:42:!> @param[in] lvol index of the volume region
src/volume_mod.F90:43:!> @param[inout] vflag
src/volume_mod.F90:44:!> - vflag=0: An invalid volume will throw an error.
src/volume_mod.F90:45:!> - vflag=1: An invalid volume will be ignored, and volume validity will be written to \c vflag as an output value.
src/volume_mod.F90:111:!> **geometry**
src/volume_mod.F90:112:!> <ul>
src/volume_mod.F90:113:!> <li> The geometry is controlled by the input parameter \c Igeometry as follows: </li>
src/volume_mod.F90:119:       case( 1 ) !> <li> \c Igeometry.eq.1 : Cartesian : \f$\sqrt g = R_s\f$
src/volume_mod.F90:121:!>                   \f{eqnarray}{ V & = & \int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\!\!d\theta \int_{0}^{2\pi/N}\!\!\!\!\! d\zeta \; R \nonumber \\
src/volume_mod.F90:122:!>                                   & = & 2\pi \; \frac{2\pi}{N} \; R_{e,1}
src/volume_mod.F90:123:!>                   \f} </li>
src/volume_mod.F90:136:       case( 2 ) !> <li> \c Igeometry.eq.2 : cylindrical : \f$\sqrt g = R R_s = \frac{1}{2}\partial_s (R^2)\f$
src/volume_mod.F90:138:!>                   \f{eqnarray}{ V & = & \frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\!\!d\theta \int_{0}^{2\pi/N}\!\!\!\!\! d\zeta \; R^2 \nonumber \\
src/volume_mod.F90:139:!>                                   & = & \frac{1}{2} \; 2\pi \; \frac{2\pi}{N} \; \frac{1}{2} \;
src/volume_mod.F90:140:!>                                         \sum_i\sum_j R_{e,i}R_{e,j}\left[\cos(\alpha_i-\alpha_j)+\cos(\alpha_i+\alpha_j)\right] \nonumber \\
src/volume_mod.F90:141:!>                                   & + & \frac{1}{2} \; 2\pi \; \frac{2\pi}{N} \; \frac{1}{2} \;
src/volume_mod.F90:142:!>                                         \sum_i\sum_j R_{o,i}R_{o,j}\left[\cos(\alpha_i-\alpha_j)-\cos(\alpha_i+\alpha_j)\right]
src/volume_mod.F90:143:!>                   \f} </li>
src/volume_mod.F90:194:       case( 3 ) !> <li> \c Igeometry.eq.3 : toroidal : \f${\bf x}\cdot {\bf e}_\theta \times {\bf e}_\zeta  = R ( Z R_\theta - R Z_\theta ) \f$
src/volume_mod.F90:195:        !>      This is computed by fast Fourier transform:
src/volume_mod.F90:196:!>                   \f{eqnarray}{ V & = & \frac{1}{3} \; \int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\!\!d\theta \int_{0}^{2\pi/N}\!\!\!\!\! d\zeta \; R \left( Z R_{\theta} - R Z_{\theta}  \right)
src/volume_mod.F90:197:!>                                   \nonumber \\
src/volume_mod.F90:198:!>                                   & = & \frac{1}{3}  \; \sum_i \sum_j \sum_k R_{e,i} \left(Z_{e,j} R_{o,k} - R_{e,j} Z_{o,k} \right) (+m_k)
src/volume_mod.F90:199:!>                                   \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \cos\alpha_i \cos\alpha_j \cos\alpha_k \nonumber \\
src/volume_mod.F90:200:!>                                   & + & \frac{1}{3}  \; \sum_i \sum_j \sum_k R_{e,i} \left(Z_{o,j} R_{e,k} - R_{o,j} Z_{e,k} \right) (-m_k)
src/volume_mod.F90:201:!>                                   \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \cos\alpha_i \sin\alpha_j \sin\alpha_k \nonumber \\
src/volume_mod.F90:202:!>                                   & + & \frac{1}{3}  \; \sum_i \sum_j \sum_k R_{o,i} \left(Z_{e,j} R_{e,k} - R_{e,j} Z_{e,k} \right) (-m_k)
src/volume_mod.F90:203:!>                                   \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \sin\alpha_i \cos\alpha_j \sin\alpha_k \nonumber \\
src/volume_mod.F90:204:!>                                   & + & \frac{1}{3}  \; \sum_i \sum_j \sum_k R_{o,i} \left(Z_{o,j} R_{o,k} - R_{o,j} Z_{o,k} \right) (+m_k)
src/volume_mod.F90:205:!>                                   \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \sin\alpha_i \sin\alpha_j \cos\alpha_k
src/volume_mod.F90:206:!>                   \f} </li>
src/volume_mod.F90:207:!>
src/volume_mod.F90:208:!> <li> (Recall that the integral over an odd function is zero, so various terms in the above expansion have been ignored.) </li>
src/volume_mod.F90:209:!>
src/volume_mod.F90:210:!> <li> The trigonometric terms are
src/volume_mod.F90:211:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \begin{array}{ccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/volume_mod.F90:212:!>           4 \; \cos\alpha_i \cos\alpha_j \cos\alpha_k & \!\!\! = & \!\!\!
src/volume_mod.F90:213:!>       + \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i+\alpha_j+\alpha_k) & \!\!\! + \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i+\alpha_j-\alpha_k) & \!\!\! + \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i-\alpha_j+\alpha_k) & \!\!\! + \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i-\alpha_j-\alpha_k) \\
src/volume_mod.F90:214:!>           4 \; \cos\alpha_i \sin\alpha_j \sin\alpha_k & \!\!\! = & \!\!\!
src/volume_mod.F90:215:!>       - \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i+\alpha_j+\alpha_k) & \!\!\! + \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i+\alpha_j-\alpha_k) & \!\!\! + \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i-\alpha_j+\alpha_k) & \!\!\! - \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i-\alpha_j-\alpha_k) \\
src/volume_mod.F90:216:!>           4 \; \sin\alpha_i \cos\alpha_j \sin\alpha_k & \!\!\! = & \!\!\!
src/volume_mod.F90:217:!>       - \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i+\alpha_j+\alpha_k) & \!\!\! + \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i+\alpha_j-\alpha_k) & \!\!\! - \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i-\alpha_j+\alpha_k) & \!\!\! + \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i-\alpha_j-\alpha_k) \\
src/volume_mod.F90:218:!>           4 \; \sin\alpha_i \sin\alpha_j \cos\alpha_k & \!\!\! = & \!\!\!
src/volume_mod.F90:219:!>       - \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i+\alpha_j+\alpha_k) & \!\!\! - \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i+\alpha_j-\alpha_k) & \!\!\! + \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i-\alpha_j+\alpha_k) & \!\!\! + \!\!\! & \cos(\alpha_i-\alpha_j-\alpha_k)
src/volume_mod.F90:220:!>       \end{array}
src/volume_mod.F90:221:!>       \f} </li>
src/volume_mod.F90:222:!>
src/volume_mod.F90:223:!> <li> The required derivatives are
src/volume_mod.F90:224:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \begin{array}{cclccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/volume_mod.F90:225:!>           3 \displaystyle \frac{\partial V}{\partial R_{e,i}} & = & \left( + Z_{e,j} R_{o,k} m_k - R_{e,j} Z_{o,k} m_k - R_{e,j} Z_{o.k} m_k \right) &
src/volume_mod.F90:226:!>                                                           \displaystyle \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \cos\alpha_i \cos\alpha_j \cos\alpha_k \\
src/volume_mod.F90:227:!>                                                     & + & \left( - Z_{o,j} R_{e,k} m_k + R_{o,j} Z_{e,k} m_k + R_{o,j} Z_{e,k} m_k \right) &
src/volume_mod.F90:228:!>                                                           \displaystyle \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \cos\alpha_i \sin\alpha_j \sin\alpha_k \\
src/volume_mod.F90:229:!>                                                     & + & \left( - R_{o,k} Z_{e,j} m_i                                             \right) &
src/volume_mod.F90:230:!>                                                           \displaystyle \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \sin\alpha_i \cos\alpha_j \sin\alpha_k \\
src/volume_mod.F90:231:!>                                                     & + & \left( - R_{e,k} Z_{o,j} m_i                                             \right) &
src/volume_mod.F90:232:!>                                                           \displaystyle \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \sin\alpha_i \sin\alpha_j \cos\alpha_k
src/volume_mod.F90:233:!>       \end{array} \f}
src/volume_mod.F90:234:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \begin{array}{cclccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc}
src/volume_mod.F90:235:!>           3 \displaystyle \frac{\partial V}{\partial Z_{o,i}} & = & \left( - R_{e,k} R_{e,j} m_i                                             \right) &
src/volume_mod.F90:236:!>                                                           \displaystyle \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \cos\alpha_i \cos\alpha_j \cos\alpha_k \\
src/volume_mod.F90:237:!>                                                     & + & \left( - R_{o,k} R_{o,j} m_i                                             \right) &
src/volume_mod.F90:238:!>                                                           \displaystyle \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \cos\alpha_i \sin\alpha_j \sin\alpha_k \\
src/volume_mod.F90:239:!>                                                     & + & \left( - R_{e,j} R_{e,k} m_k                                             \right) &
src/volume_mod.F90:240:!>                                                           \displaystyle \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \sin\alpha_i \cos\alpha_j \sin\alpha_k \\
src/volume_mod.F90:241:!>                                                     & + & \left( + R_{o,j} R_{o,k} m_k                                             \right) &
src/volume_mod.F90:242:!>                                                           \displaystyle \int \!\!\!\! \int \!\! d\theta d\zeta \; \sin\alpha_i \sin\alpha_j \cos\alpha_k
src/volume_mod.F90:243:!>       \end{array} \f} </li>
src/volume_mod.F90:244:!>
src/volume_mod.F90:245:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:2:!> \file
src/xspech.F90:3:!> \brief Main program
src/xspech.F90:5:!> \brief Main program of SPEC.
src/xspech.F90:6:!>
src/xspech.F90:7:!> This only calls the xpech() subroutine to do a stand-alone SPEC run.
src/xspech.F90:8:!> @return none
src/xspech.F90:14:!> \brief Main subroutine of SPEC.
src/xspech.F90:15:!>
src/xspech.F90:16:!> This orchestrates a stand-alone SPEC run:
src/xspech.F90:17:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:18:!> <li> read the input file </li>
src/xspech.F90:19:!> <li> solve the MRxMHD equilibrium (see spec() )</li>
src/xspech.F90:20:!> <li> run some diagnostics on the results </li>
src/xspech.F90:21:!> <li> write the output file(s) </li>
src/xspech.F90:22:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:84:!> **reading input, allocating global variables**
src/xspech.F90:85:!>
src/xspech.F90:86:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:87:!> <li> The input namelists and geometry are read in via a call to readin() .
src/xspech.F90:88:!>       A full description of the required input is given in global.f90 . </li>
src/xspech.F90:89:!> <li> Most internal variables, global memory etc., are allocated in preset() . </li>
src/xspech.F90:90:!> <li> All quantities in the input file are mirrored into the output file's group \c /input . </li>
src/xspech.F90:91:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:117:!> **preparing output file group iterations**
src/xspech.F90:118:!>
src/xspech.F90:119:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:120:!> <li> The group \c /iterations is created in the output file.
src/xspech.F90:121:!>       This group contains the interface geometry at each iteration, which is useful for constructing movies illustrating the convergence.
src/xspech.F90:122:!>       The data structure in use is an unlimited array of the following compound datatype:
src/xspech.F90:123:!> ```C
src/xspech.F90:124:!> DATATYPE  H5T_COMPOUND {
src/xspech.F90:125:!>       H5T_NATIVE_integer "nDcalls";
src/xspech.F90:126:!>       H5T_NATIVE_DOUBLE "Energy";
src/xspech.F90:127:!>       H5T_NATIVE_DOUBLE "ForceErr";
src/xspech.F90:128:!>       H5T_ARRAY { [Mvol+1][mn] H5T_NATIVE_DOUBLE } "iRbc";
src/xspech.F90:129:!>       H5T_ARRAY { [Mvol+1][mn] H5T_NATIVE_DOUBLE } "iZbs";
src/xspech.F90:130:!>       H5T_ARRAY { [Mvol+1][mn] H5T_NATIVE_DOUBLE } "iRbs";
src/xspech.F90:131:!>       H5T_ARRAY { [Mvol+1][mn] H5T_NATIVE_DOUBLE } "iZbc";
src/xspech.F90:132:!> }
src/xspech.F90:133:!> ```
src/xspech.F90:134:!> </li>
src/xspech.F90:135:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:149:!> **restart files**
src/xspech.F90:150:!>
src/xspech.F90:151:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:152:!> <li> wrtend() is called to write the restart files. </li>
src/xspech.F90:153:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:183:!> \brief Read command-line arguments; in particular, determine input file (name or extension).
src/xspech.F90:184:!>
src/xspech.F90:185:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:186:!> <li> The input file name, \c ext , is given as the first command line input, and the input file itself is then \c ext.sp .</li>
src/xspech.F90:187:!> <li> Alternatively, you can directly specify the input file itself as \c ext.sp .</li>
src/xspech.F90:188:!> <li> You can also generate a template input file using \c xspec -i .</li>
src/xspech.F90:189:!> <li> Or print help information using \c xspec -h .</li>
src/xspech.F90:190:!> <li> Additional command line inputs recognized are:
src/xspech.F90:191:!>      <ul>
src/xspech.F90:192:!>      <li> \c -readin will immediately set \c Wreadin=T ; this may be over-ruled when the namelist \c screenlist is read
src/xspech.F90:193:!>      </ul> </li>
src/xspech.F90:194:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:269:!> \brief This is the main "driver" for the physics part of SPEC.
src/xspech.F90:270:!>
src/xspech.F90:271:!> Picard iterations are performed (if in free-boundary mode)
src/xspech.F90:272:!> and within each Picard iteration, the fixed-boundary problem
src/xspech.F90:273:!> is solved (also iteratively).
src/xspech.F90:369:!> **packing geometrical degrees-of-freedom into vector**
src/xspech.F90:370:!>
src/xspech.F90:371:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:372:!> <li> If \c NGdof.gt.0 , where \c NGdof counts the geometrical degrees-of-freedom, i.e. the \f$R_{bc}\f$, \f$Z_{bs}\f$, etc.,
src/xspech.F90:373:!>       then packxi() is called to "pack" the geometrical degrees-of-freedom into \c position(0:NGdof) . </li>
src/xspech.F90:374:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:387:!> **initialize adiabatic constants**
src/xspech.F90:388:!>
src/xspech.F90:389:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:390:!> <li> If \c Ladiabatic.eq.0 , then the "adiabatic constants" in each region, \f$P_v\f$, are calculated as
src/xspech.F90:391:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ P_v \equiv p_v V_v^\gamma, \label{eq:adiabatic_xspech}
src/xspech.F90:392:!>       \f}
src/xspech.F90:393:!>       where \f$p_v\equiv\,\f$\c pressure(vvol) , the volume \f$V_v\f$ of each region is computed by volume() ,
src/xspech.F90:394:!>       and the adiabatic index \f$\gamma\equiv\,\f$\c gamma . </li>
src/xspech.F90:395:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:417:!> **solving force-balance**
src/xspech.F90:418:!>
src/xspech.F90:419:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:420:!> <li> If there are geometrical degress of freedom, i.e. if \c NGdof.gt.0 , then
src/xspech.F90:421:!>      <ul>
src/xspech.F90:422:!>      <li> \todo If \c Lminimize.eq.1 , call pc00aa() to find minimum of energy functional
src/xspech.F90:423:!>              using quasi-Newton, preconditioned conjugate gradient method, \c E04DGF
src/xspech.F90:424:!>
src/xspech.F90:425:!>      </li>
src/xspech.F90:426:!>      <li> If \c Lfindzero.gt.0 , call newton() to find extremum of constrained energy functional using a Newton method, \c C05PDF . </li>
src/xspech.F90:427:!>      </ul> </li>
src/xspech.F90:428:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:447:!> **post diagnostics**
src/xspech.F90:448:!>
src/xspech.F90:449:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:450:!> <li> The pressure is computed from the adiabatic constants from Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:adiabatic_xspech})\f$, i.e. \f$p=P/V^\gamma\f$. </li>
src/xspech.F90:451:!> <li> The Beltrami/vacuum fields in each region are re-calculated using dforce() . </li>
src/xspech.F90:452:!> <li> If \c Lcheck.eq.5 \c .or. \c LHevalues \c .or. \c LHevectors, then the force-gradient matrix is examined using hesian() . </li>
src/xspech.F90:453:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:524:!> **free-boundary: re-computing normal field**
src/xspech.F90:525:!>
src/xspech.F90:526:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:527:!> <li> If \c Lfreebound.eq.1 and \c Lfindzero.gt.0 and \c mfreeits.ne.0 ,
src/xspech.F90:528:!>       then the magnetic field at the computational boundary produced by the plasma currents is computed using bnorml() . </li>
src/xspech.F90:529:!> <li> The "new" magnetic field at the computational boundary produced by the plasma currents is updated using a Picard scheme:
src/xspech.F90:530:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \texttt{Bns}_i^{j} = \lambda \, \texttt{Bns}_i^{j-1} + (1-\lambda) \texttt{Bns}_i, \label{eq:blending_xspech}
src/xspech.F90:531:!>       \f}
src/xspech.F90:532:!>       where \f$j\f$ labels free-boundary iterations, the "blending parameter" is \f$\lambda\equiv\,\f$\c gBnbld ,
src/xspech.F90:533:!>       and \c Bns \f$_i\f$ is computed by virtual casing.
src/xspech.F90:534:!>       The subscript "$i$" labels Fourier harmonics.  </li>
src/xspech.F90:535:!> <li> If the new (unblended) normal field is _not_ sufficiently close to the old normal field, as quantified by \c gBntol ,
src/xspech.F90:536:!>       then the free-boundary iterations continue.
src/xspech.F90:537:!>       This is quantified by
src/xspech.F90:538:!>       \f{eqnarray}{ \sum_i | \texttt{Bns}_i^{j-1} - \texttt{Bns}_i | / N, \label{eq:gBntol_xspech}
src/xspech.F90:539:!>       \f}
src/xspech.F90:540:!>       where \f$N\f$ is the total number of Fourier harmonics. </li>
src/xspech.F90:541:!> <li> There are several choices that are available:
src/xspech.F90:542:!>       <ul>
src/xspech.F90:543:!>       <li> if \c mfreeits=-2 : the vacuum magnetic field
src/xspech.F90:544:!>             (really, the normal component of the field produced by the external currents at the computational boundary)
src/xspech.F90:545:!>             required to hold the given equlibrium is written to file.
src/xspech.F90:546:!>             This information is required as input by FOCUS \cite y2017_zhu
src/xspech.F90:547:!>             for example. (This option probably needs to revised.) </li>
src/xspech.F90:548:!>       <li> if \c mfreeits=-1 : after the plasma field is computed by virtual casing,
src/xspech.F90:549:!>             the vacuum magnetic field is set to exactly balance the plasma field
src/xspech.F90:550:!>             (again, we are really talking about the normal component at the computational boundary.)
src/xspech.F90:551:!>             This will ensure that the computational boundary itself if a flux surface of the total magnetic field. </li>
src/xspech.F90:552:!>       <li> if \c mfreeits=0 : the plasma field at the computational boundary is not updated; no "free-boundary" iterations take place. </li>
src/xspech.F90:553:!>       <li> if \c mfreeits>0 : the plasma field at the computational boundary is updated according to the above blending Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:blending_xspech})\f$,
src/xspech.F90:554:!>             and the free-boundary iterations will continue until either the tolerance condition is met (see \c gBntol and Eqn.\f$(\ref{eq:gBntol_xspech})\f$)
src/xspech.F90:555:!>             or the maximum number of free-boundary iterations, namely \c mfreeits , is reached.
src/xspech.F90:556:!>             For this case, \c Lzerovac is relevant:
src/xspech.F90:557:!>             if \c Lzerovac=1 , then the vacuum field is set equal to the normal field at every iteration,
src/xspech.F90:558:!>             which results in the computational boundary being a flux surface.
src/xspech.F90:559:!>             (I am not sure if this is identical to setting \c mfreeits=-1 ; the logic etc. needs to be revised.) </li>
src/xspech.F90:560:!>       </ul> </li>
src/xspech.F90:561:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:703:!> **output files: vector potential**
src/xspech.F90:704:!>
src/xspech.F90:705:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:706:!> <li> The vector potential is written to file using ra00aa() . </li>
src/xspech.F90:707:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:728:!> \brief Final diagnostics
src/xspech.F90:729:!>
src/xspech.F90:730:!> <ul>
src/xspech.F90:731:!> <li> sc00aa() is called to compute the covariant components of the magnetic field at the interfaces;
src/xspech.F90:732:!>      these are related to the singular currents </li>
src/xspech.F90:733:!> <li> if \c Lcheck=1 , jo00aa() is called to compute the error in the Beltrami equation </li>
src/xspech.F90:734:!> <li> pp00aa() is called to construct the Poincare plot by field-line following. </li>
src/xspech.F90:735:!> </ul>
src/xspech.F90:892:!> \brief Closes output files, writes screen summary.
src/xspech.F90:893:!>
